# Azurite: module map and algorithm catalog

The detailed per-module catalog. For an overview and build instructions see the
[top-level README](../README.md).

A Lean 4 library for efficient, formally verified mathematical computation.

Azurite provides array-backed data structures for polynomials, vectors, and matrices with proven equivalences to their Mathlib counterparts. Every computable operation has a corresponding proof that it agrees with the abstract Mathlib definition.

## Module Map

### Core Data Structures

| Module | Description |
|--------|-------------|
| `AzFloat/Agm` | **The arithmetic–geometric mean** `agmPrecRound x y p mode`, `agm x y p` (MCA §4.8): an interval iteration at working precision `w` (`AgmBounds`, `agmStep` rounds lower bounds down and upper bounds up, so `aLo ≤ a_n ≤ aHi`, `bLo ≤ b_n ≤ bHi`), the bracket `bLo ≤ AGM ≤ aHi` after `≥ 1` steps (`agmApprox`, stopping test `agmConverged` for speed only), Ziv's loop with fuel `agmFuel p` (four doublings from `p + 64`; termination cannot be proven — the AGM of distinct positive algebraic numbers is transcendental by deep results, not formalizable), special values (`NaN` for negatives and `AGM(∞, 0)`, `0` for a zero argument, `∞` for an infinite one, `AGM(x, x) = x` exactly). Two more variants share the engine (`agmApproxFrom` from any initial enclosure, rationals seeded by `ratBounds q w = (⌊q⌋_w, ⌈q⌉_w)`): `agmRatPrecRound q₁ q₂` (two `AzRat`s; `q₁ = q₂` is exact `ofAzRatRound`) and `agmFloatRatPrecRound x q` (`AzFloat` and `AzRat`; the exact case `x = q` is detected by the correctly rounded `x − q` at one bit, no materialization of `x` as a rational). Tests: `AzFloat/Tests/Agm`. |
| `AzFloat/Equiv/Agm` | `Spec.agm` (extended-real specification) with its special-value lemmas; directed-rounding facts `roundVal_floor_spec`/`roundVal_ceiling_spec` and the operation versions (`addFloor_spec`, `mulCeiling_spec`, `sqrtFloor_spec`, …); the enclosure invariant `Encloses` preserved by `agmStep` (monotonicity of the AGM step) and `agmIter`; `agmApprox_spec` (strict lower end via `agmB_lt_AGM`); the main theorem `agmPrecRound_eq_or`: `agmPrecRound x y p mode = liftVal₂ Spec.agm x y p mode ∨ = (nan, .eq)`; for the variants `agmRatPrecRound_eq_or` (against `liftVal₀ (Spec.agm q₁ q₂)`) and `agmFloatRatPrecRound_eq_or` (against `roundVal p mode (x.toVal.bind fun a => Spec.agm a q)`), via `ratBounds_spec`, `agmApproxFrom_spec`, `azRat_lt_zero_iff`/`azRat_eq_zero_iff` (from `AzRat.cmp_eq_compare`, `toRat_injective`), `isZero_iff_toVal` and `subRat_isZero_iff` (`subRatPrecRound x q 1 .Floor` is zero iff `x = q`, by `eq_zero_of_roundFloor_eq_zero`). `AzFloat/Equiv/Ziv` gains `zivLoop_eq_of_ne_nan` (the fuel loop is correct whenever it does not give up). |
| `AzFloat/GaussConstant` | **Gauss's constant** `G = 1/AGM(1, √2) = Γ(1/4)²/(2π)^(3/2)`: `gaussConstantPrecRound p mode`, `gaussConstant p`. The AGM engine of `AzFloat/Agm` seeded with `one` and the floor/ceiling of `√2` at the working precision (`sqrt2PrecRound`); the bracket is `⌊1/aHi⌋_w ≤ G ≤ ⌈1/bLo⌉_w` (`gaussApprox`); Ziv's loop with fuel `agmFuel p` (`G` is transcendental by Chudnovsky's algebraic independence of `π` and `Γ(1/4)`, the CM case of the general AGM theorem — not formalizable, so the spec is "correctly rounded or `NaN`"). Tests: `AzFloat/Tests/GaussConstant`. |
| `AzFloat/Equiv/GaussConstant` | `gaussConstantPrecRound_eq_or` (`= liftVal₀ (1/AGM(1, √2))` or `(nan, .eq)`), via `gaussApprox_spec` (enclosure + the lower end strictly below `G`, from the new `AGM_lt_agmA`) and the invariant `LowerGeOne` (lower bounds stay `≥ 1`, so `1/bLo` is finite); directed-rounding helpers `roundVal_floor_ge`, `addFloor_ge`, `mulFloor_ge`, `sqrtFloor_ge`, `divFloor_spec`, `divCeiling_spec`; `gaussConstant_eq_ellipticK : 1/AGM(1, √2) = √2 K(1/√2)/π`. |
| `AzFloat/Gelfond` | **Gelfond's constant `e^π`**: `gelfondPrecRound p mode`, `gelfond p`. `gelfondBracket w = expOfBracket (piBracket w) w` (`AzFloat/ExpApprox`); Ziv's loop with fuel `gelfondFuel p = zivFuel (16 (p + 64))` — the termination certificate would need the irrationality of `e^π` (Gelfond's theorem, not in Mathlib; see the pending-irrationality ledger in `docs/azfloat_plan.md`). `(nan, .eq)` at precision `0`. Tests: `AzFloat/Tests/Gelfond`. |
| `AzFloat/Equiv/Gelfond` | `gelfondBracket_spec` (strict enclosure, from `piBracket_spec`, `piEps_le`, `expOfBracket_spec`), `gelfondApprox_spec`, **`gelfondPrecRound_eq_or`** (`= liftVal₀ (some (exp π))` or `(nan, .eq)`, via `zivLoop_eq_of_ne_nan_threshold`). |
| `AzFloat/GelfondSchneider` | **The Gelfond–Schneider constant `2^√2`**: `gelfondSchneiderPrecRound p mode`, `gelfondSchneider p`. `sqrt2Ln2Bracket w` = the floats below and above `√2` times the ends of `ln2Bracket w` (directed products), `gelfondSchneiderBracket = expOfBracket` of it; Ziv's loop with fuel `gelfondSchneiderFuel p = zivFuel (16 (p + 64))` — the termination certificate would need the irrationality of `2^√2` (the Gelfond–Schneider theorem, not in Mathlib; pending-irrationality ledger). `(nan, .eq)` at precision `0`. Tests: `AzFloat/Tests/GelfondSchneider`. |
| `AzFloat/Equiv/GelfondSchneider` | `sqrt2Ln2Bracket_spec` (strict enclosure of width `≤ 1`: the float below `√2` is rational, `irrational_sqrt_two`), `gelfondSchneiderBracket_spec` (`expOfBracket_spec`, `two_rpow_eq_exp`), `gelfondSchneiderApprox_spec`, **`gelfondSchneiderPrecRound_eq_or`** (`= liftVal₀ (some (2^√2))` or `(nan, .eq)`). |
| `AzFloat/Ramanujan` | **Ramanujan's constant `e^(π √163)`**: `ramanujanPrecRound p mode`, `ramanujan p`. `piSqrt163Bracket w` = the `π` bracket times `sqrtBracket` at the exact float `163` (directed products), `ramanujanBracket = expOfBracket` of it; Ziv's loop with fuel `ramanujanFuel p` (irrationality pending: Gelfond–Schneider). The constant is within `7.5 · 10^(−13)` of `640320³ + 744`, so the first Ziv level succeeds only from precision about `100` on. `(nan, .eq)` at precision `0`. Tests: `AzFloat/Tests/Ramanujan`. |
| `AzFloat/Equiv/Ramanujan` | `piSqrt163Bracket_spec` (strict enclosure of width `≤ 1`, with `√163 < 13`, `π < 3.15`), `ramanujanBracket_spec`, `ramanujanApprox_spec`, **`ramanujanPrecRound_eq_or`** (`= liftVal₀ (some (exp (π √163)))` or `(nan, .eq)`). |
| `AzFloat/PiBracket` | **The Brent–Salamin bracket of `π`** (MCA §4.8.2, `BrentSalamin.lean`): the Gauss–Legendre iteration `a' = (a+b)/2`, `b' = √(ab)`, `s' = s − 2^(n+1)((a−b)/2)²` from `a₀ = 1`, `b₀ = 1/√2`, `s₀ = 1/2`, run `piSteps w = ⌊lg w⌋ + 1` times on enclosures with directed rounding (`PiBounds`, `piStep`, `piIter`; the lower end of `(a − b)²` clamped at `0` by `clampNonneg`), then `π_N = 2a_{N+1}²/s_N` enclosed and widened by `2^(1−w)` (`piApprox`). |
| `AzFloat/Pi` | **The constant `π`**: `piPrecRound p mode`, `pi p` — the certified Ziv loop (`zivLoopCertified`, no fuel) on `piApprox`, total because `π` is irrational and the bracket shrinks. Tests: `AzFloat/Tests/Pi`. |
| `AzFloat/Equiv/DirectedRounding` | Quantitative directed rounding: `FloorOf w x r` / `CeilOf w x r` (`r` within `2x·2^(−w)` of `x ≥ 0` on the right side, from `truncError_spec`), with the operation-level versions `addFloor_err`, `subFloor_le/err`, `mulFloor_err`, `sqrFloor_err`, `sqrtFloor_err`, `divFloor_err` and the `Ceiling` counterparts, the signed `addFloor_abs`, `divFloor_abs`/`divCeiling_abs` (error `≤ 2|x|·2^(−w)`, any sign), the precision changes `setPrecFloor_err`/`setPrecCeiling_err`; `toVal_shiftLeft_of_toVal`, `toVal_sign_of_isNegative`. The raw material for width analyses of interval iterations. |
| `AzFloat/Equiv/PiBracket` | The enclosure invariant `PiEncloses w n s` (ends enclose `a_n, b_n, s_n`; widths `≤ 2^(2n+4) 2^(−w)` and `≤ 2^(3n+7) 2^(−w)`), preserved by `piStep` (`piEncloses_step` from `mean_step`, `geo_step`, `diffsq_step`) for `w ≥ 64`; `piApprox_spec` (bracket of `π`, strict below, width `≤ piEps w = 2^19 w³ 2^(−w)`), `tendsto_piEps`, and the Ziv certificate `piApprox_isSome_exists` from `irrational_pi`. |
| `AzFloat/Equiv/Pi` | `piPrecRound_eq_liftVal₀ : piPrecRound p mode = liftVal₀ π p mode` — `π` is correctly rounded in every mode, without fuel. |
| `AzFloat/PiDiv3Bracket` | `piDiv3Approx w = divBracketByInt (piBracket w) 3 w`: the bracket of `π/3` as a pair of `Roundable`s (one directed division of each end of the `π` bracket). |
| `AzFloat/Equiv/PiDiv3Bracket` | `irrational_pi_div_three`, `piDiv3Approx_spec` (width `piDiv3Eps w = piEps w + 4(π + piEps w) 2^(−w)`, `tendsto_piDiv3Eps`), `piDiv3Approx_isSome_exists`. |
| `AzFloat/OneDivPiBracket` | `oneDivPiBracket w = divBracketByPosBracket (one, one) (maxOf (piBracket w).1 two, (piBracket w).2) w`: the bracket of `1/π` (the lower end of the `π` bracket raised to `2 < π` so that the proven width bound never pushes it to zero), `oneDivPiApprox`. |
| `AzFloat/Equiv/OneDivPiBracket` | `oneDivPiBracket_spec` (width `oneDivPiEps w = divEps (1/2) 1 0 (piEps w) w`, `tendsto_oneDivPiEps`), `oneDivPiApprox_spec` (`irrational_pi.inv`), `oneDivPiApprox_isSome_exists`. |
| `AzFloat/SqrtPiBracket` | `sqrtBracket B w` (square roots of the ends, outward) and `rsqrtBracket B w` (reciprocal square roots, swapped, outward — one `rsqrtPrecRound` per end, no division); `piBracket2` (the `π` bracket with lower end `≥ 2`), `sqrtPiBracket`/`sqrtPiApprox`, `oneDivSqrtPiBracket`/`oneDivSqrtPiApprox`, `oneDivSqrtTauBracket`/`oneDivSqrtTauApprox` (`rsqrtBracket` of the bracket shifted by one bit). |
| `AzFloat/Equiv/SqrtPiBracket` | `irrational_sqrt_of_irrational` (a rational square root would make the radicand rational), `irrational_sqrt_pi`, `irrational_sqrt_two_mul_pi`; `rsqrtFloor_err`/`rsqrtCeiling_err` (directed `rsqrtPrecRound`); `sqrt_facts`, `sqrtBracket_spec` (width `≤ (hi − lo)/2 + 4 hi 2^(−w)`, lower end `≥ 1`), `rsqrtBracket_spec` (width `≤ (hi − lo)/4 + 4 · 2^(−w)`); `sqrtPiEps`, `oneDivSqrtPiEps`, `oneDivSqrtTauEps` with limits; the three `…Approx_spec` and `…Approx_isSome_exists`. |
| `AzFloat/PiConstants` | **The constants `τ = 2π`, `π/2`, `π/3`, `π/4`, `π/6`, `π/8`, `1/π`, `2/π`, `√π`, `1/√π`, `2/√π`, `1/√τ`**: `tauPrecRound`/`tau`, `piDiv2PrecRound`/`piDiv2`, `piDiv4…`, `piDiv8…` are exact shifts of `piPrecRound` (rounding and comparison carry over); `piDiv3PrecRound`/`piDiv3` is a certified Ziv loop (no fuel) over `piDiv3Approx`; `piDiv6…` is its exact shift; `oneDivPiPrecRound`/`oneDivPi` is the Ziv loop over `oneDivPiApprox` and `twoDivPi…` its exact shift; `sqrtPiPrecRound`/`sqrtPi`, `oneDivSqrtPiPrecRound`/`oneDivSqrtPi`, `oneDivSqrtTauPrecRound`/`oneDivSqrtTau` are Ziv loops over the brackets of `AzFloat/SqrtPiBracket`, and `twoDivSqrtPi…` the exact shift of `1/√π`. `(nan, .eq)` at precision `0` like every `…PrecRound`. Tests: `AzFloat/Tests/PiConstants`. |
| `AzFloat/Equiv/PiConstants` | `shift_liftVal₀` (a correctly rounded constant shifted by `2^k` is the correctly rounded `v · 2^k`, from `roundVal_mul_two_zpow`), `tauPrecRound_eq_liftVal₀` (`2π`), `piDiv2PrecRound_eq_liftVal₀`, `piDiv3PrecRound_eq_liftVal₀`, `piDiv4PrecRound_eq_liftVal₀`, `piDiv6PrecRound_eq_liftVal₀`, `piDiv8PrecRound_eq_liftVal₀`, `oneDivPiPrecRound_eq_liftVal₀` (`π⁻¹`), `twoDivPiPrecRound_eq_liftVal₀` (`2/π`), `sqrtPiPrecRound_eq_liftVal₀`, `oneDivSqrtPiPrecRound_eq_liftVal₀`, `twoDivSqrtPiPrecRound_eq_liftVal₀`, `oneDivSqrtTauPrecRound_eq_liftVal₀`. |
| `AzFloat/Lemniscate` | **The lemniscate constant** `ϖ = 2∫₀¹ dt/√(1 − t⁴) = π·G = π/AGM(1, √2)`: `lemniscateConstantPrecRound p mode`, `lemniscateConstant p`. The bracket is the outward-rounded product of the brackets of `π` (`piBracket`) and of Gauss's constant (`gaussBracket`) at the working precision (`lemniscateBracket`); Ziv's loop with fuel `agmFuel p` (`ϖ` is transcendental by Schneider's theorem on elliptic periods, not in Mathlib — spec "correctly rounded or `NaN`"). Tests: `AzFloat/Tests/Lemniscate`. |
| `AzFloat/Equiv/Lemniscate` | `lemniscateConstantPrecRound_eq_or` (`= liftVal₀ ϖ` or `(nan, .eq)`) from `lemniscateApprox_spec` (the product of the two brackets encloses `π·G`, strictly from below) and `BZ.lemniscate_eq_pi_mul_gauss`. |
| `AzFloat/Ln2Bracket` | **The bracket of `ln 2`** as a pair of floats: `ln 2 = 18 atanh(1/26) − 2 atanh(1/4801) + 8 atanh(1/8749)`, the three series by binary splitting (`AzNat.atanhNumDen`) with term counts `ln2N₁/₂/₃ w` (`(2N+1)⌊lg q⌋ ≥ w + 6`); each exact series fraction becomes a float pair by `seriesBracket` (numerator and denominator rounded to `k = w + 8` bits, down/up for the lower end and up/down for the upper, then one directed division each — `O(M(w))` whatever the fraction's size; no GCD, no large products), the tail bounds `(4/3) t_N` are replaced by the power of two `2^(−w−5)`, and the Machin combination is evaluated on floats with directed rounding at `k` bits: `ln2Bracket w = (lo, hi)`; `ln2Approx w` (the ends as `Roundable`s); `log2EBracket w = (⌊1/hi⌋_w, ⌈1/lo⌉_w)`, `log2EApprox w`. |
| `AzFloat/Ln2` | **The constant `ln 2`**: `ln2PrecRound p mode`, `ln2 p` — the certified Ziv loop `zivLoopCertified` (in `AzFloat/Ziv`: doubles the working precision until `roundingPossible` answers, termination by a certificate `∃ f, …` instead of fuel, measure `Nat.find h − f`) over `ln2Approx`, started at `p + 64`; the certificate is `ln2Approx_isSome_exists` (irrationality of `ln 2`). Also `log2EPrecRound`/`log2E`: `log₂ e = 1/ln 2` from the reciprocal bracket `log2EBracket w` (two directed divisions, no new series), certified by `irrational_log_two.inv`. Tests: `AzFloat/Tests/Ln2`. |
| `AzFloat/Equiv/Ln2Bracket` | The exact ends `ln2Lo w ≤ ln 2 ≤ ln2Hi w` (`log_two_mem_Icc`) of width `≤ 2^(−w)` (`ln2Hi_sub_ln2Lo`); `sum_atanhTerm_le` (a partial sum of `atanh(1/q)` is `≤ 1/(q−1)`, from `log x ≤ x − 1`), `seriesBracket_spec` (`S (1 − 6·2^(−k)) ≤ lv ≤ S ≤ hv ≤ S (1 + 8·2^(−k))` for `k ≥ 4`, from `setPrecFloor/Ceiling_err` and `divFloor/Ceiling_err`; `(1 − 4x)(1 + 2x) ≤ 1 − 2x` and `(1 + 5x)(1 − 2x) ≥ 1 + 2x` for `x ≤ 1/10`), `toVal_tail`, `ln2Bracket_spec` (floats `lv ≤ ln 2 ≤ hv`, `hv − lv ≤ 8·2^(−w)` — in fact `≤ 2·2^(−w)` —, `lv ≥ 1/5` for `w ≥ 2`; every rounding of the combination within `4·2^(−k)` since all magnitudes are `≤ 2`), `exists_rat_of_toVal` (float values are rational), `ln2Approx_spec` (`Rounds`, strict by `irrational_log_two`), the certificate `ln2Approx_isSome_exists` (`exists_isSome_of_irrational`, `C = 8`); the reciprocal `log2EBracket_spec` (width `≤ 80·2^(−w)` for `w ≥ 2`), `log2EApprox_spec`, `log2EApprox_isSome_exists` (threshold form, `W₀ = 2`). |
| `AzFloat/Equiv/Ln2` | `ln2PrecRound_eq_liftVal₀ : ln2PrecRound p mode = liftVal₀ (ln 2) p mode` for `p ≠ 0`; `AzFloat/Equiv/Ziv` gains `zivLoopCertified_eq` (the certified loop is the correctly rounded value), `not_mem_floatSet_of_irrational` and the generic certificate `exists_isSome_of_irrational` (irrational value + brackets of width `≤ C (1/2)^w` ⇒ rounding possible at some `w₀·2^f`). `Equiv/Ln2` adds `log2EPrecRound_eq_liftVal₀` (and the `Real.logb 2 (exp 1)` form). |
| `AzFloat/Ln10Bracket` | **Brackets of `ln 10`, `log₁₀ e`, `log₂ 10`, `log₁₀ 2`**: `ln10Bracket w` = `lnFloorBracket` at the float `tenF = 10` (one AGM run; the floor is exact), the lower end raised to `2 < ln 10` by `maxOf` so that the proven width bound never pushes it below `0`; `log10EBracket = divBracketByPosBracket (one, one) ln10Bracket`, `log2TenBracket = ln10Bracket / ln2Bracket`, `log10TwoBracket = ln2Bracket / ln10Bracket`; each with its `…Approx` pair of `Roundable`s. |
| `AzFloat/Equiv/Ln10Bracket` | `two_lt_log_ten` (from `exp_one_lt_d9`), `irrational_log_ten`, `irrational_log_ten_div_log_two` (`irrational_log_div_log_of_rat` with base `2`: `maxRootBase 2 = 2`, `10 ≠ 2^r`), `irrational_log_two_div_log_ten`; `ln10Bracket_spec` (width `ln10Eps w = lnRatEps …`), the quotient specs through `divBracketByPosBracket_spec` + `divEps_mono` (`K ≤ 1/2` or `5`), `…Approx_spec` and `…Approx_isSome_exists` for the four constants. |
| `AzFloat/ExpConstants` | **The constants `e`, `ln 10`, `log₁₀ e`, `log₂ 10`, `log₁₀ 2`**: `ePrecRound p mode` (= `expPrecRound one`), `e p`; `ln10PrecRound`/`ln10`, `log10EPrecRound`/`log10E`, `log2TenPrecRound`/`log2Ten`, `log10TwoPrecRound`/`log10Two` — certified Ziv loops (no fuel) over the brackets of `AzFloat/Ln10Bracket`, started at `p + 64`; `(nan, .eq)` at precision `0` like every `…PrecRound`. Tests: `AzFloat/Tests/ExpConstants`. |
| `AzFloat/Equiv/ExpConstants` | `ePrecRound_eq_liftVal₀` (`exp 1`), `ln10PrecRound_eq_liftVal₀` (`log 10`), `log10EPrecRound_eq_liftVal₀` (`(log 10)⁻¹`, also `logb 10 (exp 1)`), `log2TenPrecRound_eq_liftVal₀` (`log 10 / log 2`, also `logb 2 10`), `log10TwoPrecRound_eq_liftVal₀` (`log 2 / log 10`, also `logb 10 2`). |
| `AzFloat/LnBracket` | **The bracket of `ln x`** (MCA §4.8.2, `Chapter4/AGMLog.lean`, `AGMLogBound.lean`): with `h = lnH w = ⌊w/4⌋ + 2`, the argument `x = m·2^e` is scaled to `y = x·2^ℓ ∈ [2^(2h+1), 2^(2h+2))`, `k = 4/y` by directed division, the interval AGM of `AzFloat/Agm` run for `lnSteps w = 2⌈lg 2h⌉ + 3` steps (`agmIterN`), `π/(2·AGM)` from `piBracket` with outward division, the AGM–logarithm error `2^(size(2h+2) − 4h)` added on both sides, and `ℓ·ln 2` as `|ℓ|` times the ends of the float `ln2Bracket`, rounded outward, the signs restored by negation; `lnBracket x e w = (lo, hi)`, `lnApprox` (the ends as `Roundable`s). Also `lnFloorBracket y w` (`ln` of a float `y = ⌊q⌋_w` below the argument, the upper end widened by `2^(2−w)`), `lnFloorApprox`, `lnRatApprox`, `lnP1Approx`. |
| `AzFloat/Ln` | **The natural logarithm**: `lnPrecRound x p mode`, `ln x` — the certified Ziv loop (`zivLoopCertified`, no fuel) on `lnApprox`, total because `ln x` is irrational for every float `x ≠ 1` and the bracket shrinks; IEEE/MPFR special values (`ln NaN = NaN`, `ln ∞ = ∞`, `ln 0 = −∞`, negatives `NaN`, `ln 1 = 0` exactly). Tests: `AzFloat/Tests/Ln`. |
| `AzFloat/Equiv/LnBracket` | The relative-error invariant `RelEncloses a b n δ s` of the interval AGM (`relEncloses_agmStep`: one step adds `8·2^(−w)` to `δ`, by homogeneity; `relEncloses_agmIterN`), the step count (`agm_log_gap`: gap `≤ 8·2^(−5h)` after `lnSteps`, from `agm_log_iterations` and two quadratic steps), the scaling (`ln_scale`), the AGM–logarithm bounds (`agmLog_bounds`), the pure-real width budget `ln_final_bounds`, `lnBracket_spec` (enclosure of `ln x`, width `≤ lnEps e w = 2^40 (w + |e| + 8)^5 2^(−w)` for `w ≥ 64`), `tendsto_lnEps`, `exists_rat_of_toVal`, `lnApprox_spec` (strict, via `irrational_log_rat`) and the Ziv certificate `lnApprox_isSome_exists`. Also the brackets from a float below the argument: `eq_finite_true_of_toVal_pos`, `lnEps_mono`, `lnRatEps`/`tendsto_lnRatEps`, the generic `lnFloorBracket_spec` (float ends, for `y ≤ q ≤ y (1 + 2·2^(−w))`; the exponent of `y` is bounded by `⌊2 |ln q|⌋ + 2` via `(e − 1) ln 2 ≤ ln ⌊q⌋_w ≤ ln q < (e + 1) ln 2`; `ln (q/y) ≤ q/y − 1 ≤ 4·2^(−w)`), `lnFloorApprox_spec` (strict, `Rounds`), its instances `lnRatApprox_spec` (`y = ⌊q⌋_w`) and `lnP1Approx_spec` (`y = ⌊1 + x⌋_w`, `irrational_log_one_add_of_toVal`) with the certificates `lnRatApprox_isSome_exists`, `lnP1Approx_isSome_exists`; `toRat_pos_of_sign`, `toRat_neg_of_sign`, `toRat_eq_one_of_num_eq_den`, `toRat_ne_one_of_num_ne_den` on `AzRat`. |
| `AzFloat/Equiv/Ln` | `Spec.log` (extended-real logarithm: `NaN` on negatives and `−∞`, `log 0 = −∞`, `log ∞ = ∞`) and `lnPrecRound_eq_liftVal : lnPrecRound x p mode = liftVal Spec.log x p mode` — `ln` is correctly rounded in every mode, without fuel. |
| `AzFloat/LnRat` | **The natural logarithm of a rational**: `lnRatPrecRound q p mode`, `lnRat q p` — the certified Ziv loop on `lnRatApprox` (in `AzFloat/LnBracket`: `lnFloorApprox` of the float `⌊q⌋_w` — `ln` by one AGM run of `lnBracket`, the upper end widened by `2^(2−w) ≥ ln (q/⌊q⌋_w)`), total because `ln q` is irrational for every rational `q ≠ 1`; `ln 0 = −∞`, negatives `NaN`, `ln 1 = 0` exactly. Tests: `AzFloat/Tests/LnRat`. |
| `AzFloat/Equiv/LnRat` | `lnRatPrecRound_eq : lnRatPrecRound q p mode = roundVal p mode (Spec.log q)` for `p ≠ 0` — `ln` of a rational is correctly rounded in every mode, without fuel. |
| `AzFloat/LnP1` | **`ln (1 + x)`** (MCA's `log1p`): `lnP1PrecRound x p mode`, `lnP1 x` — the certified Ziv loop on `lnP1Approx` (`lnFloorApprox` of `⌊1 + x⌋_w`: one rounded addition, one AGM run of `lnBracket`, the upper end widened by `2^(2−w)`), started at `p + 64 + leadingZeroBits e` so that small `|x|` keeps its leading bits in `1 + x`; total since `ln (1 + x)` is irrational for every float `x ≠ 0`. `lnP1 0 = 0` exactly, `lnP1 (−1) = −∞`, `NaN` below `−1`, `lnP1 (+∞) = +∞`. Tests: `AzFloat/Tests/LnP1`. |

| `AzNat/` | Computable multi-limb natural numbers over `Array UInt64`. Three-way multiplication (schoolbook / Karatsuba / Toom-Cook 3, with configurable basecase thresholds) and matching three-way `square`. Division: schoolbook `divMod` (Möller–Granlund reciprocal steps from `UInt64/`, with single- and double-limb divisor fast paths) for one- and two-limb divisors, and for longer divisors the recursive divide-and-conquer division of MCA Algorithm 1.8 (`Div/Recursive`, products through the full multiplication ladder) falling back to schoolbook below `divDispatchThreshold` quotient limbs; `divModWith threshold` exposes the threshold (`divMod`/`div`/`mod` use the default), with `divModWith_toNat` for every threshold and `divModWith_eq_divMod` (threshold confirmed by the fixed-size `az_nat_div_cross` benchmark); integer square root with remainder (`sqrtRem`); proven perfect-square test (`isSquare`, mod-256 quadratic-residue filter on the low byte + `square (sqrt n) == n` fallback). Newton–Barrett division (`Div/Newton`: an approximate reciprocal by Newton's iteration, MCA Algorithm 3.5, then Barrett's quotient step, MCA Algorithm 2.5, block by block — `O(M(n))`) with the FFT-range tricks of MCA §3.4.1/§2.4.1 (`Mul/SSCached`: cached forward transforms, products in the Fermat ring) and with Mulders' short products below it (`Mul/ShortProduct`, MCA §3.3) is dispatched from `Div` for long quotients (`useNewtonDiv`: divisor `≥ 32768` limbs and dividend `≥ 2×`, `≥ 8192` and `≥ 3×`, `≥ 4096` and `≥ 4×`, or `≥ 1024` and `≥ 8×`, measured 2026-10-19 with `az_nat_div_newton`, 56–91 % of the classical time); the classical dispatcher is `Div/Classic`. Exponentiation `pow` (sliding-window) and `powBinary` (binary, for benchmarking), both using the fast `square`. GCD (`gcd`, dispatching on size): Euclid on `UInt64` for single limbs and the binary GCD (Stein's algorithm, `Gcd/Binary`, bulk trailing-zeros shifts) up to `halfBinaryGcdThreshold` bits, then the **half-binary GCD** (`Gcd/HalfBinary`, MCA §1.6.3: `binaryDivide`, `halfBinaryGcd` on `AzInt` with a two-word base case (`Word2` operands, `Int64` cofactors with a runtime magnitude check), and the driver `gcdHalfBinary` — quadratic word rounds (the LSB analogue of MCA's DoubleDigitGcd, ≈ 57 bits per round, each output by one fused `linCombShift` pass with the shift folded in, plus a short two's-complement fix-up for negative differences) up to `halfBinaryGcdQuadraticThreshold` bits, the subquadratic `k = n/2` recursion above). Rounded division and right shift (`divRound`, `shiftRightRound`) correct against the `Rounding/` spec. Bit-level toolkit (`testBit`/`setBit`/`clearBit`/`getBits`, `modPow2`, `lowMask`, `size`, `trailingZeros`, `isMultipleOfPow2`). Base-`b` string conversion and parsing (`toStringBase`/`parseBase`) via base-10¹⁹ limb digits (`DivMod10p19`, `LimbDigits`). Also provides a `ParsableElement` instance for use as an `AzVector` / `AzMatrix` coefficient type. The verified `CommSemiring` instance routes all its data through the fast operations: `npow` is the sliding-window power, `natCast` is the limb-level `AzNat.ofNat` (not the default unary cast), and `n • a` is one cast plus one multiplication (not `n` additions). |
| `AzInt/` | Computable integers as a sign-magnitude pair `(sign : Bool, abs : AzNat)` with a canonical zero invariant (`abs = 0 → sign = true`). Includes conversions to/from all Lean fixed-width int/uint types and `AzNat`, comparison against `UInt64`/`Int64`/`AzNat`, a custom `compare` with derived `Ord`/`LE`/`LT`/`Max`/`Min`, full ring arithmetic (add/sub/mul over `AzNat` magnitudes), Euclidean division `edivMod`, shifts (`shiftLeft`/`shiftRight`) and rounded division/right-shift (`divRound`/`shiftRightRound`, correct against the `Rounding/` spec), parity tests, `pow2`, `lowMask`, `isPowerOfTwo`, exponentiation `pow` (sign rule over `AzNat`'s sliding-window power, also the `CommRing`'s `npow`), bit-size, trailing-zeros, parsing, `toString`, and a `ParsableElement` instance (so `AzInt` works as a coefficient type for `AzVector` / `AzMatrix` `parseStr`). The verified `CommRing` instance routes all its data through the fast operations: `NatCast`/`IntCast` are the limb-level `AzNat.ofNat`/`ofInt` (with `toInt_natCast`/`toInt_intCast`, not the default unary casts), and `n • z` / `i • z` are one cast plus one multiplication (not `n` additions). |
| `AzRat/` | Computable rationals in lowest terms: a `(sign : Bool, num : AzNat, den : AzNat)` with proofs `den ≠ 0`, `num = 0 → sign = true` (canonical zero), and `AzNat.coprime num den` (reduced). Computable conversions `toRat`/`ofRat` to/from `ℚ` (`toRat` builds the `ℚ` directly via `Rat.mk'`, the AzRat invariants being exactly `Rat.mk'`'s nonzero-denominator and coprimality requirements via `AzNat.coprime_iff`) with round-trip equivalence both ways (`toRat_ofRat`, `ofRat_toRat`, axiom-clean). Conversions `AzNat.toAzRat` (`n` as `n/1`), `AzInt.toAzRat` (`z` as `|z|/1` with `z`'s sign), and every primitive `UIntN`/`IntN`/`USize`/`ISize` (`.toAzRat`, delegating through `.toAzInt`) with correctness (`toRat_toAzRat`, `toRat_toAzRat_int`, and per-primitive `*.toRat_toAzRat` agreeing with `toNat`/`toInt` casts into `ℚ`). Pair constructors `ofAzNats`/`ofAzInts` (via the shared sign-aware core `ofSignAzNats`): reduce by a single `AzNat.gcd`, canonicalize zero, and map a zero denominator to `0` (the `mkRat` convention); correctness `toRat_ofAzNats`/`toRat_ofAzInts` (`= n / d` as field division in `ℚ`, no nonzero hypotheses needed). Canonical zero `0 : AzRat` and one `1 : AzRat` (`OfNat`/`Zero`/`One`/`Inhabited` instances mirroring `AzNat`/`AzInt`, with `toRat_zero`/`toRat_one`/`default_eq_zero` and the `ofRat_zero`/`ofRat_one` converses). String rendering `toChars`/`toString` (`"0"`, `"4"`, `"1/3"`, `"-1/3"`: sign on the numerator, no `"/den"` suffix for integers) and permissive parsing `parse` (unreduced fractions reduce, `"5/0" ↦ 0` per the `mkRat` convention; negative denominators and `"-0"` rejected), with the round-trip `parse_toString : parse (toString q) = some q`. Unary operations `neg` (the `Neg` instance), `abs`, and `inv` (the `Inv` instance, `0⁻¹ = 0`) — all sign/field-level with no limb arithmetic (`inv` swaps `num`/`den`, which preserves coprimality) — with correctness in both directions (`toRat_neg`/`toRat_abs`/`toRat_inv` and `ofRat_neg`/`ofRat_abs`/`ofRat_inv`). Multiplication (`Mul` instance) with **cross-gcd reduction**: exactly two `AzNat.gcd` calls on the cross pairs `(num x, den y)`/`(den x, num y)`, after which the product of the reduced parts is already in lowest terms (`coprime_cross_mul`) — no gcd of the products is ever computed; correctness `toRat_mul`/`ofRat_mul`. Division (`Div` instance, `x / 0 = 0`) duplicates the multiplication algorithm with the divisor's `num`/`den` roles swapped — no intermediate `y⁻¹` is allocated, yet `div_eq_mul_inv : x / y = x * y⁻¹` holds *structurally*; correctness `toRat_div`/`ofRat_div`. Shifts `<<<`/`>>>` (`HShiftLeft`/`HShiftRight` instances) scale by `2^n` with **no gcd at all**: the shift splits at `k = min n v₂(·)` (read off by `AzNat.trailingZeros`) into an exact limb shift on one component and a pure shift on the other (`coprime_shift`); correctness `toRat_shiftLeft`/`toRat_shiftRight` + `ofRat` converses. Addition (`Add` instance) with **cross-denominator gcd splitting**: `g = gcd(b,d)`; if `g = 1` the sum `(ad+bc)/(bd)` needs no reduction at all; otherwise the small numerator `t = a·d' + b'·c` over `g·b'·d'` is automatically coprime to `b'` and `d'`, so only `g₂ = gcd(t, g)` (small arguments) remains to divide out — signs via `combineSigned` (magnitude compare, cancellation to canonical zero); correctness `toRat_add`/`ofRat_add`. Subtraction (`Sub` instance) duplicates the addition algorithm with the subtrahend's sign flipped inline — no intermediate `-y` is allocated, yet `sub_eq_add_neg : x - y = x + (-y)` holds *structurally*; correctness `toRat_sub`/`ofRat_sub`. Exponentiation `pow`/`zpow` with **no reduction at all**: numerator and denominator are powered separately by `AzNat`'s sliding-window power (coprimes stay coprime under powers), the sign rule is `AzInt.pow`'s, and a negative exponent runs the same powers with the num/den roles swapped (the reciprocal of a reduced fraction is the swap, `0^-k = 0`) — installed as the `Field`'s `npow`/`zpow`, so `q ^ (n : ℕ)` and `q ^ (z : ℤ)` are `O(log n)` multiplications and `q.pow n = q ^ n` definitionally; correctness `toRat_pow`/`toRat_zpow` + `ofRat` converses. Rounding `AzRat.round q mode : AzInt × Ordering` (signed numerator over denominator via `AzInt.divRound`, accepting a `RoundingMode`; the `Ordering` records how the result compares to `toRat q`) with correctness `toInt_round` (value agrees with abstract `RoundingTarget.round intSet mode (toRat q)`) and `snd_round` (ordering tag = `compare` of rounded value vs `toRat q`). Base-2 logarithm `floorLogBase2Abs`/`ceilingLogBase2Abs : AzRat → ℤ` (limb-level via `AzNat.size` + `AzNat.normalizedCompare`, no GMP-backed `Nat.log2`) with correctness `floorLogBase2Abs_eq`/`ceilingLogBase2Abs_eq` (`= ⌊/⌈logb 2 |toRat q|⌋/⌉`, axiom-clean). Fast comparison `AzRat.cmp x y : Ordering` (staged, limb-level: sign → `|·|` vs 1 → num/den magnitudes → `floorLogBase2Abs` → cross-multiply, via `AzNat.compare`/`AzNat` mul) with correctness `cmp_eq_compare : cmp x y = compare (toRat x) (toRat y)`, backing the derived `Ord`/`LE`/`LT`/`Max`/`Min` instances and the verified `LinearOrder AzRat` (with `compare = cmp`, so deciding an inequality never computes in `ℚ`). Algebraic instances (`Instances.lean`): computable casts `NatCast`/`IntCast` (limb-level via `AzNat.ofNat`/`AzInt.ofInt` + `toAzRat`) and `RatCast`/`NNRatCast` (= `ofRat`) with `toRat_natCast`/`toRat_intCast`/`toRat_ratCast`, plus verified `Field` and `IsStrictOrderedRing` instances (every law transported from `ℚ` through `toRat`; all data fields are the existing verified operations — including `npow`/`zpow` = `pow`/`zpow` and `n • q` / `z • q` / `q • r` as one cast plus one multiplication — so no diamonds and no `O(n)` recursor defaults) — together with the `LinearOrder` this is the complete linearly-ordered-field API, and Mathlib's lattice `\|·\|` agrees with the constant-time `AzRat.abs` (`abs_eq`, oriented so `simp` normalizes to the `\|·\|` notation; `toRat_abs'`/`ofRat_abs'` restate the commutation facts in that normal form). `ParsableElement` and `ParsableCoeff` instances (both routing through the limb-level `toChars`/`parse`, with the proven char-set facts `mem_toChars_digit_or_dash_or_slash` etc.), so `AzRat` works as an entry type for `AzVector`/`AzMatrix` and as a coefficient type for `AzPolynomial`/`AzMvPolynomial` string conversion — e.g. `"[1/2, -1/3; 0, 2]"` parses as an `AzMatrix AzRat 2 2` and `"3/2*x^2-1/3*x+5"` as an `AzPolynomial AzRat`, both round-tripping. Scientific-notation output `toSci` (`Sci/`, `AzRat/ToSci.lean`), a port of the author's own Malachite `Rational::to_sci`: `SciOptions` (base `2`–`36`, rounding mode, `complete` / `precision p` / `scale s`, formatting flags) → `toSciNumber : AzRat → SciOptions → Option SciNumber` (sign, base, digits, scale; all the arithmetic: `floorLogBaseAbs`, `lengthAfterPoint`, one signed division) → `SciNumber.toString` (digits only); `toSciExact` is Malachite's `Exact` mode as a predicate. The inverse `fromSci` (`AzRat/FromSci.lean`, the author's `Rational::from_sci_string`) parses the notation in any base `2`–`36`: an optional exponent suffix (`e`/`E` for bases below `15`, a signed exponent after `e`/`E` from base `15` on, where `e` is a digit), at most one point, an optional sign and base-`b` digits, with the exponent confined to the `i64` range as in Malachite; `fromSci "123.4" = some 617/5`. `Pow2`: `isPowerOfTwo q` (positive, numerator and denominator powers of two — reduced, so `q = 2^k`) and `powerOfTwoExponent q = size num − size den` (`isPowerOfTwo_iff`; the value lemma `toRat_of_isPowerOfTwo` lives in `AzFloat/Equiv/Log2Bracket`); tests `AzRat/Tests/Pow2`. |
| `AzFloat/` | Arbitrary-precision binary floating point, representation after the author's Malachite `Float` (algorithms are not from Malachite, which is MPFR-derived; see `docs/azfloat_plan.md`): constructors `nan`, `infinity sign`, `zero` (no negative zero: with an unbounded exponent nothing underflows) and `finite sign exponent precision significand`, where the exponent is an `AzInt` (no minimum or maximum, no overflow or underflow), the precision a `Nat`, and the significand is **left-aligned** at a limb boundary (`FiniteValid`: bit length `alignedBits p = 64·⌈p/64⌉`, low `alignedBits p − p` bits zero); value `± m · 2^(e − m.size)`, so `2^(e−1) ≤ |x| < 2^e`. The smart constructor `mkFinite` left-aligns any significand of at most the requested precision (establishing the invariant once); classification (`isNaN`/`isInfinite`/`isZero`/`isFinite`/`isNormal`/`isPositive`/`isNegative`), accessors (`sign?`/`exponent?`/`precision?`/`significand?`), `neg`/`abs`, constants (`powerOf2`, `one`, `two`, `oneHalf`). Conversions: `ofAzNat`/`ofAzInt` (exact), `ofAzRatRound q p mode : AzFloat × Ordering` (first principles: the `p`-bit significand is the integer rounding of `q · 2^(p−1−⌊log₂\|q\|⌋)`, one signed division, with a carry to `2^p` halved) and `ofAzRat` (nearest), `toAzRat?` (exact value of a finite float). Precision changes (`Precision.lean`): `setPrecRound x p mode : AzFloat × Ordering` (raising is exact, lowering is one signed `shiftRightRound` of the padding-free significand with the carry to `2^p` halved), `setPrec` (nearest), `ulp?` (`2^(exponent − precision)` at precision 1). Comparison (`Compare.lean`): `partialCompare : AzFloat → AzFloat → Option Ordering` (`none` iff a `NaN` is involved; exponents first, then the left-aligned significands padded with whole zero limbs), and the Boolean `eqIEEE`/`lt`/`le`/`gt`/`ge` (all `false` on `NaN`; `=` stays structural and sees the precision). Shifts (`Shift.lean`): `shiftLeft`/`shiftRight` by an `AzInt` (notations `<<<`/`>>>`, also with a `Nat`) only move the exponent — exact, no rounding, no precision change, specials fixed. Debug format (`HexString.lean`), the author's Malachite `{:#x}` of a `ComparableFloat`: `toHexString` writes the exact value in hexadecimal with `#precision` (`0x1.0#1`, `0x0.8#5`, `-0x1.8#2`, `0xff.0#8`, `0x0.0050df15a4acf314#53`, `0x6.f70E+25#13`, `0x1.0E-250000000#1`; specials `NaN`, `Infinity`, `-Infinity`, `0x0.0`), with scientific layout when the hexadecimal exponent is `≤ −6` or exceeds the digit count, and `ofHexString` reads it back (strict: exactly the writer's language, either digit case). Decimal output (`ToString.lean`, the `ToString` instance): `toDecimalString` prints the **shortest round-tripping decimal** — the smallest `p` such that the exact value rounded to nearest at `p` significant decimal digits (`AzRat.toSci`) converts back to the float at its precision, found below the bound `⌈P·log₁₀ 2⌉ + 2` by galloping down from it and bisecting the last step (`searchLeastFromTop`, `searchLeast`, `expandUntil`), two or three probes in the usual case; each probe rounds the unreduced fraction `mantissa / 10^scale` with `ofFractionRound` instead of building a reduced `AzRat`, whose gcd dominated the cost; lossy by design, with the `.0` convention and `toSci`'s exponent layout (`1.0`, `0.5`, `0.3333333333333333`, `1.2676506002282294e30`, `8.0e-6`). `toDecimalAt x p` renders at a given `p`; `SciNumber.toAzRat` is the computable value of a `SciNumber`. Decimal input (`OfString.lean`): `ofDecimalStringRound s p mode` reads `AzRat.fromSci`'s language plus `NaN`/`Infinity`/`-Infinity` and rounds the exact rational with `ofAzRatRound`; `ofDecimalString` rounds to nearest. Output with options (`Sci.lean`): `toSci x o`/`toSciNumber` render the exact value with `AzRat.toSci`'s `SciOptions`. Literals (`Literals.lean`): `OfNat` (exact), `OfScientific` (nearest at `literalPrecision = 53`), `Repr` (the exact hexadecimal debug format). Lean's `Float` (`Float64.lean`): `ofFloat64` (exact, through the model's unpacked form) and `toFloat64 x mode` (53-bit rounding in the normal range, multiples of `2^-1074` below it, IEEE overflow by mode), assembled from `toUnpacked`/`finishUnpacked` and the model's `pack`. Arithmetic, one module per operation as for `AzInt` — `RoundScaled.lean` (shared primitives `roundScaled`, `normalizeCarry`, `roundFromFloor`, `combinedPrecision`), `Add.lean`, `Mul.lean`, `Div.lean`, `Sqrt.lean`, `Rsqrt.lean` (MCA §3.2–3.5 reorganized): `addPrecRound x y p mode : AzFloat × Ordering` and `subPrecRound` round the exact sum or difference once — `roundScaled` rounds an exact `±S · 2^w`; operands within `T + 1 = max(p_B, p) + 1` exponents of each other are combined exactly on the common scale (cancellation included), farther ones through the representative `8B ± 2^(e_B − T − 3)`, which rounds the same way as `B ± C` in every mode; `∞ + (−∞)` is `NaN`, a zero operand re-rounds the other. `Add`/`Sub` instances round to nearest at the larger operand precision. `mulPrecRound` multiplies the two cores in full and rounds once (MCA Algorithm FPmultiply, no truncation), `sqrPrecRound` squares with `AzNat.square`; `0 · (±∞)` is `NaN`; `Mul` instance and `sqr`. `divPrecRound` (MCA §3.4.2) shifts the dividend's core so the integer quotient has exactly `p` bits (one comparison of the aligned significands picks the shift), takes one `AzInt.divRound`, whose remainder decides the rounding and the comparison tag, and normalizes a carry with `normalizeCarry`; `∞ / ∞` and `0 / 0` are `NaN`, `x / 0 = ±∞` by the sign of `x`; `Div` instance. `sqrtPrecRound` (MCA §3.5 FPSqrt plus a nearest mode): scales the core so the integer square root `AzNat.sqrtRem` has exactly `p` bits, decides exactness from the remainder and the shifted-out bits and the midpoint from the exact comparison of `4M` with `(2s+1)²`, and rounds with the generic `roundFromFloor`; negatives give `NaN`, `√∞ = ∞`; `sqrt`. `sqrtRatPrecRound q p mode` (square root of an `AzRat` as a float, with the comparison tag): `e = ⌊log₂ (n/d)⌋` from the sizes and one normalized comparison, an even shift `2k` with `k = p − 1 − ⌊e/2⌋` applied to `n` or `d` so that `√(A/D) ∈ [2^(p−1), 2^p)`, floor = `AzNat.sqrt (A / D)` (one division, no reduction, since `⌊√⌊x⌋⌋ = ⌊√x⌋`), exactness `s²·D = A` and midpoint `4A ⋚ (2s+1)²·D` both by `AzNat.compareProduct`, then `roundFromFloor` and `normalizeCarry`; negatives give `NaN`. `rsqrtRatPrecRound q p mode`: the same core on the reciprocal fraction (`1/√(n/d) = √(d/n)` exactly), `0 ↦ +∞`. `rsqrtPrecRound` (MCA §3.5.1 by the same exact-integer method, with the scaling chosen by the exponent's parity and the power-of-two boundary case): `1/√x` correctly rounded, `0 ↦ +∞`, `+∞ ↦ 0`; `rsqrt`. `ofFractionRound s num den p mode` rounds an unreduced fraction with the same machinery (no gcd; used by the decimal round-trip test). **Mixed operations with `AzRat` by Ziv's strategy** (`Ziv.lean`, MCA §3.1.10 and Algorithm 3.1): `zivLoop approx p mode fuel w` brackets the exact result `v` at a working precision `w` between two exact values `l ≤ v ≤ h`, with `h = l` whenever the approximation is exact; the ends are not materialized but given by their rounding procedures (`Roundable`: the correct rounding at any precision in any mode, which the exact operations of the library provide cheaply in every regime). `roundingPossible` is MCA's RoundingPossible in bracket form — rounding is determined when no boundary (no float of precision `p + 1`, which covers the floats of precision `p` and the midpoints) lies in `(l, h]`, i.e. when the two ends have the same `(p + 1)`-bit truncation, the result being the rounding of `h` — and otherwise `w` doubles. The fuel is proven sufficient for each operation, so there is no exact fallback (`zivGuardBits = 64`; `zivFuel W`, the bit length of `W`, is the number of doublings reaching the working precision `W`; `truncError` is the one-ulp error of a truncation, `0` if exact, so that a truncation plus its error is the next float up). `AddSubRat.lean`: `addRatPrecRound x q p mode` (`x + q` correctly rounded; at working precision `w` the bracket is `x + lo ≤ x + q ≤ x + hi` with `lo` the `w`-bit truncation of `q` — the only division — and `hi` the next float up, both ends rounded by `addPrecRound`; the first working precision `zivStart` is `p + 64`, or the numerator's bit length for a dyadic `q`, which is then exact at once; the fuel `addRatFuel` reaches the working precision `p + |num| + 2|den| + |m| + |e| + 2`, from which rounding is always possible), `subRatPrecRound` (`x − q`), `ratSubPrecRound` (`q − x`); the `+`/`−` instances between an `AzFloat` and an `AzRat` round to nearest at the float's precision (`ratOpPrecision`: `1` for a special float, as `combinedPrecision` does — so `zero + 1/3 = 1/4`). `MulRat.lean` and `DivRat.lean`: for a product or a quotient the exponent of `x` factors out as a pure shift — `x · q = ±(m · num / den) · 2^(e − |m|)`, `x / q = ±(m · den / num) · 2^(e − |m|)`, `q / x = ±(num / (den · m)) · 2^(|m| − e)` — so `mulRatPrecRound`, `divRatPrecRound` (`x / q`) and `ratDivPrecRound` (`q / x`) are one `ofFractionRound` of the small unreduced fraction (a single division, no gcd) followed by an exact shift, with no approximation loop (Ziv is needed only for sums, whose exact value involves the exponent); special values follow `Spec.mul`/`Spec.div`; `*` and `/` instances at the float's precision. **Constants** (`Constants.lean`, after the author's Malachite `Float` constants): functions of a destination precision and a rounding mode returning the correctly rounded constant and its comparison tag — the quadratic irrationals `sqrt2PrecRound`, `sqrt3PrecRound`, `sqrt5PrecRound` (square roots of exact floats), `cbrt2PrecRound` (`∛2`, the cube root of the exact float `two`), `sqrt2Over2PrecRound`, `sqrt3Over3PrecRound`, `sqrt5Over5PrecRound` (`√n/n = 1/√n`, reciprocal square roots), and the golden ratio `phiPrecRound` (`φ = (1 + √5)/2`, through Ziv's loop with the bracket `(1 + l)/2 ≤ φ ≤ (1 + h)/2` from a truncation `l ≤ √5 ≤ h` and its successor, both ends exact floats plus one half; rounding is always possible from working precision `2p + 6` since `φ` is irrational and at distance at least `2^(−2p−4)` from every boundary), each with a nearest-rounding companion (`sqrt2 p`, …, `phi p`). The Prouhet–Thue–Morse constant (`ProuhetThueMorse.lean`, one of Malachite's just-for-fun constants): `τ = Σ tₙ/2^(n+1) = 0.0110100110010110…₂ ≈ 0.4124540336` with `tₙ` the Thue–Morse sequence (`prouhetThueMorseSeq`, the parity of the number of ones in `n`); its binary expansion *is* the sequence, and `t (64j + l) = t j xor t l`, so the first `64N` bits form an `AzNat` whose limbs take only two values, the word `0x6996966996696996` and its complement (`prouhetThueMorseLimb`, `prouhetThueMorseLimbs`); `prouhetThueMorsePrecRound p mode` extracts the first `p + 1` significant bits and rounds with `roundFromFloor`, the truncation being never exact and the value never a midpoint since the sequence is not eventually constant; `prouhetThueMorse p` rounds to nearest. The prime constant (`PrimeConstant.lean`): `ρ = Σ_{n prime} 2^(−n) = 0.011010100010100010…₂ ≈ 0.4146825099`, whose bit `n` is `1` iff `n` is prime; the first `64 N` bits are the prime sieve up to `64 N` (`primeSieve`, the existing bitmap sieve) read backwards, assembled word by word from its `testBit`s (`wordOfBits`, `primeConstantLimbs`), and `primeConstantPrecRound p mode` rounds like the Prouhet–Thue–Morse constant (never exact, never a midpoint, since there are infinitely many primes and composites); `primeConstant p` rounds to nearest. **Sums of lists** (`Sum.lean`, first principles): `sumPrecRound xs p mode : AzFloat × Ordering` rounds the exact sum of a list once — `NaN` for a `NaN` term or infinities of both signs, the infinity for one sign; otherwise the terms within `W = p + ⌈log₂ n⌉ + 3 + 64` exponents of the largest are summed exactly (`exactAdd`/`exactSum` at a precision holding the whole sum, `exactSumPrec`), the rest `R` is bounded by `T = 2^(e_max(R) + ⌈log₂ |R|⌉)` (`tailBound`); a window sum below `2^(p+4) T` has cancelled and replaces its terms (a shorter list, so the fuel is the length); otherwise the bracket `(S₁ − T, S₁ + T)`, with its ends rounded outward at precision `W` by `addPrecRound`, is handed to `roundingPossible`, and when a boundary lies inside — exactly one `(p + 1)`-bit float `b`, the truncation of the upper end, since the bracket is narrower than their spacing — the sign of the exact `(S₁ − b) + ΣR` (`signSum`, the same windowing) picks `b` or the midpoint of the half-bracket, which rounds like the sum. **Products of lists** (`Product.lean`): `productPrecRound xs p mode : AzFloat × Ordering` rounds the exact product once — `NaN` for a `NaN` factor or a zero with an infinity, the infinity with the sign of the product (`productSign`, the parity of the negative factors), zero for a zero; a finite nonzero product is formed exactly, since the value of a finite float is `±core · 2^scale` (`coreParts`): a balanced product tree of the cores (`AzNat.product`), a sum of the scales, and one `roundScaled`. The empty product is `1`. |
| `ExhaustiveGenerator/` | The `Azurite.ExhaustiveGenerator T` typeclass (a Lean port of Malachite's `exhaustive_*` iterators): a generator `gen : ℕ → Option T` bundled with the headline field `occurs_exactly_once : ∀ t, ∃! n, gen n = some t` — every value occurs at exactly one position. The `Option` codomain lets a generator be **infinite** (always `some`) or **finite** (eventually `none`, e.g. `UInt8` stops after `2^8` values). Two `@[reducible]` builders factor the proofs: `ExhaustiveGenerator.ofBijective (f : ℕ → T) (hf : Function.Bijective f)` (infinite, `gen n = some (f n)`, reuses existing bijectivity proofs) and `ExhaustiveGenerator.ofBoundedBij (card) (f) (hinj) (hsurj)` (finite, `gen n = if n < card then some (f n) else none`). The `ExhaustiveGenerator.firstN T n` helper (`(List.range n).filterMap gen`) previews the first `n` values, recovering the whole finite enumeration when `card ≤ n`. Instances (each named `<name>Gen`): `naturalsGen : ExhaustiveGenerator AzNat` (`0,1,2,…`, `f = AzNat.ofNat`, `naturals_bijective` from the `AzNat.toNat_ofNat`/`ofNat_toNat` round-trips). `PositiveNaturals` — `positiveNaturalsGen : ExhaustiveGenerator {n : AzNat // 0 < n}` (`1,2,3,…`, `ofNat_succ_pos` via the `AzNat.lt_iff_toNat_lt` bridge, surjectivity via `Nat.sub_add_cancel`). `Integers` — three half-line generators `positiveIntegersGen`/`nonnegativeIntegersGen`/`negativeIntegersGen` (subtypes `{z // 0 < z}`/`{z // 0 ≤ z}`/`{z // z < 0}`) plus the two zig-zag generators `integersGen : ExhaustiveGenerator AzInt` (`0,1,-1,2,-2,…`, the full type incl. `0`) and `nonzeroIntegersGen : ExhaustiveGenerator {z // z ≠ 0}` (`1,-1,2,-2,…`) — all on `AzInt.ofInt` with bijectivity from the `AzInt.toInt_ofInt`/`ofInt_toInt` round-trips and `AzInt.lt_iff_toInt_lt`/`le_iff_toInt_le` bridges (`split_ifs <;> omega` on parity for the zig-zags, `by_cases`/`omega` over `Int.toNat` for the surjective witnesses). `Unsigneds` — the FINITE generators `uint8Gen`/`uint16Gen`/`uint32Gen`/`uint64Gen` (Malachite `exhaustive_unsigneds`), each `ofBoundedBij (2^width) UIntXX.ofNat` with `hinj` from `UIntXX.toNat_ofNat'` and `hsurj` witness `t.toNat` via `UIntXX.toNat_lt`/`UIntXX.ofNat_toNat`; e.g. `firstN UInt8 300` has length `256` (stops at cardinality, demonstrating the finite path). The dependent `ExhaustiveGenerator.ofBoundedBijOn` builder (value `f n h` may use the in-range proof `h : n < card`) powers the FINITE monotone half-line generators over the fixed-width integers: `Signeds` (Malachite `exhaustive_natural_signeds`/`negative_signeds`/`positive_signeds`) has `nonnegative`/`negative`/`positive` × `Int8/16/32/64` (12 instances: `nonnegativeInt8Gen`…`positiveInt64Gen`, subtypes `{x // 0 ≤ x}`/`{x // x < 0}`/`{x // 0 < x}`, cards `2^(w-1)`/`2^(w-1)`/`2^(w-1)-1`), and `Unsigneds` adds `positiveUInt8Gen`…`positiveUInt64Gen` (`{x // 0 < x}`, card `2^w-1`). Three generic `positiveSignedGen`/`nonnegativeSignedGen`/`negativeSignedGen` builders (plus `positiveUnsignedGen`) do each proof once over an abstract `T` given its `toInt`/`ofInt` (or `toNat`/`ofNat`), an in-range round-trip `canon` (per width: `IntX.toInt_ofInt`'s `bmod` reduced by `Int.bmod_eq_of_le`; `UIntX.toNat_ofNat'`'s `%` by `Nat.mod_eq_of_lt`), range bounds `IntX.toInt_lt`/`le_toInt` (`UIntX.toNat_lt`), and the order↔`toInt`/`toNat` bridge `IntX.lt_iff_toInt_lt`/`le_iff_toInt_le` (`UIntX.lt_iff_toNat_lt`); each width is a one-line application. `Signeds` also has the two ZIG-ZAG signed generators (Malachite `exhaustive_signeds`/`nonzero_signeds`): `int8Gen`…`int64Gen : ExhaustiveGenerator IntX` (`0,1,-1,2,-2,…,MAX,-MAX,MIN`, full type) and `nonzeroInt8Gen`…`nonzeroInt64Gen : ExhaustiveGenerator {x // x ≠ 0}` (`1,-1,2,-2,…,MAX,-MAX,MIN`), built by the generic `zigZagSignedGen`/`zigZagNonzeroSignedGen`. These handle the ASYMMETRIC signed range `[-2^(w-1), 2^(w-1)-1]` (one extra negative): the symmetric zig-zag covers `[-MAX, MAX]` and `MIN = IntX.minValue` lands alone at the final index (via `IntX.toInt_minValue = -2^(w-1)`); injectivity/surjectivity reduce to `ℤ` through a `key` bridge (`toInt` of the finite value = the intended `ℤ`) then `split_ifs <;> omega`, the `MIN`-branch disjoint from the zig-zag branch since `MIN < -MAX`. `Count` proves how many elements each generator produces (from `occurs_exactly_once`, the count is `|T|`): the 7 infinite generators get `<name>Gen_infinite : Infinite T` (via `Infinite.of_injective` + the existing `_bijective` lemmas), and the 28 finite ones get `<name>Gen_card : Fintype.card T = N` (`N = 2^w`, `2^w-1`, `2^(w-1)`, or `2^(w-1)-1`). The ambient `Fintype` instances are Mathlib's own computable ones (`FinEnum`-derived for `UIntX`/`IntX`, `Subtype.fintype` for the order subtypes); each `<gen>_card` is a one-line application of the bridge `fintypeCard_eq_finiteCard` to the generator's `FiniteGenerator` data (below), on top of the framework lemmas `ExhaustiveGenerator.equivFin` (the finite `gen` shape yields `T ≃ Fin N`) and `fintypeCard_eq` (instance-independent card read-off, no `decide`). `Basic` also defines the two composition mixins: proof-only `Contiguous` (the `some`s form an initial segment) and `FiniteGenerator` (the finite BOUND as literal DATA `card` plus `gen_none`/`gen_some` bound proofs — the runtime radix for compositional generators, since even a computable `Fintype.card UInt64` would materialize a `2^64`-element `univ`; implies `Contiguous` via `FiniteGenerator.toContiguous`), with shape-keyed builders `FiniteGenerator.ofBoundedBij`/`ofBoundedBijOn`/`ofListNodup`/`map` giving every finite base generator a one-line instance carrying its literal bound (`2^w`, `2^w-1`, `2^(w-1)`, `2^(w-1)-1`, list length). `ZMods` supplies the modular-integer generators — `azZModGen : ExhaustiveGenerator (AzZMod m)` (residues `0..m-1`, card `m.toNat`) and `azZModPow2Gen : ExhaustiveGenerator (AzZModPow2 k)` (card `2^k`), each with `FiniteGenerator` data — the Azurite-native `exhaustive_unsigneds` analogue for the computable coefficient rings (no Malachite counterpart exists). Both types carry their canonicity proof (`⟨val, isLt⟩`), so `ofBoundedBijOn` constructs each residue DIRECTLY with no modular reduction; bijectivity = the `AzNat.toNat/ofNat` round-trips. Guards: base tables + caps, `AzZMod 3 × AzNat` (capped fair pair), `AzZMod 3 × AzZModPow2 1` (finite × finite), and **the chapter's motivating shape: `List (AzZMod 3)` — fair streams of polynomial coefficient lists over `ZMod p`, resolving through the finite rail of `exhaustive_vecs`** (same table as `List Ordering` under the card-3 relabeling — the fair machinery depends only on the cards). `Enums` ports four tiny explicit enumerations (Malachite `exhaustive_bools`/`orderings_increasing`/`exhaustive_orderings`/`exhaustive_rounding_modes`) via the `ExhaustiveGenerator.ofListNodup` builder (`gen n = l[n]?`, existence from completeness, uniqueness from `List.getElem?_inj` on a `Nodup` list): `boolsGen : ExhaustiveGenerator Bool` (`[false, true]`), `orderingsIncreasingGen`/`orderingsGen : ExhaustiveGenerator Ordering` (`[.lt,.eq,.gt]` and Equal-first `[.eq,.lt,.gt]`; the latter a named non-instance `def`), and `roundingModesGen : ExhaustiveGenerator RoundingMode` (Azurite's five-variant `RoundingMode` — no Malachite `Exact` — as `[.Down,.Up,.Floor,.Ceiling,.Nearest]`); `Nodup`/completeness by `decide` (`cases`+`decide` for `RoundingMode`). Counts `boolsGen_card = 2`, `orderingsIncreasingGen_card`/`orderingsGen_card = 3`, `roundingModesGen_card = 5` via `fintypeCard_eq_finiteCard` (`Fintype Bool`/`Ordering` exist in Mathlib; `Fintype RoundingMode` is a computable hand-written instance here). `Ranges` ports the two parametrized increasing range constructors (Malachite `primitive_int_increasing_range`/`primitive_int_increasing_inclusive_range`): given runtime bounds `(a b)`, `<type>RangeGen a b : ExhaustiveGenerator {x // a ≤ x ∧ x < b}` and `<type>RangeInclusiveGen a b : ExhaustiveGenerator {x // a ≤ x ∧ x ≤ b}` ascend `a, a+1, …` over all 8 fixed-width types (`int8RangeGen`…`uint64RangeInclusiveGen`, 16 wrappers). Four generic builders (`increasingRange{Signed,Unsigned}Gen`, `…Inclusive…`) via `ofBoundedBijOn` with the value carrying its membership proof; the EMPTY range (`a ≥ b`) needs no special case — card `0`, `gen ≡ none`, surjectivity vacuous. Each has a derived parametrized `Fintype` instance and a count `<type>RangeGen_card a b : Fintype.card … = (b−a)` (`.toNat` of `b.toInt − a.toInt`, `+1` inclusive; unsigned via `b.toNat − a.toNat`, inclusive `b.toNat + 1 − a.toNat` — the `+1` before the ℕ subtraction so reversed ranges give `0`). It also has the two MAGNITUDE-ordered signed range constructors (Malachite `exhaustive_signed_range`/`exhaustive_signed_inclusive_range`): `int8SignedRangeGen`…`int64SignedRangeGen` and `…SignedRangeInclusiveGen` enumerate the same range SET as the ascending versions but sorted by the key `(|x|, positive-first)` — realized by `List.mergeSort`ing the ascending list then `ofListNodup`, with `Nodup`/completeness transferred through `List.mergeSort_perm` (framework lemmas `nodup_range_filterMap_gen`/`mem_range_filterMap_gen`). Their counts reuse the ascending `…RangeGen_card` directly (order-independent, identical subtype). `AzRanges` adds ascending range iterators over the arbitrary-precision `AzNat`/`AzInt` (Malachite `exhaustive_natural_range`/`_inclusive_range`/`_range_to_infinity` and `integer_increasing_range`/`_inclusive_range`/`_range_to_infinity`/`decreasing_range_to_negative_infinity`) — simpler than the fixed-width ones since `ofNat`/`ofInt` round-trip unconditionally (no `MAX`/`bmod`): bounded `azNatRangeGen`/`azNatRangeInclusiveGen`/`azIntIncreasingRangeGen`/`azIntIncreasingRangeInclusiveGen` (finite, `ofBoundedBijOn`, with derived `Fintype` instances + `…_card` counts) and infinite `azNatRangeToInfinityGen`/`azIntIncreasingRangeToInfinityGen` (`[a, ∞)` ascending) plus `azIntDecreasingRangeToNegativeInfinityGen` (`(-∞, b]` descending `b, b-1, …`) — the three via `ofBijective` with `…_infinite : Infinite {…}` theorems. It also has the four MAGNITUDE-ordered `AzInt` ranges (Malachite `exhaustive_integer_range`/`_inclusive_range`/`_range_to_infinity`/`_range_to_negative_infinity`, key `(|x|, positive-first)`): bounded `azIntRangeGen`/`azIntRangeInclusiveGen` (via `List.mergeSort` of the ascending list + `ofListNodup`, counts reused from the ascending versions) and INFINITE `azIntRangeToInfinityGen` (`[a, ∞)`) / `azIntRangeToNegativeInfinityGen` (`(-∞, b]`), whose value functions dispatch on the bound's sign — monotone when the ray is one-sided, else interleave `0,+1,-1,…` up to the finite side then continue linearly — with bijectivity proved by phase/parity `split_ifs <;> omega` on the `ℤ` formula (`toInfF`/`toNegInfF`), giving `…_infinite` theorems. `LexPairs` ports the first COMPOSITIONAL generator (Malachite `lex_pairs`): given `gA : ExhaustiveGenerator A` (possibly infinite) and a FINITE `gB`, `ExhaustiveGenerator.lexPairGen`/`lexPairGenOfFinite` enumerate the newtype `LexPair A B` (a distinct `A × B` so it can carry its own generator) in lexicographic order with the LAST coordinate varying fastest — a mixed-radix odometer `k ↦ ⟨gA(k/cB), eB(k%cB)⟩`; the finite component travels as a plain computable function `Fin cB → B` plus a `Prop`-level `Function.Bijective` (NEVER the `noncomputable` `equivFin`, which would poison `#eval`), with `card = |A|*|B|`. `Basic` also has the general transport combinator `ExhaustiveGenerator.mapGen (f : S → T) (hf : Function.Bijective f) (g)` (`gen k := (g.gen k).map f`; relabel a generator along a computable bijection, `f` DATA + `hf` Prop). `LexTuples` ports `lex_triples`/`lex_quadruples` as the FLAT newtypes `LexTriple A B C`/`LexQuadruple A B C D` (faithful to Malachite's flat tuples) built by NESTING `LexPair`s and relabeling via `mapGen flattenTriple`/`flattenQuad` — reusing the pair odometer wholesale, no new mixed-radix arithmetic. The enabler is `lexPairGen_contiguous`/`lexPairGenOfFinite_contiguous` (in `LexPairs`): a composed pair generator of contiguous generators is itself contiguous (bound `cA*cB`), so the inner `LexPair …` generator can serve as the finite second argument of the next `lexPairGen` out — hence ALL BUT THE FIRST component must be finite (`B,C[,D]`), the first (`A`) may be infinite. The pair/triple/quadruple/vec generators are also genuine INSTANCES (`instExhaustiveGeneratorLexPair` etc.), gated on `[FiniteGenerator]` for the non-first components — the literal `card` is the odometer radix, keeping the instances computable — with composite `FiniteGenerator` instances (product cards) so composition nests; `instExhaustiveGeneratorLexPair_gen_eq` proves the instance path matches the `Fintype`-witness builder path (`finiteBound_none`/`finiteBound_some` from `[Fintype]+[Contiguous]` remain as the theorem-layer bridge). Counts `LexTriple.card = |A|*|B|*|C|` / `LexQuadruple.card = |A|*|B|*|C|*|D|` (via flat `equivProd`), `Infinite` when `A` is. `LexDepPairs` ports `lex_dependent_pairs` as a DEPENDENT-pair (Σ-type) generator over the newtype `LexDepPair A B` (`fst : A`, `snd : B fst`, `B : A → Type*`), where the second component's TYPE depends on the first's VALUE — for each `a` from `gA` (possibly infinite, slowest coordinate) a finite `a`-specific block of `B a` values is enumerated, then the next `a`, lexicographically. Block sizes VARY per `a` ("ragged"), so the odometer is a RAGGED prefix sum `S(i) = Σ_{j<i} card(dB aⱼ)` (via `blockSum`) rather than a fixed radix. Each per-`a` block is packaged computably as `DepFinGen B a` (a `card`, a `pos : 0 < card` REQUIRING nonempty blocks, a plain `enum : Fin card → B a`, and a `Prop` `bij`). `ExhaustiveGenerator.lexDepPairGen gA dB hA` walks the odometer via a FUEL recursion `depFind` (fuel `k+1` always resolves: nonempty blocks make the remaining index strictly decrease); `hA` is the `contig`-step hypothesis on `gA` (a `none` at `n` forces `none` at `n+1`), needed because the walk STOPS at the first `none` — every Azurite base generator supplies it via `Contiguous.contig`. `occurs_exactly_once` is the ragged-sum bijection `ℕ ≃ Σ a, Fin (dB a).card`: existence via `depFind_hit_gen` (induction on the block gap) and uniqueness via `depFind_decode` (induction on fuel) + `gA`-index uniqueness + `enum` injectivity, with the key arithmetic lemma `le_blockSum_of_some` (`i ≤ S(i)` across nonempty `some`-blocks). Counts `LexDepPair.card = ∑ a, |B a|` (via `equivSigma` + `Fintype.card_sigma`), `Infinite` when `A` is and all fibers nonempty. Its composition kit: `lexDepPairGen_contig_step`/`lexDepPairGen_contiguous` (the walk preserves the contig step, via fuel-irrelevance `depFind_fuel_irrel` + `depFind_none_step`), exact finite bounds `lexDepPairGen_gen_none`/`gen_some` at the total block count `blockSum gA dB cA` when `gA` is finite (via the miss lemma `depFind_miss_gen` + block locator `exists_block`), packaged as `lexDepPairGen_finiteGenerator`; `DepFinGen.card_eq` bridges each block size to `Fintype.card (B a)`, and `DepFinGen.ofBounded` (via `boundedEnum`/`boundedEnum_bijective`) builds block data from ANY bounded generator of the fiber — reading the generator at `Fin`-positions, with bijectivity from `occurs_exactly_once` + the bounds. Empty blocks are NOT handled (would break strict-decrease termination). `ShortlexVecs` ports `shortlex_vecs` — all vecs over a finite NONEMPTY type in SHORTLEX order (shortest first, same-length vecs lexicographically) — as the newtype `ShortlexVec T` (wrapping `List T`, kept distinct from the bare list type so the later `exhaustive_vecs` port can claim its own default order). Faithful to Malachite's own structure (`ShortlexVecs` IS `LexDependentPairs<u64, Vec<T>>` projected to the second coordinate): `shortlexVecs g hpos hnone hsome = mapGen flattenShortlex _ (lexDepPairGen lengthsGen (shortlexBlocks …) …)` — the lengths `0,1,2,…` (local non-instance `lengthsGen : ExhaustiveGenerator ℕ`; `AzNat` stays the computational number type, lengths are structural `LexVec`-indices) as the slow coordinate, per-length blocks the `cT^n` length-`n` lex vecs read off each `lexVecData` level through `DepFinGen.ofBounded`, flattened by the genuine bijection `⟨n, v⟩ ↦ v.toList` (a list determines its length). Nonemptiness (`0 < cT`, from `[Nonempty T]` via the new `FiniteGenerator.card_pos`) is required by the nonempty-blocks rule; Malachite's empty-`xs` boundary (single empty vec) is the excluded degenerate case. INFINITE (`ShortlexVec.instInfinite`) + `Contiguous`, so it composes as an infinite first component. Guards: Malachite's 20-value `Bool` doctest VERBATIM, `Ordering` through length 2, `UInt8` deep spot-checks (`gen 300 = [0, 43]`, `gen (257 + 5*256 + 7) = [5, 7]`), builder = instance, and a `LexPair (ShortlexVec Bool) Bool` composite. `RulerSequence` ports `ruler_sequence` (OEIS A007814, `0,1,0,2,0,1,0,3,…`; the length iterator pacing Malachite's fair all-length vec enumeration): `rulerSequence n = twoAdicVal (n+1)` (the recursive strip-a-factor-of-two `trailing_zeros`), with the Mathlib spec `rulerSequence_eq_padicValNat` (`= padicValNat 2 (n+1)`, wedged between `2^v ∣ n+1` and `¬2^(v+1) ∣ n+1`) and ALL THREE documented properties PROVEN: **logarithmic growth** `rulerSequence_le_log` (`≤ Nat.log 2 (n+1)`, power form `two_pow_rulerSequence_le`, TIGHT at `rulerSequence (2^k - 1) = k`), **every value infinitely often** (`infinite_setOf_rulerSequence_eq`: value `k` sits at every `2^k - 1 + m*2^(k+1)`; unbounded form `exists_le_and_rulerSequence_eq`), and **ordered first occurrences** (`rulerSequence_lt_of_lt_two_pow`: below position `2^k - 1` only values `< k` appear, so `k` first appears at `2^k - 1`). Guards replay the Malachite doctest verbatim + tightness at `2^20 - 1` + occurrence/density spot-checks. `BitDistributorSequence` ports `bit_distributor_sequence` (slot `1` of a two-output BitDistributor — Malachite streams `get_output(1)` of `new(&[y, x])`): `bitDistributorSequence P n = P.deinterleave 1 n`, GENERIC over the 2-slot `BitAssignment` (so one def covers normal/normal of any weights + both tiny arrangements); `bitDistributorSequenceNormal p q` recovers Malachite's weighted argument order (`x` weight `p`, `y` weight `q` → weights vector `![q, p]`; `p = q = 1` is OEIS A059905), tiny configs via the existing `fairPairAssignmentTinyFirst`/`TinySecond` (Malachite's "panics if both tiny" is `tinyAssignment`'s `0 < normalCount` HYPOTHESIS — not a runtime possibility). **Every number occurs infinitely many times PROVEN at the `BitAssignment` level** (`BitAssignment.infinite_setOf_deinterleave_eq`: for ANY assignment/slot with another slot to absorb variation, `interleave` with slot `j` pinned to `k` and slot `i` sweeping lands injectively in the fiber — the same one-proof-serves-all pattern as `deinterleaveTuple_bijective`), instantiated as `infinite_setOf_bitDistributorSequence_eq` + unbounded form `exists_le_and_bitDistributorSequence_eq`. Guards: both 50-value Malachite doctests VERBATIM, A059905 prefix, both tiny tables, and the theorem made concrete at depth (`interleave ![10^6, 5] ↦ 5`). `ExhaustiveDepPairs` ports `exhaustive_dependent_pairs` — FAIR dependent pairs where fibers may be INFINITE (unlike `lexDepPairGen`'s finite blocks): a scheduling sequence `s` decides which fiber to advance, step `n` emitting fiber `s n`'s `occCount s (s n) n`-th value (occurrences of `s n` before `n` = prior pulls). Malachite's scheduler contract ("must generate every natural number infinitely many times; good generators … `ruler_sequence` or `bit_distributor_sequence`") is VERBATIM the `hs` hypothesis, certified by `exists_le_and_rulerSequence_eq` / `exists_le_and_bitDistributorSequence_eq` — the two theorems proven in those ports. `occurs_exactly_once` = schedule combinatorics: pair `(i, j)` realized at exactly the `(j+1)`-th occurrence of `i` (existence `exists_occCount_eq` by induction through least-next-occurrences; uniqueness `eq_of_occCount_eq` — later occurrences strictly grow the count). Output = the bare Σ-type with DEFAULT ruler-scheduled instance `instExhaustiveGeneratorSigma` (fair owns the type, lex is the `LexDepPair` newtype — the product precedent); NO finiteness/contiguity constraints, finite parts yield holes (the Rust instead REMOVES exhausted iterators and re-targets its scheduler — a stateful liveness optimization; same coverage, different post-exhaustion order, noted honestly in the header). Guards: Malachite's 50-pair multiples doctest VERBATIM through the Σ-instance (`{n : AzNat // 0 < n} × AzNat` displayed via `(j+1)·x` — the no-holes regime where port and Rust agree step for step), a genuinely dependent mixed-fiber table (`Bool → Ordering/AzNat`, holes skipped), and the bit-distributor-scheduled builder. `ExhaustiveVecs` ports `exhaustive_vecs` — ALL lists, fairly — as the composition Malachite itself uses (`ExhaustiveVecs` IS ruler-scheduled `ExhaustiveDependentPairs` of lengths × fair fixed-length vecs): `exhaustiveVecsFromLengthGen s hs gLen gV = mapGen flattenVec _ (exhaustiveDepPairGen …)` (generic scheduler + length generator = `exhaustive_vecs_from_length_iterator` restricted to honest length GENERATORS — Rust's repeating length iterators repeat vecs), `exhaustiveVecs` pins ruler + `lengthsGen`, and the DEFAULT instance lands on the bare `List T` (`instExhaustiveGeneratorList`; shortlex keeps the `ShortlexVec` newtype) gated on the single `[∀ n, ExhaustiveGenerator (List.Vector T n)]` — resolved by EITHER rail (infinite `T` fair vecs, finite `T` capped/compressed). **The length-0 fiber is FINITE (one empty vec) — the first composition exercising `exhaustiveDepPairGen`'s finite-fiber holes**: every even counter past 0 revisits the exhausted slot (guards pin `gen 2/4/6 = none`, 19 holes in 40 counters); finite `T` makes EVERY fiber finite (hole-dense stream, `List Ordering` table). Interleaving is OURS (the Rust REMOVES the exhausted length-0 iterator at step 2 and every scheduler index shifts — its third output is `[1]`, ours `[0,0]`); the per-length SUBSEQUENCES agree step for step and the guards pin them against the Rust doctest's fiber values verbatim (length-1 `[0]..[9]`, length-2 `[0,0],[0,1],[1,0],[1,1]`, length-3 prefix), plus a 200-counter `Nodup` occurs-once check. `ExhaustiveDepPairsSim` is the Rust-EXACT simulator (Level-1 fidelity artifact for differential testing against Malachite): the `ExhaustiveDependentPairs` state machine ported literally — the live fiber list with per-fiber cursors, the pull phase (`take` semantics, `xs`-end discovered by a short pull), the retry loop with `xs_yss.remove(i)` index shifts and `i %= len` re-targeting once `xs` is done — so `DepPairsSim.firstN` emits BIT-FOR-BIT the Rust stream. Inputs are raw iterator functions (`xs : ℕ → Option X`, fibers `X → ℕ → Option Y`) read with iterator semantics BY CONSTRUCTION (a `none` permanently ends its source: the cursor never advances past it, exhausted fibers are removed not re-read) — so NO contiguity hypotheses, and `xs` needn't be a generator (duplicates give fresh fibers, as in Rust). The inner loop is FUELED (`s n + live.length + slack + 2`): Rust's documented hang (all remaining fibers empty, infinite `xs`) is exactly where no finite slack suffices; the simulator marks the stream done there instead of spinning. No occurs-exactly-once claimed (Rust's stream violates it for repeating `xs` anyway). Guards, all VERBATIM: the `exhaustive_vecs` doctest in the RUST order (third output `[1]` — the re-targeted slot the clean port renders as a hole), the duplicated-`xs`/empty-fiber doctest with its repeated `(3, 300)` and exact 9-value termination (mid-call cascaded removals + `xs`-end + wraparound, structurally out of the clean port's reach), the no-exhaustion 50-pair doctest where simulator ≡ clean generator step for step, and a sim-vs-clean cross-check (`Nodup` + membership). `ExhaustiveDepPairsSimGen` is **Level 2: the Rust-exact stream as a PROVEN `ExhaustiveGenerator`** (`DepPairsSim.simGen`, 1700 lines — the largest single proof of the generator arc): `occurs_exactly_once` for the full removal/re-targeting semantics, under exactly the hypotheses the Rust forces and the clean port doesn't need — contiguity on both sides (iterator = generator semantics), NONEMPTY fibers (excluding the documented hang, which genuinely breaks coverage), and the scheduler certificate. Architecture: per-call fuel accounting (`fuelLB`, each retry permanently removes a fiber; a filled pull emits from a FRESH nonempty fiber — `phases_trichotomy` excludes the fourth case) makes the hang-marker branch unreachable; UNIQUENESS via the monotone witness `Iwit a iA c` (created + all live cursors ≥ c) — established by an emission through the invariant-parametric emission-source lemma `next_emit_inv` (every emission = a phase-image entry pulled at its cursor, `P`-transported for free), preserved by every step (appends read fresh `xs` indices), and blocking any repeat; EXISTENCE via creation at the first step scheduling `iA` (`next_created`/`next_fresh`: the fresh entry ends its call alive at cursor ≤ 1), then the CURSOR CLIMB — removal impossible below the target (fiber contiguity), positions nonincreasing (`next_entry`), so a **strong induction on the position** replaces the stabilization argument: the scheduler hit (`next_hit`) either lands (emission, cursor bump) or the position strictly dropped. Guards: Level 2 ≡ Level 1 value-for-value on the all-infinite ℕ-pairs config AND on the `exhaustive_vecs` config (the RUST order, `[1]` third — now emitted by a PROVEN generator), plus a 60-counter `Nodup`. `VecsLengthRange` ports the length-range pair — `shortlexVecsLengthRange a b` (Malachite `shortlex_vecs_length_range`: length `a` fully lexicographic, then `a+1`, …, through `b-1`, exhausted — doctest VERBATIM, finite first components being native to `lexDepPairGen`) and `exhaustiveVecsLengthRange a b` (`exhaustive_vecs_length_range` — **the bounded-degree polynomial shape**: coefficient lists of length `< b` = polynomials of degree `< b-1`, enumerated fairly) — both on the subtype `{l : List T // a ≤ l.length ∧ l.length < b}` via `natRangeGen a b : ExhaustiveGenerator {n : ℕ // a ≤ n ∧ n < b}` (`primitive_int_increasing_range` as a length iterator; `a ≥ b` = empty, matching Malachite's clamp) and range-tagged flattens. ORDER: the FINITE length iterator makes the Rust machine exhaust it and mod-wrap its scheduler (`i %= len`), so the fair interleaving differs from our absolute-slot order — the guards pin OUR order AND replay Malachite's doctest VERBATIM through the Rust-exact simulator (finite-xs exhaustion + wrap handled exactly). Bounded-degree guard: all 13 `AzZMod 3` coefficient lists of length `< 3`, then exhausted. `Polynomials` delivers **the chapter's goal: `azPolynomialGen : ExhaustiveGenerator (AzPolynomial R)` — every polynomial of every degree, exactly once, fairly** (ORIGINAL — Malachite has no polynomials). `AzPolynomial`'s canonicity (`back? ≠ some 0`) is built into the SHAPE, not filtered (filtering discards a `1/card` fraction per length — hopeless for large/infinite coefficient rings): a length-`(n+1)` vec with nonzero last entry IS a pair `List.Vector T n × {t // t ≠ 0}` (`snocNZ` bijection), so `nonzeroLastVecGen` — the constrained analogue of the fixed-length vec generator — draws ONLY the last coordinate from the nonzero elements. The polynomial generator = fair dependent pair of lengths and these fibers (length 0 = the zero polynomial), flattened onto the canonical-list subtype and transported (all-`rfl` `List ↔ Array` round-trips) onto `AzPolynomial R` itself. Gates: `[ExhaustiveGenerator {t : R // t ≠ 0}]` + the `(n+1)`-SHAPED `[∀ n, ExhaustiveGenerator (List.Vector R (n+1) × {t // t ≠ 0})]` — instance search cannot case-split an abstract `n`, so the `n = 0` pair (singleton vec side, finite even over infinite `T`) is built by hand (`pairZeroGen`) and the ∀-gate quantifies only over the uniform shapes. Nonzero-element instances provided for `AzZMod m`/`AzZModPow2 k` (residues `1..card-1`); `AzInt` already had one; plus the new `instInfiniteVector : Infinite (List.Vector T (n+1))`. Guards: nonzero residues, structural nonzero-last vecs, and the streams themselves — `AzZMod 3` (`0, 1, x, 2, x², 2x, x³, x+1, …`) and the infinite ring `AzInt` (`0, 1, x, -1, x², 2, -x, -2, x³, 3, x+1, …`), both resolved purely by instance search, plus 200-counter `Nodup`s AND the first 100 of each stream as guard-string-style `toString` tables through the real `AzPolynomial` printer (`instFactOneLtToNatOfNat` transports `Fact (1 < n)` onto literal `AzNat` moduli for the `ParsableCoeff` gates). **Bounded degree**: `azPolynomialDegreeLtGen b : ExhaustiveGenerator {p : AzPolynomial R // p.degree < (b : WithBot ℕ)}` — the polynomial fiber family over the length range `[0, b+1)` (`degree_lt_iff_size_le` bridges the `WithBot` bound to the size form; the flatten REUSES `flattenPoly`/`listToPoly` and their bijectivity, just re-packaged with the bound). Guards: the COMPLETE 27-polynomial space of `degree < 3` over `AzZMod 3` (`1 + 2 + 6 + 18`, then exhausted) and the `degree < 2` `AzInt` prefix — the bounded-candidate shape the factorization steps will draw from. `BitInterleave` supplies the m-way bit interleaving underlying Malachite's `BitDistributor`, refactored around an ABSTRACT slot assignment: `BitAssignment m` partitions the counter-bit positions `ℕ` among `m` slots via three maps `slotOf : ℕ → Fin m` / `rank : ℕ → ℕ` / `pos : Fin m → ℕ → ℕ` and four laws (`pos_slotOf`/`rank_pos`/`pos_rank` — `(slotOf, rank)` and `pos` are mutually inverse `ℕ ≃ (j : Fin m) × ℕ` — plus `pos_strictMono`, each slot's positions ascending; derived `le_pos : r ≤ pos j r` and `pos_inj`). `A.deinterleave j k`/`A.interleave f` are BOUNDED `Finset.range` bit sums (no recursion — bits at `r ≥ k.size` vanish since `pos j r ≥ r`), and the whole theory is proved ONCE against the laws: `testBit_deinterleave : (A.deinterleave j k).testBit r = k.testBit (A.pos j r)` and `testBit_interleave : (A.interleave f).testBit i = (f (A.slotOf i)).testBit (A.rank i)` (via `testBit_sum_pow` — bit-vector reading of a distinct-power sum, the double sum flattened by `Finset.sum_sigma'` — plus the reusable carry-free `testBit_add_two_pow_of_testBit_false`), the round-trips `deinterleave_interleave`/`interleave_deinterleave`, and the headline `BitAssignment.deinterleaveTuple_bijective : Function.Bijective A.deinterleaveTuple` (`ℕ ≃ (Fin m → ℕ)`) — so future assignments (weighted round-robin, tiny/ruler) get the bijection FREE by proving the four laws; capped slots (finite components) are the sibling `BitInterleaveCapped` (below). The first instance is `roundRobin m (0 < m)`: Malachite's all-normal weight-1 case, `slotOf i = m-1-(i%m)` (last slot owns bit 0), `pos j r = r*m + (m-1-j)`, laws by `%`/`/` arithmetic; `roundRobin_deinterleave_one` is the `m=1` identity. The `#guard`s pin Malachite's exact `BitDistributor` pairs doctest against `roundRobin 2` (slot 0 owns offset 1, slot 1 offset 0). Self-contained pure-ℕ theory (imports no generator machinery); it is the mathematical core for the fair `m`-way product enumerations to be built on top. `BitInterleaveCapped` is the CAPPED sibling for finite components: `CappedBitAssignment m` adds `cap : Fin m → Option ℕ` (`some b` = the slot owns EXACTLY `b` positions, `none` = infinitely many) and an `Option`-valued `slotOf : ℕ → Option (Fin m)` (`none` = unowned, possible only when ALL slots are capped), with the same four laws guarded by rank validity (`∀ b, cap j = some b → r < b`); `deinterleave`/`interleave` are the same bounded bit sums with the guard folded into the summand/summation range, and the bijection becomes the guarded quadruple `deinterleave_interleave` (under per-slot bounds `f j < 2^b`) / `interleave_deinterleave` (under `Valid k` = every set bit of `k` owned, decidable) / `interleave_valid` / the UNCONDITIONAL output bound `deinterleave_lt_two_pow : cap j = some b → deinterleave j k < 2^b`. The instance `cappedRoundRobin cap` is Malachite `update_bit_map`'s weight-1 DROP-OUT rotation (positions go to the active slots in decreasing cyclic order, slot `m-1` first; a slot leaves the rotation once its cap is used up — the `set_max_bits` doctest is the semantics oracle: the cap belongs to the slot INDEX, not the rotation order), formalized not as a scan but by aggregating into ROUNDS: slot `j` is active in round `t` iff its cap exceeds `t`, so `pos j r = roundStart r + offsetIn r j` with `roundStart`/`offsetIn`/`rankFun`/`slotOfFun` all computable `Finset`-count/`List.find?` expressions, and the laws follow from round arithmetic (`rankFun_eq` locates the round; in-round surjectivity via `Finset.surjOn_of_injOn_of_card_le`). With `cap ≡ none` it DEGENERATES to `roundRobin` (`cappedRoundRobin_pos_none`/`_slotOf_none`); a cap of `some 0` owns nothing (the `card = 1` component case). Ownership characterizations: any uncapped slot ⟹ every position owned and every `k` valid (`cappedRoundRobin_owned_of_none`/`_valid_of_none`); all slots capped by `b` ⟹ `Owned i ↔ i < ∑ j, b j` and `Valid k ↔ k < 2^(∑ j, b j)` (`cappedRoundRobin_owned_iff`/`_valid_iff`, via the closed form `roundStart t = ∑ j, min t (b j)`). Guards pin the drop-out tables (2-slot, symmetric, 3-slot mixed) and round-trips; all axiom-clean. `FairPairs` builds the first of those: the FAIR pair generator on the PLAIN product `A × B` (Malachite `exhaustive_pairs`), the DEFAULT `ExhaustiveGenerator (A × B)` instance (`instExhaustiveGeneratorProd`) for two INFINITE contiguous components — position `k`'s bits are split by `fairPairAssignment` (= `roundRobin 2`; slot 0 = fst = odd bits, slot 1 = snd = even bits) so both component indices grow like `√k`, unlike the lex generators (which live on newtypes precisely to leave `A × B` to this instance).
