Its `gen` is unconditionally `some` via the new infiniteness bridge `ExhaustiveGenerator.gen_ne_none_of_infinite` (`[Contiguous T] + [Infinite T]` ⟹ the generator never runs out: pigeonhole — a `none` propagates rightward, so every unique index `idx` injects `T ↪ Fin n`; plus the `Option.get`-ready `gen_isSome_of_infinite`); the explicit builder `fairPairGen gA gB hA hB` takes the never-`none` witnesses directly (`Prop`-level, computability unaffected) and the instance is defined through it. Exactly-once composes the components' `occurs_exactly_once` with the `BitInterleave` round-trips: the unique index of `(a,b)` is `fairPairAssignment.interleave ![idx a, idx b]`. `Contiguous (A × B)` is vacuous (always-`some`), `Infinite (A × B)` is Mathlib's `Prod.infinite_of_left`. The `#guard`s replay the `BitDistributor` pairs doctest at the product level (`firstN (AzNat × AzNat) 10`), a heterogeneous `AzNat × AzInt` prefix, a `k = 100` fairness spot check (`(4, 10)`), and an `interleave`-computed index round-trip. `FairTuples` adds the FLAT fair triple/quadruple instances on the plain products (Malachite `exhaustive_triples_from_single`/`exhaustive_quadruples_from_single`, generalized heterogeneous): `instExhaustiveGeneratorProd3`/`Prod4` via builders `fairTripleGen`/`fairQuadrupleGen` over `fairTripleAssignment`/`fairQuadrupleAssignment` (= `roundRobin 3`/`4`; slot 0 = FIRST component = highest bit offset). WHY FLAT: nested pair-of-pair resolution of `A × B × C` gives component growth `O(√n), O(n^¼), O(n^¼)`; the flat 3-slot interleave gives all three `O(n^⅓)` (4 slots all `O(n^¼)`). Instance PRIORITIES make a bare right-nested product resolve at maximal flat width: quadruple (1200) > triple (1100) > pair (default 1000) — so a 5-tuple resolves as the flat quadruple with its last slot a fair pair; flat arity 5+ is a mechanical later extension. Exactly-once generalizes the pair proof (witness `interleave ![iA, iB, iC(, iD)]`, uniqueness pinning each `deinterleave j` through the component's uniqueness), with matching-priority `Contiguous` instances via `contiguous_of_gen_some`. The `#guard`s replay Malachite's 20-term `exhaustive_triples_from_single` test sequence, an explicit priority check (the forced nested instance yields `(1,0,0)` at index 2 where the flat instance yields `(0,1,0)`), balance spot checks (`gen 511 = (7,7,7)`; quad `gen 65535 = (15,15,15,15)`), and the 16-term quadruple prefix. `FairVecs` puts the fair fixed-length enumeration on the PLAIN `List.Vector T n` (Malachite `exhaustive_vecs_fixed_length_from_single`; the polynomial-coefficients carrier — plain types carry FAIR, the `LexVec` newtype keeps lex): `instExhaustiveGeneratorVector` by cases on `n` — length `0` is the singleton empty-vec generator (`fairVecGenZero`, with a card-1 `FiniteGenerator`), length `m+1` the flat `(m+1)`-way `fairVecGen` over `fairVecAssignment m` (= `roundRobin (m+1)`), `gen k = some (List.Vector.ofFn fun j => …deinterleave j k…)` with exactly-once via per-coordinate uniqueness (`choose`) + the `interleave` round-trips; `Contiguous` by cases (`contiguous_of_getElem?`/`contiguous_of_gen_some`). Guards match Malachite's length-2/length-3 fixed-length-vecs test sequences, the `n = 0`/`n = 1` degenerate cases, and a `gen 511 = [7,7,7]` balance check. `FairCapped` wires `BitInterleaveCapped` into the fair products/vecs, admitting FINITE components: the `FairSlot T` class packages a component's slot spec (`bits : Option ℕ` — `none` = infinite slot, `some b` = capped at `b` counter bits — plus `card` with `card ≤ 2^bits` and the initial-segment production bounds; instances `fairSlotOfInfinite` via `gen_ne_none_of_infinite`, `fairSlotOfFinite` with `bits = (card - 1).size` via `Nat.lt_size_self`), and the capped builders `fairPairGenCapped`/`fairTripleGenCapped`/`fairQuadrupleGenCapped`/`fairVecGenCapped` run the counter through `cappedRoundRobin` on the slots' cap vector: `gen k` is a `Valid k`-guarded `Option.bind` chain through the component `gen`s at the deinterleaved indices — a component's own `none` above its card IS the interior-hole check (non-power-of-two cards fill only `card` of their `2^bits` patterns), and when all slots are capped, counters `k ≥ 2^∑bits` are invalid. Exactly-once: witness `interleave ![idx a, idx b, …]` (per-slot bounds `idx < card ≤ 2^bits` feed the guarded `deinterleave_interleave`; `interleave_valid` discharges the guard), uniqueness by pinning each `deinterleave j k` through component uniqueness then `interleave_deinterleave`. Instances `instExhaustiveGeneratorProdCapped`/`Prod3Capped`/`Prod4Capped`/`VectorCapped` sit each BELOW its all-infinite counterpart and ABOVE the lower-arity capped ones (quad 1200 > capped 1150 > triple 1100 > capped 1050 > pair 1000 > capped 900; vec 1000 > capped 900), so all-infinite products keep the uncapped path and mixed products resolve capped at maximal flat width. The raw capped builders have HOLES (a non-power-of-two `card` fills only `card` of its `2^bits` slot patterns; all-capped products are invalid past `2^∑bits`), so the public instances run them through `Compress` (below): the VALUE sequence is unchanged but positions are DENSE, making every capped composite `Contiguous` (`instContiguousProdCapped`/`Prod3`/`Prod4`/`VectorCapped`) and every all-finite one a `FiniteGenerator` at the EXACT product card (`instFiniteGeneratorProdCapped` card `cardA*cardB`, triple/quad analogues, vec `card^n`, plus the length-0 card-1 instances) — restoring composite `FairSlot`s and closing 4b's 5-tuple gap: a 5-tuple's tail pair now always slots in (a MIXED tail pair is `Contiguous`+`Infinite`, so such 5-tuples resolve through the UNCAPPED quad; an all-finite tail pair rides its restored `FiniteGenerator` into the capped quad). The live count fed to `compress` is `mulCount` over the per-slot `FairSlot.count`s (`none` infinite, `some card` capped), with hypotheses from `FairSlot.hasCount` (a slot spec is honest cardinality data: `equivFin` on capped slots, the never-`none` injection on infinite ones) composed by `HasCount.prod`/`HasCount.vector`. Guards: the motivating `firstN (UInt64 × UInt64) 10` Z-order table (instant, `(1,0)` at index 2 — unreachable by lex; hole-free, identical pre/post compression), mixed `AzNat × UInt8` prefix, DENSE tables for `Ordering × AzNat` (`firstN 12` now yields 12 values) and all-capped `Ordering × Ordering` (9 values, `gen 8` some / `gen 9` none — the contiguous cutoff — and `FiniteGenerator.card = 9`), hole-free power-of-two `Bool × AzNat` (identical to 4b), a card-1 degenerate slot, mixed flat triple/quad tables, capped vec tables (`UInt8`/`Ordering`, lengths 0–2, exact cards), a finite-tail 5-tuple table, and `rfl`-pins of the instance resolutions; the DEEP post-cap spot checks (`k = 2^16` decodes to `(256, 0)`; triple `2^24`/`2^25`) are pinned on the RAW builders — the spec `unrank` reaches a compressed position by a linear scan; deep compressed positions go through `CompressFast`'s digit DP (below). `Compress` is the generic spec-layer HOLE COMPRESSION, for ANY generator: the live counters are the `k` with `(gen k).isSome`; `rankSpec g k` counts the live counters below `k` (a computable `Finset.range`-filter card — the linear-time SPEC; `CompressFast` below is the fast digit-DP replacement) and `unrank g N h i` walks to the `i`-th live counter by iterated `Nat.find` (`unrankAux` packages the walk with its invariant `(gen (unrank i)).isSome ∧ rankSpec (unrank i) = i`, so the runtime cost is ONE left-to-right scan — `rankSpec` is proof-only, never evaluated). The two regimes (infinitely many live counters vs exactly `n`) are unified by an optional live count `N : Option ℕ` with the decidable guard `Under N c` (`∀ b, N = some b → c < b`); `compress g N h h'` (`gen i = if Under N i then g.gen (unrank … i) else none`) consumes the at-least count hypothesis `h` (below the count there is always a next live counter — powers the `Nat.find`s) and the at-most hypothesis `h'` (every live counter's rank is under the count — places every value), with `occurs_exactly_once` transported along the order isomorphism `rankSpec`/`unrank` (`rankSpec_unrank`/`unrank_rankSpec`/`unrank_lt_unrank`). Always `Contiguous` (`compress_contiguous`: `some` exactly on the initial segment under `N`); `FiniteGenerator.ofCompress` supplies card-`n` data at `N = some n`; the no-holes lemmas `unrank_eq_self`/`compress_gen_eq_of_no_holes` make compression gen-pointwise the IDENTITY on already-hole-free generators (why the power-of-two tables are untouched). The count hypotheses come from pure CARDINALITY data: the live counters of any generator for `T` biject with `T` (`occurs_exactly_once`), so `HasCount T N` (`Nonempty (T ≃ Fin n)` for `some n`, an injection `ℕ → T` for `none`) yields both hypotheses for EVERY generator of `T` (`hasCount_h`/`hasCount_h'`, pigeonhole on the unique indices `idx`), and `HasCount.prod` composes counts across products via `mulCount` (optional-count multiplication with the `∞ * 0 = 0` convention, so an infinite × empty product is correctly empty). `CompressFast` is the fast digit-DP rank/unrank for capped-product compression (chunk 4d): the capped builders' liveness is `CompressFast.Live cap cards k` (= `Valid k` ∧ every capped slot's `deinterleave j k < cards j`; bridge `fairPairGenCapped_gen_isSome_iff`), and `countBelow cap cards n` computes `#{k < n | Live k}` WITHOUT scanning via the FIRST-DIFFERING-BIT partition (`lt_iff_exists_diff_bit`: `k < n` iff `k` agrees with `n` above some set bit `i` of `n` and has bit `i` clear; witness unique, `diff_bit_unique`) — per set bit the live class factors as a PRODUCT over slots of completion counts (`card_class`, an explicit `Finset.card_bij'` against a `Fintype.piFinset`: each slot reads an initial segment of `f` free sub-boundary ranks with `2^f - 1 = deinterleave j (2^i - 1)` (`exists_freeCount`), the prefix `(n >>> (i+1)) <<< (i+1)` pins the high bits AND the forced 0 at `i` (`deinterleave_add_of_prefix`), and a capped slot clamps to `min (2^f) (cards j - D)` (`card_filter_range_add_lt`)); validity is folded in by clipping `n` to `2^totalBits` in the all-capped case (`owned_iff_totalBits` unifying the two ownership regimes as `Under (totalBits cap)`). Correctness `countBelow_eq_card` (= the `Finset`-filter count) and `countBelow_eq_rankSpec` (= `rankSpec g` for any generator with the `Live` bridge). `fastUnrank` finds the `i`-th live counter as the least `k` with `i < countBelow (k+1)` — doubling upper bound (`Nat.find` over `countBelow (2^t)`, existence witnessed by the SLOW `unrank`, erased at runtime) + monotone `binSearch` (`binSearch_spec`) — with `fastUnrank_eq_unrank` (UNCONDITIONAL, junk `0` past the count on both sides) via order-iso uniqueness; `compressFast` is the gen-pointwise-identical re-pointing vehicle (`compressFast_gen_eq_compress`), and `fastUnrankPair`/`specUnrankPair` package the pair case with all hypotheses derived from the `FairSlot` specs (`fastUnrankPair_eq_specUnrankPair`). The position arithmetic is made cheap first: `roundStart` gets the mixed-cap closed form `∑ j, min t (cap j)` (`CappedRoundRobin.roundStart_eq_sum`, generalizing `roundStart_allCapped`), so `posFast`/`deiFast` avoid `posFun`'s quadratic per-round rescan. Guards: range-64 brute-force sweeps (`Ordering × Ordering`, `Ordering × AzNat`) for `countBelow` = filter count = `rankSpec`, `fastUnrankPair` = spec `unrank` sweeps (incl. past-the-count junk), and the SCALE demonstration — a `10^12 + 39`-card component (`bigDemoGen`, a 40-bit capped slot) × `Ordering`: total live count `3·(10^12+39)` read off at the `2^42` budget, and `fastUnrank` at compressed index `10^9` answering `1333333332` instantly (hand-checkable: holes at counter bits `{0,2}` both set, 6 live per 8-block) with round-trip `countBelow = 10^9`, liveness, and the decoded `.gt` coordinate pinned — where the spec path would scan ~`1.3 × 10^9` raw counters. All axiom-clean. `BitInterleaveWeighted` is the WEIGHTED round-robin `BitAssignment` (Malachite `BitDistributorOutputType::normal(w)`): `weightedRoundRobin w hm hw` has period `W = ∑ j, w j` and hands the slots CONSECUTIVE blocks of their weight within each period in DECREASING slot order (slot `m-1` owns the least-significant bits — the `[normal(2); 3]` bit-map doctest `[2, 2, 1, 1, 0, 0, …]` is a `#guard`), so slot `j`'s deinterleaved component grows like `k^(w j / W)`. Closed forms over reversed weight prefix sums (`revWeightSum`, spec lemmas `revWeightSum_total = ∑ j, w j` and `weightBlockStart_eq_sum_Ioi : weightBlockStart w j = ∑ j' > j, w j'`): `slotOf i = m - 1 - weightBlockIdx w (i % W)` (block lookup `weightBlockIdx` is a `Finset` count with uniqueness `weightBlockIdx_eq` and existence `exists_weightBlock_decomp` — every `t < W` splits as `weightBlockStart w j + s`, `s < w j`), `rank i = (i / W) * w j + (i % W − weightBlockStart w j)`, `pos j r = (r / w j) * W + weightBlockStart w j + r % w j`; the four laws are div/mod arithmetic (generic strict-monotonicity core `weightedPos_strictMono_aux`), so the `ℕ ≃ (Fin m → ℕ)` bijection is inherited FREE. All weights `1` degenerates pointwise to `roundRobin` (`weightedRoundRobin_one_slotOf`/`_rank`/`_pos`). Guards also pin both Rust `bit_distributor_sequence` doctests (`normal(1), normal(2)` and `normal(2), normal(1)`) — NB the Rust constructor SWAPS its arguments into `BitDistributor::new(&[y, x])` and streams `get_output(1)`, so the doctest sequences are slot 1 of the `![2, 1]`- and `![1, 2]`-weighted distributors. `BitInterleaveWeightedCapped` is the WEIGHTED+CAPPED `CappedBitAssignment` (Malachite `normal(w)` under `set_max_bits`): `weightedCappedRoundRobin w capBits hw` runs the weighted rounds of `weightedRoundRobin` but lets a per-slot bit-cap cut a block SHORT mid-round — round `t` gives slot `j` a block of `contrib = min (w j) (cap j − t·w j)` positions (ℕ-truncation zeroes an exhausted slot), with the bits-used closed form `∑_{t'<t} contrib = min (t·w j) (cap j)` and the performance-form round start `roundStart t = ∑ j, min (t·w j) (cap j)` (mirroring `CompressFast.roundStart_eq_sum`). Slot `j`'s `r`-th position stays in round `r / w j` until saturation (`r < cap j`), so `posFun j r = roundStart (r/w j) + offsetIn (r/w j) j + r % w j`. The Malachite `set_max_bits(&[0, 2], 5)` doctest — `[normal(2); 3]` capped at 5 on slots `{0, 2}`, bit-map `[2,2,1,1,0,0, 2,2,1,1,0,0, 2,1,1,0, 1,1,1,…]` with both capped blocks cut short mid-round — is a `#guard`, alongside a minimal mid-block-cut case, all-capped `Owned`/`Valid` ranges, weighted+capped round-trips, and two degeneration lemmas (`weightedCappedRoundRobin_one_slotOf` recovers `cappedRoundRobin`, `_none_slotOf` recovers `weightedRoundRobin`). All axiom-clean. `BitInterleaveTiny` is the TINY output-type `BitAssignment` (Malachite `BitDistributorOutputType::tiny()`): `tinyAssignment isTiny w ht hn hw` gives the tiny slots the RULER positions — `i` with `i + 1` a power of two, `{0, 1, 3, 7, 15, …}` (computable test `isRulerPos i = decide (i + 1 = 2 ^ i.size)`, spec `isRulerPos_iff`; NB `i = 0` AND `i = 1` are both ruler positions) — rotating in DECREASING slot order starting from the LAST tiny slot (`i = 0` goes to the last tiny; the `[normal(2), tiny()]` bit-map doctest `[1, 1, 0, 1, 0, 0, 0, 1, …]` is a `#guard`), so a tiny component grows like `(log k)^(1/tinyCount)`; the normal slots take the non-ruler positions in the `weightedRoundRobin` rotation. Closed forms: the `r`-th ruler position is `2 ^ r - 1` with the rank read back by `Nat.size` (`size_two_pow_sub_one`), so the tiny side is `roundRobin tinyCount` conjugated by `r ↦ 2 ^ r - 1` and the `Finset.orderEmbOfFin` slot embedding `tinyEmb`/`tinyIdx`; the non-ruler rank of a non-ruler `i` is `i - (i + 1).size` (`(i + 1).size` counts the rulers `≤ i`), with inverse `nonRulerPos n = n + rulerBlockIdx n + 1` where the inter-ruler block index `rulerBlockIdx` (the unique `s ≥ 1` with `2^s - s - 1 ≤ n < 2^(s+1) - s - 2`) is a `Finset` count in the `weightBlockIdx` style (uniqueness `rulerBlockIdx_eq`, spec via `Nat.find`, monotone; generic helper `two_pow_add_le : a ≤ b → 2 ^ a + b ≤ 2 ^ b + a`) — so the normal side is `weightedRoundRobin (normalWeights)` conjugated by `nonRulerPos`/the non-ruler rank behind `normalEmb`/`normalIdx`. Signature: an `isTiny : Fin m → Bool` mask plus weights `w : Fin m → ℕ` read only on normal slots (Malachite stores weight 0 on tinys), with hypotheses `0 < tinyCount` (the all-normal case has DIFFERENT semantics — no reserved positions — use `weightedRoundRobin`), `0 < normalCount` (Malachite panics on all-tiny), and positive normal weights; the four laws reduce to the `roundRobin`/`weightedRoundRobin` laws through the ruler characterizations, so the bijection is FREE. Convenience 2-slot assignments `fairPairAssignmentTinyFirst`/`fairPairAssignmentTinySecond` and builders `ExhaustiveGenerator.fairPairGenTinyFirst`/`fairPairGenTinySecond` (via `fairPairGenWith`). Guards: the exact `[normal(2), tiny()]` bit-map doctest (32 positions), a two-tiny rotation table pinning the decreasing start (`twoTinyDemoAssignment`, `[2, 0, 1, 2, …]`), a tiny+weighted-normals table, the logarithmic tiny stream (`[0, 1, 2, 3, 0, 1, 2, 3, 4, …]`, `≤ 7` through `k = 127`, first `8` at `k = 128` — the Rust `bit_distributor_sequence(tiny(), normal(1))` growth claim), a 30-pair `[tiny, normal]` prefix over `AzNat × AzNat` with deep spot check `gen 100 = (0, 13)`, and `interleave`/`deinterleave` round-trips. `FairWeighted` exposes the weighted enumerations as USE-SITE builders (weights are call-site parameters, so builders, NOT instances — the defaults stay weight-1): `ExhaustiveGenerator.fairPairGenWeighted wA wB …`/`fairTripleGenWeighted`/`fairQuadrupleGenWeighted` over the assignments `fairPairAssignmentWeighted` etc., each an application of the new ASSIGNMENT-GENERIC builders `fairPairGenWith (P : BitAssignment 2)`/`fairTripleGenWith`/`fairQuadrupleGenWith` (factored out of `FairPairs`/`FairTuples`; `fairPairGen`/`fairTripleGen`/`fairQuadrupleGen` are now their weight-1 specializations) — pair weights `(p, q)` give component growth `~k^(p/(p+q))`/`~k^(q/(p+q))`. Guards: hand-derived weighted pair prefixes for weights `(1, 2)` and `(2, 1)` over `AzNat`, deep imbalance spot checks (`gen 63 = (3, 15)`, `gen 100 = (3, 16)`), a weighted `(1, 1, 2)` triple table with `gen 63 = (1, 1, 15)`, and `interleave`-computed index round-trips. This COMPLETES the planned BitDistributor port surface (chunks 1–5); tiny output types (`BitInterleaveTiny` above) and the fast rank/unrank (`CompressFast` above, chunk 4d) have since landed, leaving the weighted-capped assignment as the flagged extension. `Stream` — `StreamFor g`, the sequential COMPUTE rail (chunk C1 of the caching layer): computable iterator data (`σ`/`init`/`next`) tied to the pure spec by the bridge law `emits : ∀ n, (emitAt next init n).1 = g.gen n` (`emitAt` = `n`-fold `advanceN`, then step), with `firstNStream` collecting a prefix in ONE forward pass and provably equal to `firstN` (`firstNStream_eq_firstN`). Streams: `ofGen` (trivial, state = index, total coverage); the five Calkin–Wilf rational streams (`positiveRationalsStream` … `rationalsStream`, state = the Stern–Brocot pair in a reachability-carrying `CWState`, at most one `sternBrocotStep` per position — `firstN n` drops from O(n²) to O(n) bignum steps; pinned by the doctest prefixes plus a 300-term guard with spot value `18/25`); `compressStream` (state = `CompressState` = compressed index + raw scan position with a `rankSpec` invariant, each `next` resumes the raw scan via `Nat.find`, so a prefix is ONE pass over the raw counters — key lemma `unrank_eq_find`; exercised by the hole-y `holeyDemoGen`/`holeyDemoCompressed` demo); and cheap exemplars `naturalsStream` (`+ 1` per step instead of re-running the limb-decomposition `AzNat.ofNat` per index), `integersStream` (zig-zag by negate/increment via `zigZag`), `azNatRangeStream` (current value + remaining count). Streams for the FAIR COMPOSITES are the cached drivers of `Cache` (the `IteratorCache` port): `GenCache s` wraps a component stream in an Array-backed fill-forward cache — data `state`/`cache` plus the `Prop` invariant `state = advanceN … cache.size ∧ ∀ i < size, cache[i] = gen i` — with `get i` reading covered indices for free and otherwise filling forward one `next` per new entry (`get_fst : (c.get i).1 = gen i`, `get_snd_cache_size`: any access pattern touching indices `≤ M` costs `≤ M + 1` stream steps TOTAL; no Malachite `done` flag — `Option` entries mark exhaustion and `next` is total). On top: `StreamFor.map` (transport along `mapGen`) and the cached drivers `fairPairFirstNCached`/`fairTripleFirstNCached`/`fairQuadrupleFirstNCached`/`fairVecFirstNCached` (one counter walk, per-component caches threaded through the fold, each component value computed ONCE — the vec driver shares a single cache across all coordinates), each PROVEN equal to the spec rail at instance level (`… = firstN _ n`, e.g. `fairPairFirstNCached_eq_firstN`) via assignment-generic walker laws; plus the capped siblings `fairPairCappedFirstNCached` / `fairTripleCappedFirstNCached` / `fairQuadrupleCappedFirstNCached` / `fairVecCappedFirstNCached` on the RAW capped builders (laws `fair*CappedFirstNCached_eq` against `fair*GenCapped`'s prefix — the public capped instances are hole-compressed, so their prefixes are the walker run until `n` values accumulate; the vec driver threads one shared cache across coordinates and reconstructs each vec through the capped validity-and-`isSome` guard). Guards replay the Z-order tables and the motivating Calkin–Wilf case (each rational computed once; 100-pair run pinned to the spec rail). |
| `FoerSequence/` | Finite-Or-Eventually-Repeating sequences over `[DecidableEq T]` (a Lean port of Malachite's `RationalSequence`): a `FoerSequence T` denotes `nonRepeating ++ (repeating cycled forever)`, stored in a *unique canonical (reduced)* form — `repeating` at its minimal period and no trailing `nonRepeating` element absorbable into the cycle. Core list algorithms `minRepeatingLen` (smallest divisor-period, via `find?` over `range' 1 (len/2)` with the adjacent-difference period check `periodicB`), `trailingCount`/`trailingMatch` (cyclic reverse-match count), `foerIsReduced` (the checkable invariant), and `reduce` (canonicalization: truncate to minimal period, then absorb + `rotateRight` trailing matches). The canonicalization guarantee **`reduce_isReduced`** is fully proved (pure 3-axiom) — resting on the reusable periodicity theory `HasPeriod`/`hasPeriod_take_compose` (period composition), `hasPeriod_rotate`/`minRepeatingLen_rotate` (rotation-invariance of the minimal period), `minRepeatingLen_take` (truncation idempotence), and the `trailingCount_stop`/`trailingMatch_reduce` "no residual match after absorption" lemmas. The bundled type carries the invariant proof; smart constructors `ofLists`/`ofList` canonicalize, with accessors `isEmpty`/`isFinite`/`len`/`componentLen`, `get`, `mutate`/`set` (expand-then-`reduce`), a `ToString` matching Malachite's `Display` (`[1, 2, [3, 4]]`), and `#guard`s translating the Rust doctests. |
| `AzPolynomial/` | Dense univariate polynomials over a semiring `R`, stored as `Array R` with a trailing-nonzero invariant. Includes add, mul (basecase + Karatsuba), negation, scalar multiplication, multiplication by `X^n` (`mulXPow`), truncation (`truncate`, BPR Notation 1.16), derivative, evaluation, composition (Horner), exponentiation (sliding-window), quotient/remainder (Euclidean division), signed pseudo-remainder (`pRem`, works over any `CommRing`), root bounds, the Sturm sequence (`sturmSequence`, BPR Chapter 2.2), parsing, and `toString`. |
| `AzMvPolynomial/` | Sparse multivariate polynomials over `R` in variables `σ`, stored as a sorted array of monomials (descending by monic part). Supports multiple monomial orderings (lex, deglex, degrevlex). Includes add, mul (naive + optimized), negation, scalar multiplication, partial derivative, evaluation (`eval`, plus generic `eval₂`/`aeval` into any commutative semiring or `R`-algebra), exact division, monomial exponentiation, rename, map, merge-sorted operations, `bind₁`/`bind₂`/`join₂` substitution, `finSuccEquiv` (both computable directions: `AzMvPolynomial (n+1) R ord ↔ AzPolynomial (AzMvPolynomial n R ord)`), and the multivariate gcd suite over `AzInt` in `Gcd.lean` (`AzMvPolynomial.gcd`: full descent to the nested univariate tower `NestedPoly n`, recursive `NormalizedGcd` there — see `AzPolynomial/GcdTower.lean` — and ascent back; plus `normalized`, first-variable `content`/`primitivePart`, `isPrimitive`, fraction-field `coprime` (with `coprime_toAzMvPolynomial`: univariate-lift preservation), fraction-field `isSquarefree` (gcd of `P` with its whole gradient `squarefreeGradientGcd` is a nonzero constant), and the canonical pair `gcdGcdFreePart = (g, P/g)`). `IntContent.lean` adds the full-content helpers over `AzInt` (`leadingCoeff`, `intContent`, `signedIntContent`, `primPos`) — with `Equiv/IntContent.lean` proving `leadingCoeff_mul` (leading coeff multiplicative, no Gauss) — backing the factored canonical form of `AzMvRationalFunction` (`AzMvRationalFunction/Basic.lean`: the multivariate `AzRationalFunction` analogue — `factor·num/den` with primitive, positive-leading, coprime parts; structure + normalizer `ofNumDen` + conversions, canonicity proofs deferred). |
| `AzPolynomialQ/` | Rational univariate polynomials with a shared denominator: stores `numerators : Array ℤ` and `denom : ℕ` in canonical (GCD-reduced) form. Enables exact arithmetic without per-coefficient rational normalization, fast pointwise negation, and integer-level `Monic` property evaluation. |
| `AzVector/` | Fixed-length vectors wrapping Lean's `Vector R n`. Includes addition, negation, subtraction, scalar multiplication, dot product, cross product, and basis vectors. |
| `AzMatrix/` | Fixed-size `m × n` matrices wrapping `Vector (Vector R n) m`. Includes addition, negation, subtraction, scalar multiplication, matrix multiplication, matrix-vector multiplication, transpose, row/column access, Gaussian elimination (`gauss`, BPR Algorithm 8.15, early-abort; `gaussSteps`, indexed step iteration), row-echelon reduction (`rowEchelon`, always upper-triangular), determinant via Gauss (`det`), determinant via Bareiss recurrence (`bareissDet`, BPR Algorithm 8.16, fraction-free over a domain), the Bareiss block/minor (`bareissBlock`, `bareissMinor`, `principalMinor`; BPR Notation 8.19), the computable symmetry test (`isSymmetricB`/`isSkewSymmetricB`, proven `= (toMat M).IsSymm` in `AzMatrix/Equiv/Symmetric`), trace and trace-of-product (`trace`, `traceMul`), and the characteristic polynomial via baby-step/giant-step Newton sums (`charPoly`, BPR Algorithm 8.17, over a field; proven equal to Mathlib's `Matrix.charpoly` in `AzMatrix/Equiv/CharPoly` via the eigenvalue-power-sum identity `Module.End.trace_pow_eq_sum_roots_pow`). |
| `AzFermat/` | Integers modulo `2^N + 1` (the coefficient ring of the Schönhage–Strassen multiplication), as canonical `AzNat` residues in `[0, 2^N]`: `add`/`sub`/`neg` with one conditional correction, `reduceSplit`/`reduceAny` (`hi·2^N + lo ≡ lo − hi`), `mulWith mulFn` (a caller-supplied `AzNat` multiplier, reduced), `mulPow2 t`/`divPow2 t` (shifts, since `2^N ≡ −1` and `2^{2N} ≡ 1`), and in `FFT.lean` Algorithms 2.2/2.3 of Brent–Zimmermann on arrays of residues with root `2^e` (`forwardFFT`: natural to bit-reversed order; `backwardFFT`: back). |
| `AzFormula/` | Computable first-order formulas over `AzFieldAtom` (field atoms using `AzMvPolynomial`). Provides computable free-variable computation, bound-variable computation, sentence checking, formula constructors, variable renaming, negation normal form (`toNNF`), prenex normal form conversion (`toPrenex`), and noncomputable realization. Generic `AtomVars`, `AtomRename`, `AtomRealization` typeclasses. Modular simplification passes (`elimDoubleNeg`, `elimVacuousQuantifiers`). |

### Equivalence Proofs (`Equiv/` subdirectories)

Each core data structure has an `Equiv/` subdirectory containing proofs that Azurite's computable operations agree with Mathlib's abstract definitions.

#### AzNat ↔ Nat

| File | What it proves |
|------|----------------|
| `Equiv/Basic` | Computable equivalence `equivNat : AzNat ≃ Nat` via `toNat`/`ofNat`, including base invariant preservation, plus the foundational limb-list value lemmas (`toNatLimbsList_cons`, `toNatLimbsList_append`, `toNatLimbsList_lt_pow`). |
| `Equiv/Add` | `toNat_add` / `toNat_addUInt64`: limb-level carry-chain addition agrees with `Nat` addition. |
| `Equiv/Sub` | `toNat_sub`: limb-level borrow-chain subtraction agrees with `Nat` subtraction. |
| `Equiv/Compare` | `compare_eq_compare_toNat`: custom limb-by-limb `compare` logic mapping equivalently to `Ord.compare` on `Nat`, providing the formally verified `LinearOrder AzNat` instance with `≤` and `<`. |
| `Equiv/CompareDouble` | `cmpDouble_eq_compare_toNat`: `cmpDouble x y = Ord.compare x.toNat (2 * y.toNat)` for the allocation-free comparison against a doubled operand (the Malachite `cmp_double` oracle): the limbs of `2 y` formed on the fly telescope to twice the value plus a carry that vanishes past `y` (`seqVal_doubleLimb`), and the top-down sweep compares values because the lower limbs are below one unit of the position compared (`cmpDouble.go_eq`). |
| Comparing products (`AzNat.compareProducts a b c d`, the ordering of `a · b` against `c · d` without multiplying unless necessary: zero factors; the bit lengths, a surplus of two bits deciding (constant-time bit counts, so first); the pairing `(a, c), (b, d)` and then `(a, d), (b, c)`, each deciding when the two comparisons agree or one is an equality; and only then the two products — the same staged screens as `AzRat.cmp`. PROVEN) | staged screens, multiplication last | `AzNat/CompareProducts` | screens `O(size)`, fallback `O(M(size))` |
| Comparing a product with a number (`AzNat.compareProduct a b c`, the ordering of `a · b` against `c`: zero factors or `c = 0`; a unit factor; the bit lengths, `size a + size b ≥ size c + 2` or `≤ size c − 1` deciding; and only then the product. PROVEN) | staged screens, multiplication last | `AzNat/CompareProduct` | screens `O(size)`, fallback `O(M(size))` |
| Signed comparison of a product (`AzInt.compareProduct a b c`, the ordering of `a · b` against `c`: the signs decide every case except equal nonzero signs, which defer to `AzNat.compareProduct` on the magnitudes, so multiplication remains the last resort. PROVEN) | sign dispatch over the `AzNat` screens | `AzInt/CompareProduct` | as `AzNat.compareProduct` |
| Signed comparison of two products (`AzInt.compareProducts a b c d`, the ordering of `a · b` against `c · d`: the signs decide every case except two nonzero products of the same sign, which defer to `AzNat.compareProducts` on the magnitudes. PROVEN) | sign dispatch over the `AzNat` screens | `AzInt/CompareProducts` | as `AzNat.compareProducts` |
| Comparing a product of rationals with a rational (`AzRat.compareProduct x y z`, the ordering of `x · y` against `z` without forming or reducing `x · y`: signs first; the magnitudes `nx·ny·dz` against `nz·dx·dy` through the bit lengths with a surplus of three bits (constant-time, so first), three factorwise screens (`|x| ≤ 1, |y| ≤ 1, |z| ≥ 1` and its reverse, then the two pairings matching `x` or `y` with `z`), and finally the products `nx·ny`, `dx·dy` handed to `AzNat.compareProducts`, whose screens may still avoid the last multiplications. PROVEN) | staged screens, multiplication last, no gcd | `AzRat/CompareProduct` | screens `O(size)`, fallback two to four multiplications |
| Comparing two products of rationals (`AzRat.compareProducts w x y z`, the ordering of `w · x` against `y · z` without forming or reducing either product: signs first; the magnitudes `nw·nx·dy·dz` against `ny·nz·dw·dx` through the bit lengths with a surplus of four bits (constant-time, so first), three four-way factorwise screens (`|w|, |x| ≤ 1` against `|y|, |z| ≥ 1` and its reverse, then the pairings `w↔y, x↔z` and `w↔z, x↔y`), and finally the four two-factor products handed to `AzNat.compareProducts`. PROVEN) | staged screens, multiplication last, no gcd | `AzRat/CompareProducts` | screens `O(size)`, fallback four to six multiplications |
| Sign of a dot product of natural vectors (`AzNat.dotSign v w`, the ordering of `v ⬝ w` against `0`: `.gt` when some coordinate is nonzero in both vectors, else `.eq`; one pass of zero tests, no multiplication or addition. PROVEN) | zero tests | `AzVector/DotSign` | `O(n)` |
| Sign of a dot product of integer vectors (`AzInt.dotSign v w`, the ordering of `v ⬝ w` against `0`: the nonzero terms are split by sign into magnitude pairs; one side empty decides without multiplying; one term each side is `AzNat.compareProducts`; otherwise an interval screen on bit lengths (a side's sum is below `2^(K + size m)` for its largest pair size `K` and term count `m`, the other side's at least `2^(K' − 2)`, so a surplus of `size m + 2` bits between the maxima decides without any product); otherwise the single largest pair is set aside, the other products are summed into `P'` and `N'`, and the largest term is decided against `|N' − P'|` by `AzNat.compareProduct`, so the most expensive multiplication happens only when the screens cannot settle it. PROVEN) | sign split, largest term last via `compareProduct` | `AzVector/DotSignInt` | `n − 1` multiplications plus the screened last one |
| Sign of a dot product of rational vectors (`AzRat.dotSign v w`, the ordering of `v ⬝ w` against `0`: nonzero terms split by sign into four-factor magnitude terms; one side empty decides without multiplying; one term each side is `AzRat.compareProductsMag`; the interval screen on exponents `size nx + size ny − size dx − size dy` (a term lies in `[2^(e−2), 2^(e+2))`) decides lopsided cases without any product; otherwise each side is summed as an unreduced fraction by a balanced tree (`Frac`, `balancedSum`, no gcd) and the two fractions are compared by `AzNat.compareProducts` on their cross products. PROVEN) | sign split, interval screen, balanced unreduced sums, cross products last | `AzVector/DotSignRat` | `O(M(S) log n)` for total size `S`, no gcd |
| `Equiv/CompareProducts` | `compareProducts_eq` (`compareProducts a b c d = compare (a.toNat * b.toNat) (c.toNat * d.toNat)`): each screen is a monotonicity fact about products (`Nat.mul_lt_mul_of_lt_of_le`, cancellation of a common positive factor), and the bit-length screen rests on `2^(size x − 1) ≤ x < 2^(size x)` (`Nat.lt_size`, `Nat.lt_size_self`). |
| `Equiv/CompareProduct` | `compareProduct_eq` (`compareProduct a b c = compare (a.toNat * b.toNat) c.toNat`), screen by screen with the shared `compare_Nat_lt/eq/gt_iff` and the bit-length bounds. |
| `Equiv/Conversion` | `UInt64.toNat_toAzNat` and friends: conversions from every fixed-width unsigned type (and `Int64.toNat_toAzNatClampNeg`) agree with `toNat`. |
| `Equiv/Mul/Basic` | `schoolbookMulLimbs_toNat` / `toNat_mulUInt64`: schoolbook multiplication agrees with `Nat` multiplication, via the single-row (`mulAddLimbs_toNat`) and fused two-row (`mulAdd2Limbs_toNat`) multiply-accumulate passes. |
| `Equiv/Mul/Karatsuba` | `karatsubaMulLimbs_toNat`: Karatsuba limb multiplication agrees with `Nat` multiplication; `karatsubaMulLimbsRec.assemble_toNat` for the in-place two-pass assembly of `C₀ + (C₀ + C₁ ∓ C₂) β^k + C₁ β^(2k)` (exact modulo `β^(2 len)`, carries out of the top dropped). |
| `Equiv/Mul/ToomCook3` | `toomCook3MulLimbs_toNat`: Toom-Cook 3 multiplication agrees with `Nat` multiplication. |
| `Equiv/Mul/ToomEval` | `polyEvalInt`/`polyEvalNat` specs; `toInt_hornerInt64`, the triangle bound `natAbs_polyEvalInt_le`, `toInt_signedMulWith` (given the multiplier's spec and `n`-limb bounds), `AzInt.toInt_exactDivOdd`, `toNat_assemble`, and the block decomposition `sliceVal_eq_polyEval_blocks` (`len ≤ r·k`). A variant's algebra reduces to a `ring` identity between `polyEvalInt` values. |
| `Equiv/Mul/ToomCook4`, `Equiv/Square/ToomCook4` | `toomCook4MulLimbs_toNat` / `toNat_mulToomCook4` and `toomCook4SquareLimbs_toNat` / `toNat_squareToomCook4`: Toom-4 agrees with `Nat` multiplication and squaring. Three layers: `toomCook4Interpolate_toInt` (the interpolation is exact — each step a `ring` identity plus one exact division), the array-free `toomCook4_core_toNat` (eight block values below `β^k` and any multiplier correct on `(k+1)`-limb buffers), and the strong induction on `len` supplying the recursive call as that multiplier. |
| `Equiv/Mul/ToomUnbalanced` | `toom32MulLimbs_toNat`, `toom42MulLimbs_toNat`, `mulChunksLimbs_toNat`: the unbalanced Toom-(3,2) and Toom-(4,2) variants and the chunk loop agree with `Nat` multiplication, given any balanced multiplier correct on every size (`hbal`). Interpolations `toomInterp4_toInt`/`toomInterp5_toInt`; the length-generic Horner bound `hornerInt64_abs_lt` (any `|c| ≤ 2`, up to 64 blocks). |
| `Equiv/Mul/SchonhageStrassen` | MCA Theorem 2.3 for the implementation: `fftMulModWith_toNat` / `fftSquareModWith_toNat` (`= A·B mod (2^n+1)` for `A, B < 2^n`, via `ssFinish_toNat`: digit decomposition `toNat_eq_sum_blocks`, weighted digits `toZModFun_ssWeighted`, transforms and products `toZMod_backwardFFT_of_products` (`= K θ^j c_j`), unit division and window recovery `toZMod_ssCoefficients_getElem`, Horner assembly `toZMod_ssAssemble`, and `BZ.sum_weight_mul_sum_weight` at `θ = 2^M`), then `toNat_fftMul` / `toNat_fftSquare` (exact products for the parameters of `ssParams`) and the slice forms `fftMulLimbs_toNat` / `fftSquareLimbs_toNat`. |
| `Equiv/Mul/Dispatch` | `toomLadderLimbs_toNat`, `toomLadderMul_toNat`, `balancedMulLimbs_toNat` (FFT from `th.fft` limbs, the Toom ladder below), `mulLimbsOrdered_toNat`, `mulLimbsWith_toNat`, `mulLimbs_toNat` and the top-level `toNat_mul` / `ofNat_mul`: the thresholds-driven dispatcher (`MulThresholds`) agrees with `Nat` multiplication on every branch (schoolbook, padded balanced ladder, Toom-(3,2), Toom-(4,2), chunk loop, FFT). |
| `Equiv/Square`, `Equiv/Square/*` | `toNat_square` (in `Equiv/Square/Dispatch`) via `schoolbookSquareLimbs_toNat`, `karatsubaSquareLimbs_toNat`, `toomCook3SquareLimbs_toNat`, `toomCook4SquareLimbs_toNat` and `fftSquareLimbs_toNat`: all five squaring strategies agree with `n * n`. |
| `Equiv/Div/*` | Staged correctness of schoolbook division: per-phase lemmas (`subMulLimbs_toNat`, `bodyStep_toNat`, `addback_toNat`), single-/double-limb divisor fast paths (`divModLimb_toNat`, `divModLimb2_toNat`), the full algorithm (`schoolbookDivModLimbs_toNat`), and the top-level `divMod_toNat` / `ofNat_div` / `ofNat_mod`. |
| `Equiv/Mul/SSCached` | `SSPlan.mulModF_transform_toNat` / `mulModF'_toNat` (`A B mod (2^bits + 1)` with the cached transform of `A`, for `A, B < 2^bits`: `fftMulModWith_toNat` restated), `SSPlan.ofLimbs_bits : 64 L ≤ bits`, `toZMod_eq_natCast`. |
| `Equiv/Mul/ShortProduct` | `mulLow_toNat` (the low short product is `A B mod β^n`; `mulLow_step_mod`, an identity modulo `β^n`), `mulHigh_spec` (**MCA Theorem 3.5**: `C' β^n ≤ A B < (C' + 3(n−1) + 1) β^n` for `A, B < β^n`; the step `mulHigh_step_bounds` for any base counts the four dropped pieces, each below `β^n`), `toNat_shl_pow`/`toNat_shr_pow`. |
| `Equiv/Div/Classic` | `divModClassic_toNat` (`Q V + R = U`, `R < V`) for the classical dispatcher (one- and two-limb divisors, schoolbook, divide-and-conquer), `divClassic_eq_divModClassic_fst`, `modClassic_eq_divModClassic_snd`. Formerly the body of `Equiv/Div/DivMod`. |
| `Equiv/Div/Newton` | Correctness of Newton–Barrett division: `newton_step_bounds` (MCA Lemma 3.8 in integer form, for any base `β ≥ 10`: `β^(2h) A X ≤ β^(2n+2h) − D²`, and the two truncations lose less than `A β^(2h) ≥ 5 β^(2n) > D² + A x β^ℓ`), `reciprocalBase_spec`, `reciprocalCorrect_spec`, `approxReciprocal_spec : A X < β^(2n) < A (X + 2)`, `barrett_bounds` (MCA Theorem 2.4 with the approximate reciprocal: `Q V ≤ A < V (Q + 5)`, so at most four corrections), `barrettCorrect_spec`, `barrettStep_toNat`, `newtonDivBlocks_spec` (block invariant `Q V + R = ⌊U/β^(nj)⌋`), `divModNewton_toNat`. |
| `Equiv/Div/DivMod` | The dispatcher: `divModWith_toNat` (classical or Newton–Barrett path), `divModWith_eq_divMod`, `divMod_toNat`, `divWith_eq_divModWith_fst`, `modWith_eq_divModWith_snd`, `toNat_div`, `toNat_mod`, `ofNat_div`, `ofNat_mod`. |
| `Equiv/DivRecursiveLimbs` | `recursiveDivModLimbsAux_spec`: the recursive (divide-and-conquer) divmod satisfies the division specification. |
| `Equiv/DivRound` | `toNat_divRound` (+ `divRound_fst`/`divRound_snd`): rounded division agrees with `round natBotSet mode (x / y)` from the abstract `Rounding/` spec. |
| `Equiv/ShiftLeft` | `toNat_hShiftLeft`: left shift agrees with `Nat` shift. |
| `Equiv/ShiftRight` | `toNat_shiftRight`: right shift agrees with `Nat` shift. |
| `Equiv/ShiftRightRound` | `toNat_shiftRightRound`: rounded right shift agrees with rounding `v / 2^sh` against the `natBotSet` rounding target. |
| `Equiv/ShiftRightNearestTernary` | `toNat_shiftRightNearestTernary`: the nearest right shift with an exact tie resolved by a ternary (the comparison of the shifted value with a true value) satisfies `NearestTernary (n / 2^sh) q o` (`Rounding/IntNearest`). |
| `Equiv/Gcd` | `toNat_gcd`: `(gcd a b).toNat = Nat.gcd a.toNat b.toNat` for the size-dispatching `gcd`, plus `coprime_iff`. |
| `Equiv/Gcd/Binary` | `toNat_gcdBinary`: the binary GCD (Stein's algorithm) and the single-limb Euclid (`toNat_gcdUInt64`, constant fuel 130 justified by two Euclid steps halving the second argument) agree with `Nat.gcd`. |
| `Equiv/Gcd/HalfBinary` | `toNat_gcdHalfBinary`: the half-binary GCD computes `Nat.gcd`, proved from the **determinant invariant alone** — every matrix returned by `halfBinaryGcd` has determinant `±2^{2j}` (`natAbs_detInt_halfBinaryGcd`), and such a matrix applied with exact division by `2^{2j}`, a binary division with any quotient, or an exact division by a power of two all preserve the odd part of the GCD (`OddEquiv`, `oddEquiv_gcd_of_mat`, `oddEquiv_gcd_binaryStep`); MCA Theorem 1.9's remainder-sequence theory governs progress only.  The `Int64` base case is exact by its runtime entry check `|m| ≤ 2^{61−j₀}` (`abs_toInt_le_of_check`, `wordStep_exact`); the fused pass by `linCombLimbs.go_spec_sub`/`_add`, the folded shift by refinement to the pure `emitShift` (`linCombShift.goLow_eq`/`goHigh_eq`, `emitShift_val`), the negation by `toNatLimbsList_negLimbs`, and the whole by `toInt_fusedCombine?`. The quadratic round's normalization case (powers of two stripped, pair re-formed as `(a₁, a₁ + b₁)` when the word base case cannot progress) is covered by `oddEquiv_gcd_div_pow` and `oddEquiv_gcd_binaryStep` with `q = 1`. |
| `Equiv/SumProduct` | `toNat_sum`/`toNat_product` (and the `Array` versions): the left-fold sum and the balanced product compute the sum and the product of the values, the latter through `balancedProduct_eq_prod` in the monoid `AzNat` and `List.prod_eq_zero` for the zero-factor exit. |
| `Equiv/Factorial` | `toNat_factorial`: the prime-factorization factorial computes `Nat.factorial` — `legendreExp_eq_padicValNat` (Legendre's exponent loop is `padicValNat p n!`, via Mathlib's `padicValNat_factorial`), `toNat_product` (the balanced product is the list product), `toNat_hornerPow` (`acc^(2^B) · ∏ P_i^(2^i)`), `sum_testBit` (binary expansion), and the assembly through `Nat.prod_pow_prime_padicValNat` restricted to the primes up to `n` with `2` split off. |
| `Equiv/DoubleFactorial` | `toNat_doubleFactorial`: `(doubleFactorial n).toNat = n‼` — the even case from `Nat.doubleFactorial_two_mul`, the odd case from `n! = n‼ · 2^k · k!`, which gives the exponents `v_p(n‼) = v_p(n!) − v_p(k!)` for odd primes and `n‼` odd, assembled by the factorial's `toNat_prodPrimePowers`. |
| `Equiv/MultiFactorial` | `Nat.multiFactorial_add` (the recurrence), `_one`/`_two` (factorial and double factorial), `_mul` (`(q m)!⁽ᵐ⁾ = m^q q!`), `_mul_add` (`(q m + r)!⁽ᵐ⁾ = ∏_{i ≤ q} (i m + r)`), and `toNat_multiFactorial` checking each branch of `AzNat.multiFactorial`. |
| `Equiv/Subfactorial` | `subfactorialSplit_spec` (the interval product and alternating sum, by induction on the fuel with `Finset.prod_Ico_consecutive`/`sum_Ico_consecutive`), `toNat_subfactorial` (assembled against Mathlib's `numDerangements_sum`, `ascFactorial` as an interval product), and `toNat_subfactorial_card` (the combinatorial meaning via `card_derangements_fin_eq_numDerangements`). |
| `Equiv/RisingFallingFactorial` | `toNat_factorialQuotient` (`a!/b!` for `b ≤ a` from the differences of Legendre exponents, the `padicValNat` of the quotient by `padicValNat.mul`, assembled like `toNat_factorial`), `prod_map_range_add` (the list product `x (x+1) ⋯` is `Nat.ascFactorial`), `toNat_risingFactorial` (`= x.toNat.ascFactorial k`, through `Nat.ascFactorial_eq_div'` in the quotient regime) and `toNat_fallingFactorial` (`= x.toNat.descFactorial k`, through `Nat.add_descFactorial_eq_ascFactorial'`, with `Nat.descFactorial_eq_zero_iff_lt` when `k > x`). |
| `Equiv/Binomial` | `toNat_primePowers_of_dvd_factorial` (a nonzero divisor of `n!` is the product of its prime powers over the primes up to `n`, assembled by `prodPrimePowers` with the power of two shifted — the common core of the factorial-quotient proofs), `toNat_binomialPrime` (`= Nat.choose` for `k ≤ n`, exponents from `Nat.choose_mul_factorial_mul_factorial` and `padicValNat.mul`), `toNat_binomialProduct` (`Nat.choose_eq_descFactorial_div_factorial`) and `toNat_binomial` (`(binomial n k).toNat = n.toNat.choose k`, the symmetry by `Nat.choose_symm`). |
| `Equiv/Fibonacci` | `FibPair n p` (the invariant `p.1 = F_n ∧ p.2 + F_n = F_{n+1}`), `fibPair_double` (one doubling step preserves it, by `Nat.fib_two_mul` and `Nat.fib_two_mul_add_one`), `fibPair_go` (induction on the fuel), `toNat_fibonacciPair`, `toNat_fibonacciPair_snd` (`= Nat.fib (n − 1)` for `n ≥ 1`), `fibonacciPair_zero`, `toNat_fibonacci` (`= Nat.fib n`). |
| `Equiv/Lucas` | `Nat.lucas_add_fib` (`L_n + F_n = 2 F_{n+1}`), `toNat_lucasNumber` (`= Nat.lucas n`, the reference recurrence defined in `AzNat/Lucas`), `toNat_lucasNumberPair_fst`/`_snd` (`= Nat.lucas (n − 1)` for `n ≥ 1`), `toNat_lucasNumberPair_zero` (`(2, 0)`). |
| `Equiv/Landau` | The reference: `Nat.ell M = Σ p^{v_p(M)}` over the prime factors (`ell_prime_pow_mul` splits off a prime power, `le_ell_of_mem_primeFactors`, `le_two_pow_ell`) and `Nat.landau n`, the greatest `M ≥ 1` with `ℓ(M) ≤ n` (`isGreatest_landau`, a `Nat.findGreatest` below `2^n`). The table invariant `LandauInv ps g` (`g[m]` is the greatest `M ≥ 1` with `ℓ(M) ≤ m` whose prime factors lie in `ps`), `landauInv_init`, `landauInv_step` (the paper's induction relation: the optimum either avoids the new prime or is `p^k` times an optimum for `m − p^k`, via `Nat.ordProj_mul_ordCompl_eq_self`), `landauInv_foldl`, `landauFunctionPrefix_spec` (entry `i` is the greatest `M ≥ 1` with `ℓ(M) ≤ i`; every prime factor of such an `M` is at most `i`), `toNat_landauFunctionPrefix` (`= Nat.landau i`), `length_landauFunctionPrefix`. |
| `Equiv/Primorial` | `toNat_primorial` (`= primorial n`, Mathlib's `n#`, via `List.prod_toFinset` on the sieve's nodup list and `toFinset_primesUpTo`), `getElem_primesUpTo_eq_nth` (the sorted sieve enumerates `Nat.nth Nat.Prime`: the primes below entry `i` are the `i` earlier entries, a `Finset.card_bij`, then `Nat.nth_count`), `le_card_primes_two_pow` (Bertrand: at least `k + 1` primes up to `2^(k+1)`), `firstPrimes.go_spec` (the doubling loop ends with at least `n` primes), `firstPrimes_spec` (`size = n` and `(firstPrimes n)[i] = Nat.nth Nat.Prime i`), `toNat_productOfFirstNPrimes` (`= ∏ i < n, Nat.nth Nat.Prime i`). |
| `Equiv/Bell` | `bellTri n k = Σ_{i ≤ k} C(k, i) B_{n−i}` (the triangle entry), `bellTri_self` (`= Nat.bell (n+1)` from `Nat.bell_succ`), `bellTri_succ` (Pascal's rule gives the triangle recurrence), the row invariant `BellRow` with `bellRow_init`/`bellRow_next` (via `List.getElem_scanl_zero`/`getElem_succ_scanl`), `bellNumbersPrefix.go_spec`, `bellNumbersPrefix_spec` (`n` entries, entry `i` is Mathlib's `Nat.bell i`). For the single value: `Nat.stirlingSecond_succ_succ_eq_sum` (`S(n+1,k+1) = Σ_i C(n,i) S(i,k)`), `Nat.bell_eq_sum_stirlingSecond` (`Bₙ = Σ_k S(n,k)`, absent from Mathlib), `Nat.bell_le_pow` (`Bₙ ≤ nⁿ`, binomial theorem), the exact Dobinski identity `dobinskiQ_mul_bell` (`Q Bₙ = P + R`, from `pow_eq_sum_stirlingSecond_mul_descFactorial` and `k^{(j)} N!/k! = N!/(k−j)!`), the bound `dobinskiR_lt` (`R < Q` when `(N−n+1)! > n^{n+1}`), `bell_eq_dobinski_div`, and `bellNumber_spec` (`= Nat.bell n`). |
