| `Equiv/Pow` | `toNat_pow`, `toNat_powBinary`, `pow_eq_powBinary`, `ofNat_pow`: both exponentiation strategies agree with `Nat` powers. |
| `Equiv/RootInt` | `rootInt_spec` (via `toNat_rootInt` to the ℕ reference `rootIntNat` and MCA Thm 1.7 `rootIntNat_spec`): the integer k-th root satisfies `s^k ≤ m < (s+1)^k`; `isPow_eq_true_iff` (perfect-power test); `rootInt_two` (= `sqrt`); reusable `tangent_ineq` (integer Bernoulli) and `eq_of_pow_le_of_lt_pow`. |
| `Equiv/JacobiSym` | `jacobi_eq` (via the ℕ reference `jacobiNat_eq`): the binary Jacobi-symbol algorithm agrees with Mathlib's `jacobiSym` for odd moduli. |
| `Equiv/Pow2` | `toNat_pow2 k = 2 ^ k` and `pow2_eq_ofNat`. |
| `Equiv/LogBase2` | `floorLogBase2_eq` (`n.floorLogBase2 = some (Nat.log 2 n.toNat)` for `n ≠ 0`, `none` for `0`: one less than the bit length), `ceilingLogBase2_eq` (`= some (Nat.clog 2 n.toNat)`: the bit length, or one less for a power of two), `checkedLogBase2_eq_some_iff` (`n.checkedLogBase2 = some k ↔ n.toNat = 2 ^ k`) and `checkedLogBase2_eq_none_iff`; `size_bounds` (`2^(size−1) ≤ n < 2^size`).  Power-of-two bases `2^k` (`0 < k`): `floorLogBasePowerOf2_eq` (`= some (Nat.log (2 ^ k) n.toNat)`, the base-2 exponent divided by `k`), `ceilingLogBasePowerOf2_eq` (`Nat.clog`, ceiling division) and `checkedLogBasePowerOf2_eq_some_iff` (`= some e ↔ n.toNat = (2 ^ k) ^ e`, base-2 exponent divisible by `k`); `none` for base `1`. |
| `Equiv/LogBase` | General base `b ≥ 2`, `n ≠ 0`: `floorLogBase_eq` (`floorLogBase b n = some (Nat.log b.toNat n.toNat)`), `ceilingLogBase_eq` (`Nat.clog`), `checkedLogBase_eq_some_iff` (`= some e ↔ n.toNat = b.toNat ^ e`) and `_none_iff`.  The floor is a binary search on the exponent (`floorLogSearch_eq`: correct from any valid bracket `b^lo ≤ n < b^hi`), seeded by a `Float` estimate of `log₂ n / log₂ b` from the bit lengths and top 64 bits (`approxLog2`, `estimateLogBase`); the estimate's bracket is verified by two power comparisons and the proof never looks at it. |
| `Equiv/LowMask` | `toNat_lowMask k = 2 ^ k - 1`. |
| `Equiv/ModPow2` | `toNat_modPow2`: reduction mod `2^k` agrees with `Nat` mod. |
| `Equiv/TestBit` | `testBit_eq_toNat_testBit`: bit testing agrees with `Nat.testBit`. |
| `Equiv/SetBit` | `toNat_setBit`: bit setting agrees with the `Nat` semantics. |
| `Equiv/ClearBit` | `toNat_clearBit`: bit clearing agrees with the `Nat` semantics. |
| `Equiv/GetBits` | `toNat_getBits` / `toNat_getBitsAsLimb` and `getBits_eq_shiftRight_modPow2`: bit-range extraction agrees with shift-then-mod. |
| `Equiv/IsMultipleOfPow2` | `isMultipleOfPow2_iff`: the power-of-two divisibility test agrees with `2 ^ k ∣ toNat`. |
| `Equiv/Parity` | `isEven_iff` / `isOdd_iff` agree with `Even` / `Odd` on `Nat`. |
| `Equiv/Size` | `size_toNat` / `size_ofNat`: the limb-level bit-size agrees with Mathlib's `Nat.size`. |
| `Equiv/TrailingZeros` | `toNat_trailingZeros` / `trailingZeros_iff`: trailing-zero count correctness. |
| `Equiv/NormalizedCompare` | `normalizedCompare_eq_cross`: the allocation-free MSB-aligned comparison equals the cross-multiplied `compare` (see Utility Modules below). |
| `Equiv/RingEquiv` | Bundled forms of `toNat`: `ringEquivNat : AzNat ≃+* ℕ`, `toNatRingHom : AzNat →+* ℕ`, and `orderIsoNat : AzNat ≃o ℕ`, packaging the component lemmas so Mathlib's generic `map_*` and order-iso lemmas apply directly. |
| `Equiv/LimbDigits` | `toBase10p19Digits_eq_toBaseUInt64Digits`: the optimized base-10¹⁹ digit extraction agrees with the generic base-`UInt64` digit extraction. |
| `Equiv/LimbDigitsPow2` | `toNatLimbsList_eq_ofDigits` / `ofDigits_trimTrailingZeros`: power-of-two-base digit extraction correctness. |
| `Equiv/OfLimbDigits` | `toNat_horner_foldr` / `toNat_ofLimbs_single`: digit-sequence reconstruction via Horner evaluation. |
| `Equiv/OfLimbDigitsPow2` | `testBit_ofDigits_pow2`: bit-level characterization of power-of-two-base reconstruction. |
| `Equiv/DivBy6` | Correctness of the precomputed normalized-divisor/reciprocal constants used by `divBy6` (`divBy6_dNorm_toNat`, `divBy6_inv_eq`, …). |
| `ExactDivOdd` | Exact division by an odd limb `d` given its inverse `dinv = d⁻¹ mod 2^64` (Jebelean's algorithm, Brent–Zimmermann Alg. 1.10): quotient limbs low to high, one `wideMul` and one borrow subtraction per limb; inverse constants `inv3`/`inv5`/`inv9`/`inv45` checked by `decide`. For the Toom–Cook interpolation divisions. |
| `Equiv/ExactDivOdd` | `exactDivOdd_toNat`: `d * dinv = 1 → d ∣ n → (exactDivOdd d dinv n).toNat = n.toNat / d.toNat`, by the loop invariant `T_i = hi + borrow + d · (Q / β^i)` on the unprocessed limbs. |
| `Mul/SchonhageStrassen` | MCA Algorithm 2.4 on `AzFermat`: `fftMulModWith`/`fftMulMod` (`A·B mod (2^n+1)`, `n = 64 w · 2^(k+1)`, digits = limb blocks weighted by `θ^j`, two `forwardFFT`s, pointwise products by a caller-supplied multiplier, `backwardFFT`, division by `K θ^j`, window recovery `ssRecover`, Horner assembly in `AzFermat n`), the squaring variants, the parameter choice `ssParams`/`ssFermatExponent` (`K ≈ 2√L`, `N` the least multiple of `max K 64` above `2M + k + 1`), and `fftMul`/`fftSquare`/`fftMulLimbs`/`fftSquareLimbs` for exact integer products. |
| `Equiv/DivMod10p19` | Correctness of the precomputed base-10¹⁹ divisor constants used for decimal conversion (`divMod10p19_d_toNat`, `divMod10p19_inv_eq`, …). |
| `Equiv/ParseBase` | Base-`b` parsing helpers: `charToDigit_digitToChar` round-trip and the `parseDigitsInto` fold characterization. |
| `Equiv/ToStringBase` | `toStringBase_toNat`: base-`b` string rendering agrees with `Nat.toDigits`. |

#### AzFermat ↔ ZMod (2^N + 1)

| File | What it proves |
|------|----------------|
| `Equiv/Basic` | `toZMod` (injective on canonical residues), `toZMod_add`, `toZMod_sub`, `toZMod_neg`, `toZMod_reduceSplit`, `toZMod_reduceAny`, `toZMod_mulWith` (for any multiplier with `toNat (mulFn x y) = x·y`), `toZMod_mulPow2` (`= toZMod a · 2^t`), `toZMod_divPow2_mul_two_pow`; `two_pow_eq_neg_one` and `two_pow_two_mul_eq_one` for the modulus. |
| `Equiv/FFT` | `toZMod_forwardFFT` / `toZMod_backwardFFT`: the array transforms agree, position by position, with the functional `BZ.forwardFFT` / `BZ.backwardFFT` at `ω = 2^e`, hence (Theorems 2.1–2.2) are the DFT in bit-reversed order and its inverse in natural order. |

#### AzInt ↔ Int

| File | What it proves |
|------|----------------|
| `Equiv/Basic` | Bijection `AzInt ≃ Int` via `toInt`/`ofInt`, including the canonical-zero invariant round-trip. |
| `Equiv/Compare` | `compare_eq_lt_iff_toInt_lt` and friends: custom sign-magnitude `compare` matches `Ord.compare` on `Int`, providing the `LinearOrder AzInt` instance. |
| `Equiv/Conversion` | `toInt_toAzInt` / `toInt_toAzInt = toNat` for every fixed-width `UInt*`/`Int*`/`USize`/`ISize` and `AzNat`, and the reverse `toInt_toUInt*` / `toInt_toInt*` direction. |
| `Equiv/Add` | `toInt_add`: sign-magnitude addition agrees with `Int` addition. |
| `Equiv/Sub` | `toInt_sub`: sign-magnitude subtraction agrees with `Int` subtraction. |
| `Equiv/MulSmall`, `Equiv/Mul` | `toInt_mulUInt64` / `toInt_mulInt64` (multiplication by one limb, used by the Toom–Cook evaluation framework and kept free of the `AzNat` dispatcher's imports) and `toInt_mul`: sign-magnitude multiplication agrees with `Int` multiplication. |
| `Equiv/DivMod` | `toInt_edivMod`: `edivMod` agrees with `Int`'s Euclidean division and modulus. |
| `Equiv/DivRound` | `toInt_divRound`: rounded signed division agrees with `round intSet mode (x / y)` from the abstract `Rounding/` spec. |
| `Equiv/ShiftLeft` | `toInt_shiftLeft`: left shift agrees with `Int` semantics. |
| `Equiv/ShiftRight` | `toInt_shiftRight`: right shift agrees with floor-rounded division by `2^sh`. |
| `Equiv/ShiftRightRound` | `toInt_shiftRightRound`: rounded right shift agrees with rounding `z / 2^sh` against the `intSet` rounding target. |
| `Equiv/ShiftRightNearestTernary` | `toInt_shiftRightNearestTernary`: the signed version (the ternary swaps for negative values, `nearestTernary_neg`). |
| `Equiv/Parse` | `parse_toString`: parsing round-trips with `toString`. |
| `Equiv/RingEquiv` | Bundled forms of `toInt`: `ringEquivInt : AzInt ≃+* ℤ`, `toIntRingHom : AzInt →+* ℤ`, and `orderIsoInt : AzInt ≃o ℤ`, packaging the component lemmas so Mathlib's generic `map_*` and order-iso lemmas apply directly. |
| `Equiv/Parity` | `isEven_iff`/`isOdd_iff` agree with `Even`/`Odd` on `Int`. |
| `Equiv/Pow` | `toInt_pow z n = z.toInt ^ n` and `ofInt_pow`: `AzInt.pow` (sign rule over `AzNat`'s power, the `CommRing`'s `npow`) matches `Int` exponentiation. |
| `Equiv/Pow2` | `toInt_pow2 k = 2 ^ k`, `pow2_eq_ofInt`, and `isPowerOfTwo_iff`. |
| `Equiv/LowMask` | `toInt_lowMask k = 2 ^ k - 1` and `lowMask_eq_ofInt`. |
| `Equiv/Size` | `z.size = BPR.Int.size z.toInt` (bit-size agrees with the BPR Chapter 8 notion). |
| `Equiv/TrailingZeros` | `trailingZeros z = some (padicValInt 2 z.toInt)` for nonzero `z`, and `trailingZeros 0 = none`. |
| `Equiv/NormalizedGcd` | `toInt_ngcd`: the `NormalizedGcd` instance's nonnegative gcd represents Mathlib's normalized `ℤ` gcd; bundled as `intNormalizedGcdBridge : NormalizedGcdBridge AzInt ℤ` (+ surjectivity), the base of the nested-gcd correctness tower. |
| `Equiv/SumProduct` | `toInt_sum`/`toInt_product` (and the `Array` versions): the left-fold sum and the balanced product of a list of integers compute the sum and the product of the values, through `balancedProduct_eq_prod` in the ring `AzInt` and `List.prod_eq_zero` for the zero-factor exit. |
| `Equiv/CompareProduct` | `compareProduct_eq` (`compareProduct a b c = compare (a.toInt * b.toInt) c.toInt`): the product of two signed magnitudes is the signed product of the magnitudes, and `compare_signed` settles the sign cases, deferring to `AzNat.compareProduct_eq` for equal signs. |
| `Equiv/CompareProducts` | `compareProducts_eq` (`compareProducts a b c d = compare (a.toInt * b.toInt) (c.toInt * d.toInt)`): `toInt_mul_signed` writes each product as a signed product of magnitudes, `compare_signed` settles the sign cases and `AzNat.compareProducts_eq` the equal-sign ones. |
| `Equiv/ExtendedGcd` | `egcd_bezout` (`s·a + t·b = g` over `ℤ`) and `egcd_gcd` (`g = Nat.gcd a b`) for the size-dispatching `AzInt.egcd`, by dispatch. |
| `Equiv/ExtendedGcd/Binary` | `egcdBinary_bezout`/`egcdBinary_gcd` for the quadratic extended binary GCD (HAC Alg. 14.61): the Bézout identity needs only the loop invariant (exact halving + a parity lemma over `ZMod 2`); the gcd value needs fuel sufficiency, bounded on the odd parts `oddpart u + oddpart v ≤ 2^fuel`. |
| `Equiv/ExtendedGcd/HalfBinary` | `egcdHalfBinary_bezout`/`egcdHalfBinary_gcd` for the subquadratic extended half-binary GCD.  Every intermediate `ScaledBezout` row `r` satisfies `Spec` (`r.s x + r.t y = 2^r.e r.g`) and `GcdSpec` (`r.g` = odd part of `gcd(x, y)`): the base case from `egcdBinary_*`, each pull-back through a round's matrix by a ring identity (`pullBack_spec`, `pullBackWord_spec`, `pullBackStep_spec`) and the odd-part lemmas of the plain driver (`egcdDriver_spec`); `fixUp_spec` strips `2^e` — the correction `k ≡ −t x⁻¹ (mod 2^e)` (via `AzZModPow2.mul_invOdd` through `toZMod`) makes `t + k x` divisible by `2^e` and `s − k y` too because `x` is odd (`IsCoprime`), so the two exact shifts give a Bézout row for `g`; `egcdHalfBinaryWith_spec` undoes the preparation as `toNat_gcdHalfBinaryWith` does. |

#### AzRat ↔ ℚ

| File | What it proves |
|------|----------------|
| `Equiv/Basic` | Round-trips `toRat_ofRat` / `ofRat_toRat` bundled as `equivRat : AzRat ≃ ℚ`, including the canonical-zero and coprimality invariants; `toRat_injective` for transporting `ℚ`-side identities. |
| `Equiv/Order` | The verified `LinearOrder AzRat` built on `cmp` (`compare = cmp`; laws transferred from `ℚ` via `cmp_eq_compare` and `le_iff_toRat_le`/`lt_iff_toRat_lt`), the bundled `orderIsoRat : AzRat ≃o ℚ`, `toRat_max`/`toRat_min`, and `abs_eq_max_neg` (`AzRat.abs` is the order-theoretic absolute value). |
| `Equiv/Conversion` | `toRat_toAzRat` (`AzNat`), `toRat_toAzRat_int` (`AzInt`), and per-primitive `UInt*`/`Int*`/`USize`/`ISize` `*.toRat_toAzRat` — every conversion into `AzRat` agrees with the `toNat`/`toInt` cast into `ℚ`. |
| `Equiv/Compare` | `cmp_eq_compare`: the staged limb-level fast comparison agrees with `compare` on `ℚ`. |
| `Equiv/Construct` | `toRat_ofAzNats` / `toRat_ofAzInts` (via `toRat_ofSignAzNats`): the pair constructors compute exactly `n / d` as field division in `ℚ` — no nonzero hypotheses, since `den = 0 ↦ 0` matches `x / 0 = 0`. Also `toRat_zero` and `ofSignAzNats_self` (rebuilding an `AzRat` from its own fields is the identity). |
| `Equiv/Parse` | `parse_toString : parse (toString q) = some q` — the string round-trip. Compositional over `AzInt.parse_toString`/`AzNat.parse_toString`, the slash-free-numerator lemma `AzInt.toString_ne_slash`, the `splitAtSlash` characterization lemmas, and `ofSignAzNats_self`. |
| `Equiv/Unary` | `toRat_neg`/`toRat_abs`/`toRat_inv` and `ofRat_neg`/`ofRat_abs`/`ofRat_inv` (all `@[simp]`): negation, absolute value, and reciprocal agree with the `ℚ` operations in both directions. `toRat` proofs in `Rat.divInt` normal form; `ofRat` forms by `toRat_injective`. |
| `Equiv/Mul` | `toRat_mul`/`ofRat_mul` (`@[simp]`): cross-gcd-reduced multiplication computes `ℚ` multiplication in both directions, via `Rat.divInt_mul_divInt` + the cross-multiplication identity (substitute `a = a'·g₁` etc. and re-associate). |
| `Equiv/Div` | `div_eq_mul_inv : x / y = x * y⁻¹` (structural — `inv` swaps the divisor's fields, so `mul`'s cross-gcds against `y⁻¹` are syntactically `div`'s); `toRat_div`/`ofRat_div` (`@[simp]`) then chain the multiplication and reciprocal theorems. |
| `Equiv/Shift` | `toRat_shiftLeft`/`toRat_shiftRight` and `ofRat_shiftLeft`/`ofRat_shiftRight` (`@[simp]`): the gcd-free shifts compute `· * 2^n` and `· / 2^n` exactly, via the reassembly identity `2^(n-k) · 2^k = 2^n` after undoing the exact division. `roundVal_mul_two_zpow`: rounding commutes with an exact shift (`roundVal` of `r · 2^k` is `roundVal` of `r` shifted, same tag), via `val_round_mul_two_zpow` and `log_abs_mul_two_zpow`. |
| `Equiv/SumProduct` | `toRat_sum`/`toRat_product` (and the `Array` versions): both balanced trees compute the sum and the product of the values (`balancedSum_eq_sum`/`balancedProduct_eq_prod` in the field `AzRat`, `List.prod_eq_zero` for the zero-factor exit). |
| `Equiv/CompareProduct` | `compareProduct_eq` (`compareProduct x y z = compare (toRat x * toRat y) (toRat z)`): `screen3_spec` (three factorwise comparisons pointing one way decide the comparison of the three-factor products, by `mul3_lt_of_le`), `mul3_lt_of_size` (a surplus of three bits), `compareProductMag_eq` (`nx·ny·dz` against `nz·dx·dy`, the last resort through `AzNat.compareProducts_eq`), `compare_div` (two fractions with positive denominators compare like their cross products) and the sign dispatch through `AzInt.compare_signed`. |
| `Equiv/CompareProducts` | `compareProducts_eq` (`compareProducts w x y z = compare (toRat w * toRat x) (toRat y * toRat z)`): the four-factor screens `screen4_spec` (via `mul4_lt_of_le`) and `mul4_lt_of_size`, `compareProductsMag_eq` (`nw·nx·dy·dz` against `ny·nz·dw·dx`), and the cross-product and sign reductions shared with `compareProduct`. |
| `Equiv/Add` | `toRat_add`/`ofRat_add` (`@[simp]`): gcd-splitting addition computes `ℚ` addition in both directions. Key bridge `combineSigned_int_spec` (`±(combineSigned …) = ±u + ±v` in `ℤ`); the sign-conditionals are then treated as shared atoms by `linear_combination`, avoiding any sign-case explosion. |
| `Equiv/Sub` | `sub_eq_add_neg : x - y = x + (-y)` (structural — negation only flips the sign bit, so `add`'s body applied to `-y` is syntactically `sub`'s body applied to `y`); `toRat_sub`/`ofRat_sub` (`@[simp]`) then chain the addition and negation theorems. |
| `Equiv/Pow` | `toRat_pow`/`ofRat_pow` and `toRat_zpow`/`ofRat_zpow` (`@[simp]`): componentwise powers compute `ℚ` exponentiation (sign side mirrors `AzInt.toInt_pow`'s parity split); `zpow_negSucc : q.zpow (-(n+1)) = (q.pow (n+1))⁻¹` (structural — the direct swapped implementation *is* `inv` of the power). |
| `Equiv/RingEquiv` | Bundled forms of `toRat`: `ringEquivRat : AzRat ≃+* ℚ`, `toRatRingHom : AzRat →+* ℚ`, and `orderRingIsoRat : AzRat ≃+*o ℚ` (the `OrderRingIso` merging `ringEquivRat` with `orderIsoRat` from `Equiv/Order` — the strongest bundled form for a linearly ordered field: a field's `⁻¹`/`/` are ring-determined, so there is no separate "field equivalence" beyond `≃+*`, whose `map_inv₀`/`map_div₀` apply since both sides are fields). |
| `Equiv/LogBase2` | `floorLogBase2Abs_eq` / `ceilingLogBase2Abs_eq`: the limb-level base-2 logarithms equal `⌊logb 2 \|toRat q\|⌋` / `⌈logb 2 \|toRat q\|⌉`.  `checkedLogBase2_eq_some_iff` (`q.checkedLogBase2 = some k ↔ toRat q = 2^k`, `k : AzInt`) and `checkedLogBase2_eq_none_iff`, via `toRat_of_isPowerOfTwo` (moved here from the `AzFloat` layer) and its converse `isPowerOfTwo_of_toRat_eq_zpow` (a reduced fraction equal to `2^j` has numerator or denominator `1`, by coprimality). |
| `Equiv/LogBase`, `Equiv/LengthAfterPoint` | `floorLogBaseAbs_eq` (`= Int.log b |toRat q|`, by bracketing and binary search on `b^e` versus `|q|`, no floating-point estimate), `ceilingLogBaseAbs_eq` (`= Int.clog b |toRat q|`: the floor plus one unless `|q| = b^floor`, decided by `cmpPowAbs`), `checkedLogBaseAbs_eq_some_iff` (`= some e ↔ |toRat q| = b^e`, `e : ℤ`) and `_none_iff`; **rational bases** `floorLogBaseRatAbs`/`ceilingLogBaseRatAbs`/`checkedLogBaseRatAbs b q : Option ℤ` (`none` unless `0 < b`, `b ≠ 1`, `q ≠ 0`): `floorLogBaseRatAbs_eq` (`= some ⌊logb (toRat b) |toRat q|⌋`), `ceilingLogBaseRatAbs_eq` (`⌈…⌉`), `checkedLogBaseRatAbs_eq_some_iff` (`= some e ↔ |toRat q| = (toRat b)^e`).  For `b > 1` a binary search on the exponent with the cross-multiplied comparison `cmpPowRatAbs` (`cmpPowRatAbs_eq`), seeded by a `Float` estimate whose bracket is verified (`floorLogSearchRat_eq` from any valid bracket) with a doubling fallback whose fuel is justified by Bernoulli's inequality (`lt_pow_two_pow`: `b^(2^F) > m` for `F ≥ size (den b) + size m`); a base in `(0, 1)` goes through its reciprocal (`Real.logb_inv_base`, `Int.floor_neg`/`ceil_neg`); **power-of-two bases** `floorLogBasePowerOf2Abs`/`ceilingLogBasePowerOf2Abs`/`checkedLogBasePowerOf2Abs q k : Option ℤ` (base `2^k`, `0 < k`): the base-2 exponents floor-/ceiling-divided by `k` (`floorLogBasePowerOf2Abs_eq : = some (Int.log (2 ^ k) |toRat q|)`, `ceilingLogBasePowerOf2Abs_eq` with `Int.clog`, via `int_log_two_pow`/`int_clog_two_pow`) and `checkedLogBasePowerOf2Abs_eq_some_iff` (`= some e ↔ |toRat q| = (2^k)^e`, via `abs_toRat_eq_zpow_iff`: `|q| = 2^j` iff numerator and denominator are powers of two with exponent difference `j`) and `lengthAfterPoint_some_iff` / `lengthAfterPoint_none_iff` (`some L` iff `L` is the least exponent with `den ∣ b^L`, via `Nat.factorization` and the prime-power table of the base). |
| `Equiv/ToSci` | Stage 1 of `toSci`: `toInt_scaledRound` (the scaled rounding is `round intSet mode (toRat q · b^scale)`), `toSciNumber_value_scale` (`scale s` yields `round (scaleSet b s) mode (toRat q)`), `toSciNumber_value_precision` (`precision p` yields `round (precisionSet b p) mode (toRat q)`, including the carry into a power of the base), `toSciNumber_complete` (`complete` is exact and succeeds iff the expansion terminates), `toSciExact_iff` (the exactness predicate holds iff the printed value is `toRat q` itself), and `toSciNumber_wellFormed` (requested base, digits below the base, nonzero leading digit). |
| `Equiv/FromSci` | Round trips: `fromSci_toString` (parsing the rendering of a well-formed `SciNumber` — digits below the base, nonzero leading digit — recovers its value, for every `SciFormat` with a negative exponent threshold; scale and exponent within `i64`), `fromSci_toSci` (`fromSci` undoes `toSci` up to the rounding `toSci` performed: the parsed value is the intermediate `SciNumber`'s value) and `fromSci_toSci_of_exact` (`fromSci` recovers `q` itself when `toSciExact` holds). |
| `Equiv/Round` | `toInt_round`: rounding agrees with the abstract `RoundingTarget.round intSet mode (toRat q)`; `snd_round`: the returned `Ordering` tag is `compare` of the rounded value vs `toRat q`. |

#### AzFloat ↔ Option EReal

| File | What it proves |
|------|----------------|
| `Equiv/Basic` | The value model `toVal : AzFloat → Option EReal` (`none` for `NaN`, `⊤`/`⊥` for `±∞`, the exact real `finiteVal` otherwise); `toVal_mkFinite` (left-aligning does not change the value), `precision?_mkFinite`, `toVal_neg`, `toVal_abs`. |
| `Equiv/Conversion` | `toVal_ofAzNat`/`toVal_ofAzInt` (exact), `toVal_of_toAzRat?` (`toAzRat?` returns the value), and `toVal_ofAzRatRound` (`= round (precisionSet 2 p) mode (toRat q)`: rounding a rational to `p` bits is rounding to the numbers with at most `p` significant bits, the same target as `toSci` in base 2), with `precision?_ofAzRatRound` (the precision is `p`) and `snd_ofAzRatRound` (the `Ordering` compares the result with `q`). |
| `Equiv/Rounding` | The floats of a fixed precision as a rounding target: `floatSet p` (values of the non-`NaN` floats of precision `p`, zero and `±∞` included) equals `precisionSet 2 p ∪ {⊤, ⊥}` (`floatSet_eq`), giving a `RoundingTarget` instance whose rounding of a real agrees with `precisionSet 2 p`'s (`val_round_floatSet`). The noncomputable conversions `ofEReal p mode : EReal → AzFloat` (`±∞` stay, a real is rounded to precision `p`) and `ofVal p mode : Option EReal → AzFloat` (`none ↦ nan`), with `toVal_ofEReal_coe`, `ofEReal_top`/`ofEReal_bot`. Uniqueness of the representation: `toVal_injective` (two floats of precision `p`, or special, with the same value are equal), hence `ofAzRatRound_eq_ofEReal` (the computable rounding of a rational *is* `ofEReal` of its value) and `ofVal_toVal` (rounding a float's own value at its precision gives it back). `floatSymmetricRoundingTarget`: the target is symmetric (finite candidates defer to `precisionSet`'s tiebreak, a finite one beats an infinite one), so `round_neg` applies. **Lifting**: `roundVal p mode : Option EReal → AzFloat × Ordering` (round a specification value and compare the result with it), `liftVal f x p mode` / `lift (f : ℝ → ℝ) top bot x p mode`, the binary `liftVal₂`/`lift₂` and the nullary `liftVal₀ v p mode` (a constant is its correctly rounded value): every `AzFloat` operation is specified as a computable implementation of a lift; `roundVal_coe`, `roundVal_of_toVal`, `lift_coe`, `liftE` for total `EReal → EReal` functions, and the first instance `neg_eq_lift` (negation is the exact lift of `EReal` negation, whose `-(±∞) = ∓∞` needs no special-value table). |
| `Equiv/Precision` | `setPrecRound_eq_liftE` (`setPrecRound x p mode = liftE id x p mode`: re-rounding is the lift of the identity), via `normalize_spec` (an integer `r` with `2^(p−1) ≤ \|r\| ≤ 2^p` placed at exponent `e`, carry halved, is a precision-`p` float of value `r · 2^(e − p)`), `log_abs_finiteVal` (`⌊log₂ \|v\|⌋ = e − 1`), `precScale_finiteVal`, `finiteVal_eq_core`; `ulp?_spec`. |
| `Equiv/Compare` | `partialCompare_eq` (the comparison of the `toVal`s, `none` iff a `NaN` is involved), `compareMagnitude_eq` (exponents decide by `2^(e−1) ≤ \|v\| < 2^e`; at equal exponents the limb-padded significands compare as the values), `partialCompare_eq_none_iff`, `eqIEEE_iff`. |
| `Equiv/Shift` | `toVal_shiftLeft`/`toVal_shiftRight` (the value is multiplied by `2^k`, with `EReal` fixing `±∞`) and `shiftLeft_eq_liftE` (at its own precision the shift is the exact lift of `v ↦ v · 2^k`). |
| `Equiv/HexString` | `ofHexChars_toHexChars` / `ofHexString_toHexString`: reading back the hexadecimal debug rendering gives the float itself, for every float (structural equality, no side conditions). The writer's digits `N` satisfy `value = N · 16^(E − D + 1)`; the reader recovers `N`, the fractional digit count and the exponent, and the rebuilt binary exponent equals the original in each of the three layouts. |
| `Equiv/ToString` | `SciNumber.toRat_toAzRat` (the computable rational of a well-formed `SciNumber` is its `value`) and `SciNumber.value_eq_fraction` (so is the unreduced `fraction`); `searchLeast_pred`/`le_searchLeast`/`searchLeast_min` and `searchLeastFromTop_pred`/`le_searchLeastFromTop`/`searchLeastFromTop_min` (the binary search returns a value satisfying the predicate, within bounds, and the least one for a monotone predicate); `toDecimalString_spec` (the output is the `toSci` rendering of a `SciNumber` at the found precision, whose value converts back to the float whenever the round-trip predicate holds there, which `decimalRoundTrips_shortest` reduces to the search's upper bound). |
| `Equiv/OfString` | `toVal_ofDecimalStringRound` (a parsable decimal is read as the rounding of its exact value, with the comparison tag) and `ofDecimalString_toDecimalString` (reading `toDecimalString x` back at the precision of `x` gives `x`, under the run-time round-trip predicate and the parser's exponent bounds), via `fromSci_ensurePoint_toString`: parsing undoes the writer's `.0` convention. |
| `Equiv/Sci` | `toSci_eq`/`toSciNumber_eq` (the rendering is `AzRat.toSci` of the exact value) and `toSciNumber_value_precision` (at `p` significant digits the printed number is the rounding of the value to `p` base-`b` digits). |
| `Equiv/Literals` | `toVal_ofNat` (integer literals are exact) and `toVal_ofScientific` (a scientific literal is the decimal rounded to nearest at `literalPrecision` bits). |
| `Equiv/Float64` | `toVal_ofFloat64` (exact), `unpack_pack`/`model_unpack_pack` (the model's `pack` and `unpack` are inverse on binary64-representable unpacked floats, `UnpackedFloat.Binary64`), `toUnpacked_binary64` (every result of `toUnpacked` is representable), hence `ofFloat64_toFloat64 : ofFloat64 (toFloat64 x mode) = ofUnpacked (toUnpacked x mode)` with no side condition, and the value theorems `toVal_ofFloat64_toFloat64_normal` (`round (precisionSet 2 53) mode (toVal x)` for exponents in `[-1021, 1023]`) and `toVal_ofFloat64_toFloat64_subnormal` (rounding to a multiple of `2^-1074` below `2^-1022`). Helpers: `roundFromFloor`-style sign and bound lemmas for `round intSet`, `size_eq_54_iff`, `smallToNat_eq`. |
| `Equiv/RoundScaled` | `roundScaled_eq_roundVal` (rounding an exact `±S · 2^w` is `roundVal`), `roundVal_congr_cell` (two reals in the same open cell `(k·2^j, (k+1)·2^j)` with `j ≤ ⌊log₂\|v\|⌋ − p` round identically in every mode, with the same comparison tag — the lemma behind the far case of addition and behind Ziv's strategy), `normalizeCarry_spec`, `roundFromFloor_spec` (integer rounding of a real in `[s, s+1)` from its floor, exactness and midpoint position, all modes with the even tiebreak), the special values `roundVal_top/bot/zero/inf`, and value lemmas for finite floats with an arbitrary significand. |
| `Equiv/Add` | `addPrecRound_eq_liftVal₂` (`addPrecRound x y p mode = liftVal₂ Spec.add x y p mode`, where `Spec.add` is `EReal` addition with `∞ + (−∞)` undefined) and `subPrecRound_eq_liftVal₂`, via the exact near case and `roundVal_far` (the far operand only moves the sum inside one cell). |
| `Equiv/Mul` | `mulPrecRound_eq_liftVal₂` (`Spec.mul`: `EReal` multiplication with `0 · (±∞)` undefined; the sign of an infinite product from `Spec.mul_inf_coe`/`mul_coe_inf`) and `sqrPrecRound_eq_liftE` (squaring is the exact-value lift of `v ↦ v · v`). |
| `Equiv/Div` | `divPrecRound_eq_liftVal₂` (`Spec.div`: `EReal` division with `∞ / ∞`, `0 / 0` undefined and `a / 0 = ±∞`), via `quotient_scale_bounds` (the shifted quotient lies in `[2^(p−1), 2^p)`) and `divCores_eq` (the rounded integer quotient is the `precisionSet` rounding of the exact quotient, by `AzInt.toInt_divRound`); `divCores_eq_gen` (the quotient lemma for any two nonzero integers), `ofFractionRound_eq` and `fst_ofFractionRound` (rounding the unreduced fraction is rounding the rational it represents). |
| `Equiv/Sqrt` | `sqrtPrecRound_eq_liftVal` (`Spec.sqrt`: `EReal` square root, undefined on negatives) via `scaled_floor` and `sqrtCore_eq`. |
| `Equiv/SqrtRat` | `sqrtRatPrecRound_eq` (`sqrtRatPrecRound q p mode = roundVal p mode (Spec.sqrt q)`) via `floorLog2_bounds` (the exponent from the sizes and `normalizedCompare_eq_cross`), `sqrtRatCore_eq` (floor of the integer quotient, exactness and midpoint by `compareProduct_eq`) and the reusable tail `roundFromFloor_normalizeCarry_eq` (a `roundFromFloor` result normalized with exponent `E` is `roundVal` of `y · 2^(E − p)`); `rsqrtRatPrecRound_eq` (`= roundVal p mode (Spec.rsqrt q)`) from the same core via `Real.sqrt_inv`. |
| `Equiv/Rsqrt` | `rsqrtPrecRound_eq_liftVal` (`Spec.rsqrt`: `1/√x`, undefined on negatives) via `rsqrtCore_eq` and the three scalings of `2^D / n`. |
| `Equiv/Ziv` | Correctness of Ziv's loop: `roundVal_congr_of_no_boundary` (two reals with no float of precision `p + 1` between them, inclusive, have the same `roundVal` — the cell lemma applied to the cells cut out by the boundaries), `Rounds` (a `Roundable` is the rounding procedure of a given real), `roundingPossible_eq`, `zivLoop_eq` (if every approximation brackets the exact value, rounding is possible from a working precision `W` on, and the fuel reaches `W`, the loop returns `roundVal` of the exact value), `le_mul_two_pow_zivFuel`; the truncation errors `lt_add_ulp_of_floor` (a float whose value is the floor of `x` at its precision lies less than one of its ulps below `x`), `sub_roundFloor_lt_precScale`, `eq_zero_of_roundFloor_eq_zero` (only `0` truncates to `0`), `truncError_spec` (including that the truncation plus its error is again a float of the working precision); `ofAzRatRound_eq_roundVal` (the pair form of `ofAzRatRound_eq_ofEReal`); and the arithmetic of boundaries shared by the termination proofs: `mem_floatSet_mul_zpow`, `boundary_repr` (a nonzero boundary is `M · 2^k` with `k ≥ ⌊log₂|b|⌋ − p`), `exists_int_mul_sub_dyadic`, `one_div_le_abs_of_mul_eq_int`; and the geometry shared by the termination proofs: `adjacent_boundaries` (the boundaries adjacent to a non-boundary `v` have at least its binade), `no_boundary_of_dist` (a bracket of `v` narrower than the distance to them contains no boundary), `roundingPossible_isSome_of_no_boundary`, `two_zpow_lt_one_div`, `neg_le_log_of_one_div_le`. |
| `Equiv/AddSubRat` | `addRatPrecRound_eq_liftVal` (`addRatPrecRound x q p mode = liftVal (fun a => Spec.add a q) x p mode` with `q` the rational's value), `subRatPrecRound_eq_liftVal`, `ratSubPrecRound_eq_liftVal`, via `addRatApprox_spec` (the two ends of the bracket round `x + lo` and `x + lo + ε` with `lo ≤ q ≤ lo + ε`, `ε = 0` exactly when the truncation was exact) and the termination theorem `addRatApprox_possible`: from the working precision `p + |num| + 2|den| + |m| + |e| + 2` on rounding is possible, because the exact sum is either a boundary — then `q` is dyadic (`toRat_mem_precisionSet_of_dyadic`) and the bracket is a point — or at distance at least `1/(den · 2^a)` from the adjacent boundaries (`dist_sum_boundary`), while the bracket's width is at most `2^(|num|+1−w)`; `addRatBound_le` (the fuel reaches that precision). |
| `Equiv/MulRat` | `mulRatPrecRound_eq_liftVal` (`mulRatPrecRound x q p mode = liftVal (fun a => Spec.mul a q) x p mode`), via `shift_ofFractionRound_eq` (rounding an unreduced fraction and shifting the result exactly is `roundVal` of the fraction times the power of two, from `ofFractionRound_eq` and `roundVal_mul_two_zpow`), `finiteVal_mul_toRat` (the value as a shifted fraction) and `sign_eq_decide_pos` (the sign of a nonzero rational is the sign of its value). |
| `Equiv/DivRat` | `divRatPrecRound_eq_liftVal` (`Spec.div a q`) and `ratDivPrecRound_eq_liftVal` (`Spec.div q a`), via `finiteVal_div_toRat`, `toRat_div_finiteVal` and the special-value lemmas of `Spec.div`. |
| `Equiv/Constants` | Each constant is the nullary lift of its value: `sqrt2PrecRound_eq_liftVal₀`, `sqrt3PrecRound_eq_liftVal₀`, `sqrt5PrecRound_eq_liftVal₀` (from the square-root lift at the exact floats, `toVal_two`, `toVal_ofAzNat_ofNat`), `sqrt2Over2PrecRound_eq_liftVal₀`, `sqrt3Over3PrecRound_eq_liftVal₀`, `sqrt5Over5PrecRound_eq_liftVal₀` (from the reciprocal-square-root lift, `inv_sqrt_eq`). For `φ`: `phiApprox_spec`/`phiApprox_encloses` (width `≤ 2^(1 − w)`), `irrational_phi`, `phiApprox_isSome_exists` (the Ziv certificate; the loop is in `Phi`).; `cbrt2PrecRound_eq_liftVal₀` (`= liftVal₀ (some (realCbrt 2))`, from the cube-root lift). |
| `Phi` | **The golden ratio** `φ = (1 + √5)/2`: `phiPrecRound p mode`, `phi p` — the certified Ziv loop (`zivLoopCertified`, no fuel) over `phiApprox` of `Constants`, certified by `irrational_phi`; `(nan, .eq)` at precision `0`. (Previously fuel-based with an ad hoc boundary-distance bound `phiApprox_possible`, replaced 2026-10-25.) |
| `Equiv/Phi` | `phiPrecRound_eq_liftVal₀` (`= liftVal₀ (some φ)`) via `zivLoopCertified_eq_threshold` and `phiApprox_encloses`. |
| `Equiv/ProuhetThueMorse` | The real number `prouhetThueMorseConstant := ∑' n, tₙ / 2^(n+1)` and `prouhetThueMorsePrecRound_eq_liftVal₀`. The sequence: `prouhetThueMorseSeq_pow_mul_add` (`t (2^k a + b) = t a xor t b` for `b < 2^k`), `t (2^k) = 1`, `t (3·2^k) = 0`. The words: `ptmWord k` (`T₀ = 0`, `T_(k+1) = T_k ∥ ¬T_k`) is the integer of the first `2^k` terms (`ptmWord_eq_sum`) and `ptmWord 6` is the literal word by `decide`; `toNat_prouhetThueMorseLimb`, `toNat_prouhetThueMorseLimbs` (the integer of the first `64N` bits, via `toNatLimbsList_ofFn` and the regrouping `sum_limbs_eq`). The real: `sum_ptmTerm` (the partial sum is that integer over `2^K`), `tail_bounds` (the tail lies strictly between `0` and `2^-K`), hence `1/4 < τ < 1/2`; the rounding assembles as for the square root from `roundFromFloor_spec` and `normalizeCarry_spec`. |
| `Equiv/PrimeConstant` | The real number `primeConstantReal := ∑' n, [n prime] / 2^n` and `primeConstantPrecRound_eq_liftVal₀`. `toNat_wordOfBits` (a word assembled bit by bit is `Σ [f l] 2^l`, with the no-overflow invariant), `sum_mul_pow_regroup` (the limbs regroup into one sum over bit positions), `toNat_primeConstantLimbs` (the integer of the first `64 N` bits is `Σ_{n ≤ 64N} [n prime] 2^(64N − n)`, by `primeSieve_testBit_iff` and `Finset.sum_range_reflect`); `sum_pcTerm`, `tail_bounds'` (the tail lies strictly between `0` and `2^-E` by `Nat.exists_infinite_primes` and the composite `4(E+1)`), `1/4 < ρ < 1/2`, and the rounding assembly shared with the Prouhet–Thue–Morse constant. |
| `Equiv/Sum` | `sumPrecRound_eq` (`sumPrecRound xs p mode = roundVal p mode (Spec.sum xs)`, where `Spec.sum` folds `Spec.add` over the values from `0`, so a `NaN` or an `∞ + (−∞)` anywhere gives `NaN`), through `Spec.sum_eq` (the four-way case form), the exactness of `exactAdd`/`exactSum` (`exactSumPrec` holds the sum: integers at the common scale, bounded by the span), `splitWindow_spec` (the window and the rest, whose exponents lie more than `W` below the maximum), `tailBound_spec` (`|ΣR| < T`), `signSum_eq` and `sumFinite_eq` (induction on the fuel, which exceeds the length; in the main branch `floor_err_le` bounds the outward rounding of the ends by `2^(E+3−W)`, `boundary_dist` separates two `(p+1)`-bit floats of magnitude at least `2^(E−2)` by `2^(E−2−p)`, so the bracket holds at most one boundary, `setPrecRound_floor_spec` identifies it as the truncation of the upper end, and `roundVal_congr_of_no_boundary_Ioo` rounds the midpoints like the sum). `toVal_sumFloat64`: the correctly rounded `Float` sum `sumFloat64` (defined in `Sum`) is the binary64 rounding (`roundBinary64Val`) of the fold of `Spec.add` over the `Float`s' values. |
| `Equiv/Product` | `productPrecRound_eq` (`productPrecRound xs p mode = roundVal p mode (Spec.product xs)`, where `Spec.product` folds `Spec.mul` over the values from `1`), through `Spec.product_eq` (the five-way case form: a `NaN`; a zero with an infinity; an infinity with the sign of the product; a zero; the real product), `realProd_spec` (the real product of finite nonzero floats is nonzero and its sign is `productSign`) and `realProd_eq` (it is the signed product of the cores on the summed scale that `roundScaled` rounds, by `AzNat.toNat_product` and `AzInt.toInt_sum`). Also the constructor forms of `isNaN`/`isInfinite`/`isZero`/`isNegative` as simp lemmas. `toVal_productFloat64`: the correctly rounded `Float` product `productFloat64` (defined in `Product`) is the binary64 rounding (`roundBinary64Val`) of the fold of `Spec.mul` over the `Float`s' values. |

#### AzPolynomial ↔ Polynomial R

| File | What it proves |
|------|----------------|
| `Equiv/Basic` | Bijection `AzPolynomial R ≃ Polynomial R` via `toPoly`/`ofPoly`; degree, natDegree, coeff, leadingCoeff, nextCoeff, and monic preservation. |
| `Equiv/Add` | `toPoly (p + q) = toPoly p + toPoly q` |
| `Equiv/Sub` | `toPoly (p - q) = toPoly p - toPoly q` |
| `Equiv/Neg` | `toPoly (-p) = -(toPoly p)` |
| `Equiv/Mul` | `toPoly (p * q) = toPoly p * toPoly q` (basecase) |
| `Equiv/MulXPow` | `toPoly (mulXPow n p) = toPoly p * X ^ n` |
| `Equiv/Karatsuba` | `mulKaratsuba p q = mulBasecase p q` (Karatsuba agrees with basecase, hence with Mathlib) |
| `Equiv/SMul` | `toPoly (c • p) = c • toPoly p` |
| `Equiv/Monomial` | `toPoly (monomial n c) = Polynomial.monomial n c` |
| `Equiv/Map` | `toPoly (map f p) = Polynomial.map f (toPoly p)` |
| `Equiv/Eval` | `eval x p = Polynomial.eval x (toPoly p)` and `evalSpecial p b c = c^natDeg · (toPoly p).eval(b·c⁻¹)` (BPR Algorithm 8.8) |
| `Equiv/QuoRem` | `toPoly (quo P Q) = toPoly P / toPoly Q`, `toPoly (rem P Q) = toPoly P % toPoly Q`, and `degree(rem) < degree(Q)` |
| `Equiv/PRem` | `(toPoly (pRem P Q)).map (algebraMap D K) = BPR.PRem K (toPoly P) (toPoly Q)` for `D` a domain with fraction field `K` (matches BPR §1.3 signed pseudo-remainder) |
| `Equiv/RootBound` | Cauchy root bound correctness: all roots lie within `(-rootBound, rootBound)` |
| `Equiv/Algebra` | Ring homomorphism and algebra structure preservation |
| `Equiv/Comp` | `toPoly (comp p q) = (toPoly p).comp (toPoly q)` |
| `Equiv/Derivative` | `toPoly (derivative p) = Polynomial.derivative (toPoly p)` |
| `Equiv/Pow` | `toPoly (p.pow n) = toPoly p ^ n` and `(ofPoly p).pow n = ofPoly (p ^ n)` |
| `Equiv/Translate` | `toPoly (translate p c) = (toPoly p).comp (X - C c)` and root translation: root at `r` ↔ root of `P` at `r-c` (BPR Algorithm 8.9) |
| `Equiv/Truncate` | `toPoly (truncate i p) = BPR.truncate i (toPoly p)` and coefficient characterization `(truncate i p).coeff j = if j ≤ i then p.coeff j else 0` (BPR Notation 1.16) |
| `Equiv/SpecialTranslate` | `eval z (specialTranslate p b c) = evalSpecial p (c·z−b) c`, field identity `= c^deg · eval(z−b/c) P`, root translation (field and via ring hom `f : R →+* K`) (BPR Algorithm 8.10) |
| `Equiv/SturmSequence` | `toPoly (sturmSequence P n) = Azurite.BPR.sturmSequence (toPoly P) n` (BPR Chapter 2.2). Computable Sturm sequence via the signed remainder sequence agrees with the noncomputable Polynomial-side construction. |
| `Equiv/GcdTower` | `map_toPoly_ngcdPoly`: UFD-generic one-level correctness of the tower gcd — under a bridge package (`ρ : D →+* M` injective, divisibility-reflecting, with `ρ ∘ ngcd = gcd`), `(toPoly (ngcdPoly P Q)).map ρ` is Mathlib's normalized `M[X]` gcd. Bundled as `NormalizedGcdBridge` with the induction step `NormalizedGcdBridge.step` (surjective bridge `D → M` ⟹ bridge `AzPolynomial D → M[X]`). Generalizes `Equiv/GcdInt`'s ℤ/ℚ argument to `M`/`Frac(M)`. Also `contentGen_mul` (bundled `NormalizedGcdBridge.contentGen_mul'`): the concrete content fold is **multiplicative** (`contentGen (p·q) = contentGen p · contentGen q`), the multivariate Gauss core, via `map_contentGen` + Mathlib `Polynomial.content_mul` over the `NormalizedGCDMonoid` model. |

### Generic Algorithms

| Module | Description |
|--------|-------------|
| `Airy/Series` | Airy functions, foundation of the planned `AzFloat` `Ai`/`Bi`/`Ai′`/`Bi′` (plan in `docs/azfloat_plan.md`, section "Airy functions"). The Maclaurin series `f = ∑ x^(3k)/d_k`, `g = ∑ x^(3k+1)/e_k` (`fDen`, `gDen`, `fTerm`, `gTerm`, term ratios, `d_k ≥ (2k)!`), growth bounds `|f| ≤ cosh(|x|^(3/2))`, `|g| ≤ |x| cosh(|x|^(3/2))`, truncation `|f − ∑_{k<K}| ≤ 2|t_K|` once `|x|³ ≤ (3K+2)(3K+3)/2` (`abs_f_sub_sum_le`, `halving_of_le`), exact rational partial sums (`sum_fTerm_div_eq`, `fPartialNum/Den`), termwise derivatives (`hasDerivAt_f`, `hasDerivAt_g`), the equation `f″ = xf`, `g″ = xg` (`f_ode`, `g_ode`), `W(f,g) = 1`, the constants `c₁ = 3^(−2/3)/Γ(2/3)`, `c₂ = 3^(−1/3)/Γ(1/3)` with `c₁c₂ = 1/(2√3π)`, and `Ai`, `Bi`, `AiD`, `BiD` with `Ai″ = x Ai`, `Bi″ = x Bi` and `W(Ai,Bi) = 1/π` (`wronskian_Ai_Bi`). |
| `Algorithm/FastPow` | Right-to-left binary exponentiation (exponentiation by squaring) for any `Monoid`. Computes `a ^ n` in O(log n) multiplications. Proven equivalent to `HPow.hPow` (Mathlib's `^`). |
| `Algorithm/SlidingWindowPow` | Left-to-right **sliding-window** exponentiation for any `Monoid`. Precomputes the `2^(w-1)` odd powers `a^1,…,a^(2^w-1)` with the window width `w` growing with the exponent's bit-length (`slidingWindowSize`), so it uses fewer multiplications than `FastPow` for larger exponents (cubing is one squaring + one multiplication). Proven equivalent to `HPow.hPow` (`slidingWindowPow_eq_pow`). Kept alongside `FastPow` for benchmarking. |
| `Algorithm/BalancedProduct` | `balancedProduct`: the product of a list by a balanced binary tree of pairwise products (`mulAdjacentPairs` rounds driven by a fuel of the list length, a fold as fallback), so every multiplication has operands of similar size; PROVEN `balancedProduct_eq_prod` in any `Monoid`, for every fuel; `balancedSum`/`balancedSum_eq_sum` are the `to_additive` twins. |
| `Algorithm/PrimeSieve` | The sieve of Eratosthenes with the table **bitpacked into an `AzNat`** (8× denser than `Array Bool`; `clearBit` mutates a limb in place under linear use): `primeSieve n` has bit `j` set iff `j` is prime, `primesUpTo n` extracts the sorted prime array, and `smallPrimes` caches the first 100 primes (through 541) as a literal guarded against the sieve. Correctness in `Algorithm/Equiv/PrimeSieve` via the loop invariant “bit `j` is set iff `2 ≤ j` and every prime `p < d` dividing `j` has `p² > j`”: `primeSieve_testBit_iff`, `mem_primesUpTo`, `primesUpTo_sorted`, `smallPrimes_prime` — all 3-axiom clean (bit-level only, no division). |
| `Algorithm/ExactDiv` | `ExactDiv D` typeclass: `exactDiv a b : D` returns `c` such that `b * c = a` when `b ∣ a`. Default instance for any `Field K` (uses `a / b`). Instances: `AzInt` (`AzInt/ExactDiv`), `AzPolynomial R` over any `[CommRing R] [DecidableEq R] [ExactDiv R]` (`AzPolynomial/ExactDiv`, synthetic division), and `AzMvPolynomial n R ord` over any `[CommRing R] [IsDomain R] [DecidableEq R] [ExactDiv R]` (`AzMvPolynomial/ExactDivCommRing`, sibling of the existing field-only `AzMvPolynomial.exactDiv`). Used by `AzMatrix/BareissDet` so the fraction-free Bareiss determinant runs end-to-end over `AzInt`, `AzPolynomial AzInt`, or `AzMvPolynomial n AzInt ord` via the chained instances. |

### Utility Modules

| Module | Description |
|--------|-------------|
| `Rounding/` | Abstract rounding framework. `RoundingMode` (`Floor`/`Ceiling`/`Down`/`Up`/`Nearest`, with a negation involution swapping `Floor`/`Ceiling`) and the `RoundingTarget S` typeclass on subsets `S : Set EReal` (every real has a greatest element of `S` below it and a least element above it, plus a `tiebreak` for `Nearest`); `SymmetricRoundingTarget` adds `0 ∈ S`, negation closure, and a sign-compatible tiebreak, yielding the symmetry theorem `round_neg`. Instances: ℤ (`intSet`, round-to-even tiebreak), ℕ∪{−∞} (`natBotSet`; bare ℕ proven *not* a target), bounded integer ranges with ±∞ (`intIccBotTopSet`), `UInt64`/`Int64` ranges (`uInt64BotTopSet`/`int64BotTopSet`), and ℝ (trivially); ℚ proven *not* a target (density without order-closedness fails at irrationals). Bridges/helpers: `NatBotInt` (`natBotSet` and `intSet` rounding agree on nonnegative reals), `NatDivPow` (shared lemmas for rounding `v / 2^sh`). This is the correctness spec for the computable rounding operations `divRound`/`shiftRightRound` in `UInt64/`, `AzNat/`, `AzInt/` and for `AzRat.round`. `Rounding/Sci` and `Rounding/SciPrecision` add the targets of `toSci`: `scaleSet b s` (multiples of `b^(−s)`; `val_round_scaleSet`: rounding is integer rounding of `x · b^s` scaled back) and `precisionSet b p` (numbers with at most `p` significant base-`b` digits; `val_round_precisionSet`: for `x ≠ 0` it is integer rounding at the local scale `b^(⌊log_b |x|⌋ − p + 1)`, tiebreak to the even coordinate); `precisionSet` is moreover a `SymmetricRoundingTarget` (`precisionSymmetricRoundingTarget`: its tiebreak only looks at magnitudes, so `round_neg` applies). `Rounding/IntPrecision`: `intPrecisionSet p`, the integers with at most `p` significant bits, as a `RoundingTarget` (floor/ceiling are the integer floor/ceiling below `2^(p−1)` and the `p`-bit ones from there on; tiebreak `ipTiebreak`, the parity rule at the local scale floored at `1`), with the characterizations `val_round_intPrecision_small` (`= round intSet`, ties to even) and `val_round_intPrecision_large` (`= round (precisionSet 2 p)`) — the specification of `AzFloat.roundToIntegerPrecRound`. `Rounding/IntNearest`: facts about `round intSet .Nearest` (`abs_sub_toInt_round_nearest_le`, `abs_toInt_round_nearest_sub_le`: it is a nearest integer; `toInt_round_nearest_half`: ties to even) and `NearestTernary y R o` (a nearest integer with an exact tie resolved by a ternary) with `toInt_round_nearest_of_ternary`: re-rounding a nearest rounding on a finer grid that contains the coarse grid and its midpoints, with the ternary, equals direct nearest rounding — the double-rounding repair behind `AzFloat/Float64Emulate`. |
| `Transcendental/Exp` | **Hermite's theorem**: `e` is transcendental (`transcendental_exp_one : Transcendental ℚ (Real.exp 1)`, also over `ℤ`), from Mathlib's `LindemannWeierstrass.exp_polynomial_approx` applied to `rootsPoly n = ∏ (X − k)`: a relation `∑ a_k e^k = 0` gives the integer `E = a₀nₚ + p∑ a_k gₚ(k)` with `|E| ≤ (∑|a_k|) c^p/(p−1)! < 1` for a large prime `p`, so `p ∣ a₀ nₚ`, contradiction (`aeval_exp_one_ne_zero`). Corollaries: `transcendental_exp_nat`, `transcendental_exp_rat` (`e^q`, `q ≠ 0`), `irrational_exp_one`, `irrational_exp_rat`, `irrational_log_rat` (`ln r`, `r ∈ ℚ_{>0}∖{1}`), `irrational_log_two` — the termination certificate for the `AzFloat` constant `ln 2`. |
| `Transcendental/Ramanujan` | **`e^(π √163)` is not an integer, but within `10^(−12)` of `640320³ + 744` — by computation**: the kernel evaluates `ramanujanPrecRound 110 .Floor`/`.Ceiling` (`decide +kernel` on `ratData`, the sign/numerator/denominator of the result; about 4 minutes each), `ramanujanPrecRound_eq_or` + `roundVal_floor_err`/`roundVal_ceiling_err` + `toVal_of_toAzRat?` turn the rounded floats into `⌊x⌋₁₁₀ ≤ x ≤ ⌈x⌉₁₁₀` (`toVal_of_ratData`), and two rational comparisons with 34-digit numerators finish `ramanujan_gt`/`ramanujan_lt`; corollaries `ramanujan_near_integer`, `ramanujan_not_integer`, `floor_ramanujan`. No `native_decide`, no big-operand `norm_num` certificates. |
| `UInt64/` | Helpers on Lean's built-in `UInt64` type. **Carry/borrow and wide arithmetic**: `addWithCarry`, `subWithBorrow`, `mulWithCarry`, `mulAddWithCarry`, `wideMul` (full 128-bit product as a `(hi, lo)` pair), and multi-limb primitives `wideAdd`/`wideAdd3`/`wideSub`/`wideSub3`. The multiply carry chains add their carries as conditional *scalars* (never branching on the result pair), so Lean's codegen keeps the `(hi, lo)` pairs in registers instead of boxing them at control-flow join points — measured 1.5–1.6× on AzNat mul/square; the bridge lemma `add_ite_zero` (`Equiv/WideMul`) lets the correctness proofs still reason about the branching form. **Division**: `divMod`, plus the Möller–Granlund machinery — precomputed reciprocals (`reciprocal`, `reciprocal3By2`) and the two-/three-limb-by-one/two division steps (`div2By1`, `div3By2`) underlying `AzNat`'s schoolbook division. **Rounded division**: `divRound` and `shiftRightRound`, correct against the `Rounding/` spec. **Integer square root**: `sqrtRem` (floating-point initial guess + integer correction; the correctness proof needs no floating-point semantics). **Bit operations**: `isPowerOfTwo`, `isMultipleOfPow2`, `testBit` (returns `false` for `i ≥ 64`), `setBit`/`clearBit` (identity for `i ≥ 64`), `leadingZeros`, `trailingZeros` (lowest-set-bit isolation plus the hardware `ln2`; `Equiv/TrailingZeros` proves `trailingZeros_eq_ctz`, so no hot path goes through the slow `BitVec.ctz`). **Halves and digits**: `splitInHalf`/`joinHalves` (`UInt64` ↔ pair of `UInt32` halves), base-`b` digit conversion (`digits`/`ofDigits` with optimized power-of-two paths), and `maxPow` (largest base power fitting in 64 bits, for batched string conversion). `Equiv/Basic` provides `UInt64.eq_of_toNat_eq`; the remaining `Equiv/*` files prove the `toNat` semantics of every helper (e.g. `toNat_wideAdd`, `toNat_wideMul`, `toNat_div2By1`, `toNat_div3By2`, `toNat_divRound`). |
| `AzNat/NormalizedCompare` | `normalizedCompare`: **allocation-free** comparison of two `AzNat`s by their normalized (MSB-aligned) bit representations — reads the `k`-th limb of `x·2^shift` on the fly via `getBitsAsLimb` (no temporary shifted `AzNat`), comparing top-down against `y`'s limbs. Proven (`normalizedCompare_eq_cross`, axiom-clean) equal to the cross-multiplication `compare (x·2^size(y)) (y·2^size(x))`, i.e. comparing `x / 2^size(x)` vs `y / 2^size(y)`. |
| `Random/` | Random generators for `Nat`, `Int`, `Rat`, `Bool`, `AzPolynomial`, pairs, and geometric distributions. Used for testing and benchmarks. |
| `Benchmark/` | Performance benchmarks: polynomial multiplication (basecase vs Karatsuba), `AzNat` add/sub/mul/square (schoolbook vs Karatsuba vs Toom-Cook 3, and vs `Nat`), division algorithms (schoolbook vs recursive, div/mod vs divMod, for `AzNat` and `AzInt`), exponentiation strategies, integer square root (`AzNat` and `UInt64`), and a limb-width experiment (`LimbWidth`: boxed 64-bit versus tagged 32-bit limbs, addition pass and schoolbook product). Uses a C FFI nanosecond timer; result charts live in `charts/`. `az_nat_div_newton` (`AzNatDivNewton`): classical `divModClassic` vs `divModNewton base` at exact sizes, config `limbs:…,bases:…,ratio:r,iters:k` (note that `az_nat_div_cross` goes through `divModWith`, whose columns all take the Newton path once `useNewtonDiv` holds). |
| `Oracle/` | The Malachite oracle, `lake exe oracle <mode> <file>`: reads the lines a Malachite demo printed, recomputes each with Azurite, and exits nonzero on the first disagreement. The Azurite backend of Malachite's differential-testing driver (`oracle-test/` in the Malachite repository); every mode is strict, erroring on an unrecognized line or an input in which nothing was checked. `Parse` takes apart Malachite's `Display`/`Debug` renderings (tuples, vectors, options, orderings, rounding modes, `&` borrow markers); `AzNat` holds one check per `Natural` demo line shape, including Malachite's base-62 digit rule and its `Exact` rounding mode, which Azurite lacks; `AzInt` does the same for the `Integer` demos, checking Malachite's Euclidean and floor division families by name (its `/` truncates, Azurite's is Euclidean) and the documented normalization of its Bézout coefficients; `AzZModPow2` checks the `mod_power_of_2_*` demos against the residue type, requiring the printed inputs to be reduced as Malachite does, and `AzZMod` the general-modulus `mod_*` demos (including `mod_div`, checked by its documented existence condition and the defining congruence, since the quotient is not unique); `AzRat` the `Rational` demos, including `to_sci` with Malachite's `ToSciOptions` parsed into `SciOptions` and `from_sci_string` in every base; `AzFloat` the `Float` demos in their exact hexadecimal `_debug` form, through `MalachiteFloat`, which emulates Malachite's exponent range (`clamp`: `±∞` or the largest finite value above `2^30 - 1`, zero or the smallest positive value below `-(2^30 - 1)`, by Malachite's documented per-mode rules, the `Nearest` tie at half the smallest value settled by the `Ordering` of Azurite's rounded result); `AzNatExtra` the remaining `Natural` demos built from existing Azurite functions (comparisons with primitives, Rust's `format!` padding, limbs, logarithms, CRT, scientific strings, the exhaustive generators); `AzNatFloat` the `Natural`–`f32`/`f64` conversions and scientific mantissas, reading each printed float back as an exact rational rounded to the IEEE 754 format (`ieeeNearest`, subnormals included) and overflowing after rounding, as IEEE 754 does. |

### Formalized Textbook Content

| Module | Content |
|--------|---------|
| `DLMF/Chapter1/Section2` | Formalization of the NIST *Digital Library of Mathematical Functions* (<https://dlmf.nist.gov>), one theorem `dlmf_C_S_N` per equation C.S.N, each a thin restatement of a Mathlib lemma (named in its docstring) or proven here; ledger `docs/dlmf_plan.md`. §1.2(i) Binomial coefficients, 1.2.1–1.2.9: `C(n, k) = n!/((n−k)! k!) = C(n, n−k)` (`Nat.choose_eq_factorial_div_factorial`, `Nat.cast_choose`, `Nat.choose_symm`), the binomial theorem (`add_pow`), `∑ C(n, k) = 2^n`, the vanishing alternating sum, the even-index sum `2^(n−1)` (proven from the two), the generalized `C(z, k)` for `z : ℂ` as Mathlib's `Ring.choose` with its three forms (falling product, `(−1)^k (−z)_k / k!`, `(−1)^k C(k − z − 1, k)`), Pascal's rule (`Ring.choose_succ_succ`), the hockey-stick sum `∑_{k ≤ m} C(z + k, k) = C(z + m + 1, m)` and the alternating sum `(−1)^m C(z − 1, m)` (both by induction). §1.2(ii) Finite series, 1.2.10–1.2.11: the arithmetic progression (`2 ∑ = 2na + n(n−1)d` in any commutative ring, the `/2` forms in a field of characteristic zero) and the geometric progression (`geom_sum_eq`). §1.2(iii) Partial fractions, 1.2.12–1.2.16: simple poles (`partialFractions_poly`, the polynomial identity `f = ∑ Aⱼ ∏_{k≠j} (X − αₖ)` by evaluation at the `n` poles, then `dlmf_1_2_13` with `Aⱼ = f(αⱼ)/∏_{k≠j}(αⱼ − αₖ)` and the existence form `dlmf_1_2_12`), an `n`-fold pole (`dlmf_1_2_15` with `Bⱼ = f^{(n−j)}(α)/(n−j)!` from the Taylor expansion `sum_taylor_eq`/`taylor_coeff`/`factorial_smul_hasseDeriv`, existence form `dlmf_1_2_14`), and mixed multiplicities (`dlmf_1_2_16` from Mathlib's `eq_quo_mul_prod_add_sum_rem_mul_prod` with the quotient shown to vanish by degrees). §1.2(iv) Means, 1.2.17–1.2.26: `arithMean`, `geomMean`, `harmMean`, the weights `IsWeights`, the weighted mean `weightedMean p a r` with the convention `M(r) = 0` for `r < 0` and a vanishing `aⱼ`; `M(r) → max` as `r → ∞` (a squeeze), `M(r) → min` as `r → −∞` (through the reciprocals), `M(1) = A`, `M(−1) = H` and `M(r) → G` as `r → 0` (the derivative of `log` of the weighted power sum at `0`) for the uniform weights. §1.2(v) Matrices, 1.2.27–1.2.37: `Matrix (Fin m) (Fin n) ℂ`, transpose, conjugate, Hermitian conjugate, scalar multiplication, the addition laws, the row-times-column rule, associativity, distributivity and `(AB)ᵀ = BᵀAᵀ` (all Mathlib restatements). |
| `CrandallPomerance/Chapter2` | Formalization of Crandall & Pomerance, *Prime Numbers: A Computational Perspective* (2nd ed.), Chapter 2 (number-theoretical tools), as the primality-proving arc reaches back for them. `Theorem_2_1_6.lean`: **the Chinese remainder theorem in the book's explicit form** — for positive pairwise coprime moduli `m_0, …, m_(r−1)` with product `M` and residues `n_i`, the system `n ≡ n_i (mod m_i)`, `0 ≤ n < M` has a unique solution (`theorem_2_1_6`), given explicitly by the least nonnegative residue mod `M` of `Σ n_i v_i M_i` where `M_i = M/m_i` and `v_i M_i ≡ 1 (mod m_i)` (`theorem_2_1_6_explicit` — the CRT analogue of Lagrange interpolation; Mathlib carries only the list-iterated two-moduli construction). The inverses exist because `M_i` is the product of the moduli other than `m_i` (`exists_crt_inverse`); uniqueness via the reusable `modEq_prod_iff` (congruence mod a pairwise-coprime product ⟺ congruence mod each factor, Fin-indexed). `Algorithm_2_1_7.lean`: **Garner's CRT reconstruction with preconditioning** — the proven reference rail for the limb-level `AzNat.garner`/`AzNat.invMod` (see the algorithm table), including `invMod_unique` (the reduced inverse is unique below the modulus — how differently-computed Bézout pairs are reconciled). |
| `CrandallPomerance/Chapter4` | Formalization of Crandall & Pomerance, *Prime Numbers: A Computational Perspective* (2nd ed.), Chapter 4, toward a verified APR-CL primality proving algorithm. `Theorem_4_1_2.lean`: **the Pépin test** (`theorem_4_1_2`) — for `k ≥ 1`, the Fermat number `F_k = 2^(2^k) + 1` is prime **iff** `3^((F_k−1)/2) ≡ −1 (mod F_k)`. Sufficiency is Mathlib's `Nat.pepin_primality'` (Lucas's theorem with witness 3); the NECESSITY direction is ours (`pepin_necessity`): `F_k ≡ 5 (mod 12)` (`fermatNumber_mod_twelve`, via `2^(2^k) = 4^(2^(k−1))` and `4^m ≡ 4 (mod 12)`), so `F_k ≡ 1 (mod 4)` flips quadratic reciprocity sign-free and `F_k ≡ 2 (mod 3)` reduces the Legendre symbol to `(2|3) = −1`; Euler's criterion (`legendreSym.eq_pow`) pins the half-exponent power. The `k ≥ 1` hypothesis is needed only for necessity (`F_0 = 3` is prime but `3 ≡ 0 (mod 3)`). The book's Theorem 4.1.1 (Lucas) is Mathlib's `lucas_primality`, already load-bearing in `AzNat/Equiv/Pratt`. `Theorem_4_1_3.lean`: **Pocklington's theorem** (`pocklington`) — if `n − 1 = F·R` and a witness satisfies `a^(n−1) = 1` in `ZMod n` with `a^((n−1)/q) − 1` a **unit** of `ZMod n` (the structural form of `gcd(·, n) = 1`) for every prime `q ∣ F`, then every prime factor of `n` is `≡ 1 (mod F)` — the workhorse of partial-factorization `n−1` tests and the ancestor of the APR-CL divisor-confinement idea. Proof by transport along `ZMod.castHom` to `ZMod p`: for each `q^e ∥ F` the element `b^((n−1)/q^e)` has order exactly `q^e`, so `F` divides `orderOf b`, which divides `p − 1` by Fermat. `Corollary_4_1_4.lean`: **the Pocklington certificate** (`corollary_4_1_4`) — add `n ≤ F²` (i.e. `F ≥ √n`) and `n` is prime outright (every prime factor exceeds `F ≥ √n`, but a composite `n` has one with `p² ≤ n`, Mathlib's `Nat.minFac_sq_le_self`); so a PARTIAL factorization of `n−1` past `√n` suffices. Worked example: `97` certified prime through the corollary with `96 = 32·3`, witness `5`, kernel-checked power conditions. `Theorem_4_1_5.lean`: **the Brillhart–Lehmer–Selfridge test** (`theorem_4_1_5`) — with the Pocklington conditions and only a CUBE-ROOT-sized factored part (`F² < n ≤ F³`), write `n = c₂F² + c₁F + 1` in base `F`; then `n` is prime **iff** `c₁² − 4c₂` is not a square (over `ℤ`). Forward: a square discriminant makes `x² − c₁x + c₂` factor with integer roots `u, v ≥ 1` (parity + `c₂ ≥ 1` from `F < √n`), giving `n = (uF+1)(vF+1)`. Converse: a composite `n` has EXACTLY two prime factors (each `≡ 1 (mod F)` exceeds `F`, and `n ≤ F³ < (F+1)³`), and the base-`F` digits of `n − 1` match `(ab, a+b)` — the boundary `a+b = F` is killed by `c₂ < F` — so the discriminant is `(a−b)²`. Worked example: `191` prime via `190 = 10·19` (Pocklington's corollary does NOT apply at `F = 10 < √191`), witness `7`, discriminant `77`. `Theorem_4_1_6.lean`: **the Konyagin–Pomerance test** (`theorem_4_1_6`) — for `n ≥ 214` with the Pocklington conditions and a factored part of only exponent-3/10 size (`F³ < n`, `n³ ≤ F¹⁰`), primality is equivalent to (1) the six shifted discriminants `(c₁+tF)² + 4t − 4c₄` (`t ≤ 5`) all non-square, and (2) a cubic built from a rational approximation `u/v` of `c₁/F` (any pair with `v²n < F⁴` and `(uF−c₁v)²F² ≤ n` — the book's continued-fraction convergent qualifies) having no integer root giving a nontrivial factor `xF+1`. The composite direction runs the book's argument integer-natively: digit matching forces `t ≥ 6`, the estimate `2|DF − c₄v| < F` (via `9X²F⁸ ≤ n³ ≤ F¹⁰` and `36Y²v²F¹²n < F¹⁴n`) pins `D = a₁u + a₁tv` to the nearest integer `d = ⌊c₄v/F + 1/2⌋`, and `a₁` becomes a root of the cubic. `n ≥ 214` enters as `214³ > 5¹⁰ ⟹ F ≥ 6`. Reusable `not_isSquare_intCast_of_lt_of_lt` (strictly between consecutive squares). Worked example: `223` prime via `222 = 6·37`, `F = 6` (below both Pocklington-corollary and BLS reach), witness `3`, `u/v = 0/1`, cubic `x³ − x² − 1`. `theorem_4_1_6'` restates KP with condition (2) replaced by the bounded divisor condition its proof delivers (no `(u,v)` pair or cubic; scan range `~n^(1/10)`) — the form the computable checker discharges. **Algorithm 4.1.7 (the n−1 test)** is implemented and proven sound at the limb level in `AzNat/NMinusOneTest` (see the algorithm table). `LucasSequences.lean` opens §4.2: the Lucas sequences `(U_k), (V_k)` for `f(x) = x² − ax + b` (defined by the recurrence, manifestly integers and computable), with the book's `ℤ[x]/(f)` formulation (4.13) recovered as a theorem — Binet in an arbitrary commutative ring (`lucasU_spec`/`lucasV_spec`: `U_k(α−β) = α^k − β^k`, `V_k = α^k + β^k` whenever `α+β = a`, `αβ = b`; no division, no separability), specialized to `AdjoinRoot` with `α, β` the images of `x, a−x`; `(α−β)² = Δ` (`sub_sq_eq_disc`); rank of appearance `r_f(n)` (Def 4.2.1) as an `sInf` with membership/minimality lemmas. `Theorem_4_2_2.lean`: the addition formula `U_{m+1+n} = U_{m+1}U_{n+1} − bU_mU_n`, **`(U_k)` is a divisibility sequence** (`lucasU_dvd_lucasU`), consecutive terms share no divisor coprime to `b`, and the **rank characterization** `n ∣ U_j ↔ r_f(n) ∣ j` (`dvd_lucasU_iff_rankApp_dvd`, for `gcd(n,b) = 1`). **Theorem 3.6.3** (`theorem_3_6_3`): `p ∣ U_{p−(Δ/p)}` for `p ∤ 2bΔ` — split case by Fermat on the explicit roots `(a ± √Δ)/2` in `ZMod p`; inert case in `GF(p²) = AdjoinRoot` via Frobenius swapping the roots (`α^p = β`, with the fixed-point exclusion by root-counting `X^p − X`). **Theorem 4.2.2** (`theorem_4_2_2`): `r_f(p) ∣ p − (Δ/p)`. `Theorem_4_2_3.lean`: **Morrison's n+1 test** — under `gcd(n,2b) = 1`, Jacobi `(Δ/n) = −1`, `F ∣ n+1`, `U_{n+1} ≡ 0 (mod n)`, and `gcd(U_{(n+1)/q}, n) = 1` for primes `q ∣ F`, every prime `p ∣ n` has `p ≡ (Δ/p) (mod F)` (`theorem_4_2_3`; via the Pocklington-style valuation climb showing `F ∣ r_f(p)`, then Thm 4.2.2); with `n < (F−1)²` (i.e. `F > √n + 1`), `n` is prime (`morrison_test`). Worked example: `13` proven prime via Fibonacci with `F = 7`. `Theorem_4_2_4.lean`: **the density theorem** (C&P's exercise, our proof) — for odd `p`, the pairs `(a,b) ∈ (ZMod p)²` with `(Δ/p) = −1` and `r_f(p) = p+1` number exactly `(p−1)φ(p+1)/2` (`theorem_4_2_4`, stated as `2N = (p−1)·φ(p+1)`). Proof in a constructed `GF(p²) = AdjoinRoot (X² − c)`: the rank is the multiplicative order of `α^{1−p}` (`rankApp_eq_orderOf`), discrete logs reduce the condition to `gcd(p+1, i) = 1` (`card_units_orderOf_pow_sub_one`), and trace/norm is 2-to-1 onto the pairs. Reusable: Frobenius-fixed-points-are-the-prime-field descent (`mem_range_algebraMap_of_pow_card`), quadratic root existence in `GF(p²)` via GG Thm 14.2 (`exists_root_of_irreducible_quadratic`), Euler nonsquare criterion (`legendreSym_disc_eq_neg_one`), block-counting of coprime residues (`card_range_mul_filter_coprime`). `Theorem_4_2_5.lean`: **Morrison's test in V-form** (the version binary Lucas chains compute) — with `F` an EVEN divisor of `n+1`, `V_{F/2} ≡ 0 (mod n)`, and `gcd(V_{F/2q}, n) = 1` for odd primes `q ∣ F`, every prime `p ∣ n` has `p ≡ (Δ/p) (mod F)` (`theorem_4_2_5` — here the rank equals `F` *exactly*), and `n < (F−1)²` gives primality (`morrison_test_V`). Supporting ℤ-elementary Lucas identities in `LucasSequences.lean`: `V_m = 2U_{m+1} − aU_m`, the norm invariant `U_{m+1}² − aU_{m+1}U_m + bU_m² = b^m` (one-step induction), `V_m² − ΔU_m² = 4b^m`, and the doubling `U_{2m} = U_mV_m`. Worked example: `13` prime via `x² − x + 2` with `F = 14`. `Theorem_4_2_6.lean`: **the Lucas–Lehmer test** (`theorem_4_2_6`) — for odd prime `p`, the Mersenne number `M_p = 2^p − 1` is prime **iff** `v_{p−2} ≡ 0 (mod M_p)`, where `v_0 = 4`, `v_{k+1} = v_k² − 2` (stated with Mathlib's `LucasLehmer.s`; Mathlib has only sufficiency — the NECESSITY direction is ours, `s_of_mersenne_prime`). Take `f = x² − 4x + 1`, `Δ = 12`; the V-doubling `V_{2m} = V_m² − 2b^m` (`lucasV_two_mul`) at `b = 1` gives `V_{2^k}(4,1) = v_k` (`lucasV_two_pow_eq_s`), and `M_p ≡ 3 (mod 4)`, `≡ 1 (mod 3)` give `(12/M_p) = −1` by Jacobi reciprocity (`jacobiSym_twelve`). Sufficiency (`mersenne_prime_of_s`) is Theorem 4.2.5 with `F = 2^(p−1)` — the odd-prime gcd conditions are vacuous. Necessity: in `GF(M²) = AdjoinRoot (x² − 4x + 1)` with `α^M = β = 4 − α` (Frobenius), `(α−1)² = 2α` plus Euler's criterion for `2` (`M ≡ 7 (mod 8)`) evaluates `(α−1)^(M+1)` two ways to `2·α^((M+1)/2) = −2`, so `α^((M+1)/2) = β^((M+1)/2) = −1`; hence `M ∣ U_{2^(p−1)}` but `M ∤ U_{2^(p−2)}` (else `−1 = (αβ)^(2^(p−2)) = 1`), and `U_{2m} = U_mV_m` forces `M ∣ V_{2^(p−2)} = v_{p−2}`. Worked instances: `M_7 = 127` proven prime, and `M_11 = 2047 = 23·89` proven COMPOSITE from `2047 ∤ v_9` via the necessity direction. **Algorithm 4.2.7 (the Lucas–Lehmer test)** is implemented and proven correct at the limb level in `AzNat/LucasLehmerTest` (see the algorithm table). `Theorem_4_2_8.lean`: **the cube-root `n+1` test** (`theorem_4_2_8`, the `n+1` analogue of BLS) — under the Morrison hypotheses with `n + 1 = (r₁F + r₀)·F`, `r₀ < F`, and `F > n^(1/3) + 1` (integer form `n < (F−1)³`), `n` is prime **iff** neither `x² + r₀x − r₁` nor `x² + (r₀−F)x − r₁ − 1` has a positive integral root; contains `morrison_test` (when `R < F`, `r₁ = 0` kills both quadratics). Composite direction: two prime factors `p = cF+1`, `q = dF−1` with split Jacobi signs and `1 ≤ c,d ≤ F−1`, then base-`F` digit-matching of `R = cdF + (d−c)` makes `c` a root. Prime direction: a root `c` rebuilds `n = (cF+1)((c+r₀−iF)F−1)` exactly, uniformly in `i`. Worked instances: `127` prime via Fibonacci `F = 8` (below `morrison_test`'s `√n`-reach), and `119 = 7·17` — which PASSES the Morrison conditions for `x² − 3x − 1`, `F = 8` — caught by the `i = 1` quadratic, whose root `c = 2` reveals the factor `17`. `Theorem_4_2_9.lean`: **the Konyagin–Pomerance-style `n+1` test** (`theorem_4_2_9`) down to `F ≥ n^(3/10)` — the book leaves the proof as a “completely analogous to 4.1.6” exercise; the proof is ours, and the formalization uncovered TWO ERRORS in the book's statement. (i) The stated band `F ≤ n^(1/3)+1` is too generous: for the PRIME `n = 223`, `F = 7` (in band, `c₄ = 4`) the `t = 4` discriminant is `32²` — the trivial split `1·n` masquerades as a factorization and condition (1) fails for a prime; the corrected hypothesis is `6 ≤ c₄` (automatic when `F³ < n`). (ii) The book's second cubic `vx³ + (uF−c₁v)x² − (c₄v + dF + u)x + d` has a sign error; the correct one is `vx³ + (uF−c₁v)x² + (dF − c₄v − u)x − d` (both verified numerically). Statement: under (4.14) with `n + 1 = c₄F² + c₁F`, `c₁ < F`, `6 ≤ c₄`, `n³ ≤ F¹⁰`, any `(u,v)` with `v²n < F⁴`, `(uF−c₁v)²F² ≤ n`, and `d = ⌊c₄v/F + 1/2⌋`: prime ⟺ the ELEVEN discriminants `(c₁+tF)² − 4t + 4c₄` (`|t| ≤ 5`) are non-squares AND neither cubic has a root giving a nontrivial `xF+1` / `xF−1` factor. Composite direction: divisors are `≡ ±1 (mod F)` so `n = (xF+1)(yF−1)` splits off the least prime factor directly (no two-prime-count needed); `t := c₄ − xy`; `|t| ≤ 5` → disc `= (x+y)²`; `t ≥ 6` → `D := x(u+tv)` pinned to `d` by the budget `6(5xW) + 2(15x²v) + 5(6tv) ≤ 13(F−1)`; `t ≤ −6` → `F ≥ 124` forced (kernel check of 118 cube inequalities), `D := y(−tv − u)` pinned, `y` roots cubic 2. Worked instance: `251` prime via `(1,2)`, `Δ = −7`, `F = 6`, cubics `x³ − x + 1`, `x³ − x − 1` rootless. `Theorem_4_2_10.lean`: **the Brillhart–Lehmer–Selfridge COMBINED `n−1`/`n+1` test** — with the Pocklington conditions (4.3) at `F₁ ∣ n−1` and the Morrison conditions (4.14) at `F₂ ∣ n+1`, every prime factor of `n` is `≡ 1` or `≡ n (mod lcm(F₁,F₂))` (`theorem_4_2_10`; case `(Δ/p) = 1` puts `p ≡ 1` mod both parts, case `(Δ/p) = −1` puts `p ≡ n` mod both since `n ≡ 1 (mod F₁)`, `≡ −1 (mod F₂)`); and if `lcm(F₁,F₂) > √n` with `n mod lcm` not a nontrivial factor, `n` is prime (`bls_combined_test`) — partial factorizations past `n^(1/4)` on EACH side suffice, doubling the one-sided reach. Worked instance: `199` prime from `198 = 9·22` (witness `2`) + `200 = 8·25` (Lucas `(2,−2)`, `Δ = 12`), `lcm(9,8) = 72 > √199`, `199 mod 72 = 55` not a factor — neither one-sided √-test applies. `Theorem_4_2_12.lean`: **Lenstra's divisors-in-residue-classes** (Algorithm 4.2.11 / Theorem 4.2.12) — `d ∣ n ∧ d ≡ r (mod s)` ⟺ `d = xs + r` from a nonnegative solution of (4.16) with `xaᵢ + ybᵢ` in the `i`-th window (`theorem_4_2_12`), plus the congruence (4.20) `xaᵢ + ybᵢ ≡ cᵢ (mod s)` (`lenstra_congruence`) that drives the enumeration. The Euclidean chain (`lchain`/`lA`/`lB`/`lC`/`lT`, kernel-computable structural iteration, fuel-based step count — no WF recursion) alternates floor and ceiling-minus-one quotients; sign pattern (4.19) and alternating identity (4.18) proven from `q ≥ 1`; the window-walk finds the discrete sign change of `S_{2k}`. TWO MORE BOOK ERRATA (validated over 466k divisor instances): (i) the odd window must be CLOSED (`2aᵢbᵢ ≤ c`) — the book's strict `<` misses e.g. the divisor `5` of `50` at `(r,s) = (1,4)`, its printed algorithm returning only `{1, 25}`; (ii) the book's `c₁ = (nr* − ra₁)/s mod s` is wrong whenever `s ∤ r'r*` — the correct initialization is `c₁ = r*(n − rr')/s mod s` (book off by `r⌊r'r*/s⌋`). Also noted: the statement needs `s ∤ n` (else `a₁ = 0` and the chain cannot start). Worked instance: `(91, 2, 5)`, chain `a = (5,4,1,1,0)`, `b = (0,1,−1,4,−5)`, divisor `7` caught at `i = 2`. **The executable algorithm** (`lenstraDivisors n r s rs`, same file) enumerates window candidates `c ≡ cᵢ (mod s)` per index and solves (4.16) via the quadratic `aᵢu² − Ru + bᵢn = 0` in `u = xs + r` (discriminant necessarily `(2aᵢu − R)²`, located by an integer sqrt test; linear at `i = t`); every candidate is VERIFIED before reporting, so soundness is by construction and completeness comes from 4.2.12 + (4.20) + root-location exactness: `mem_lenstraDivisors : d ∈ lenstraDivisors n r s rs ↔ d ∣ n ∧ d % s = r`. Kernel-checked guards include `(50,1,4) → {1,5,25}` (finding the `5` the book's printed window loses) and `1001 = 7·11·13` at `(1,10)`. **The limb-level port** lives in `AzNat/LenstraDivisors` (see the algorithm table): the whole pipeline on `AzNat`/`AzInt`, with `map_toNat_lenstraDivisors` transporting it onto the reference list so the spec transfers verbatim (`AzNat.mem_lenstraDivisors`). `Definition_4_3_1.lean` opens §4.3: the book's coprimality definition for `Z_n[x]` is verbatim Mathlib's `IsCoprime`; its accompanying remark (“every ideal in `Z_n[x]` is principal iff `n` prime”) is FALSE — the FIFTH erratum of this chapter: `Z_6[x]` is a principal ideal ring (`(2,x) = (3x+4)`; CRT product of PIRs). The corrected statement is formalized: **`Z_n[x]` is a principal ideal ring ⟺ `n` is squarefree** (`polynomial_zmod_isPrincipalIdealRing_iff`). Forward via the general `isReduced_of_polynomial_isPrincipalIdealRing` — over ANY commutative ring, `R[X]` PIR forces `R` reduced, by a four-line coefficient computation on a putative generator of `(ε, x)` for square-zero `ε` — plus Mathlib's `isReduced_zmod`; backward by strong induction, CRT, and the splitting `(R × S)[X] ≅ R[X] × S[X]` + product-of-PIRs (both proved here). Examples: `Z_6[x]` PIR; `Z_4[x]`, `Z_12[x]` not. `Algorithm_4_3_2.lean`: **finding a principal generator** (`gcdOrFactor`) — the Euclidean algorithm in `Z_n[x]`, where the only obstruction is inverting a leading coefficient `c`, and any failure to invert exhibits the nontrivial factor `gcd(c, n)` of `n` (since `c ≠ 0` forces `gcd(c,n) ≤ c < n`); the Euclid step is division with remainder by a monic polynomial, which needs no invertibility at all. Specification split: `gcdOrFactor_factor` (a factor verdict is a nontrivial divisor), `gcdOrFactor_gcd` (a gcd verdict is monic and generates the pair ideal — the two Euclidean moves preserve `(f, g)`), `isCoprime_iff_of_gcdOrFactor` (coprime ⟺ the generator is a unit, tying back to Definition 4.3.1), `gcdOrFactor_ne_inl_of_prime` (over a prime modulus the algorithm never fails). WF recursion on `deg f`; proofs by the functional induction principle. **The computable rail** lives in `AzPolynomial/GcdOrFactor` (see the algorithm table). `Theorem_4_3_3.lean`: **Lenstra's divisor-confinement theorem** (`theorem_4_3_3`) — the engine of the finite field primality test: with `F ∣ n^I − 1` and `f, g ∈ Z_n[x]` (`f` monic of positive degree — a STATEMENT GAP implicit in the book: without it `f = g = 1` satisfies the hypotheses vacuously) such that (1) `f ∣ g^(n^I−1) − 1`, (2) `g^((n^I−1)/q) − 1` coprime to `f` for primes `q ∣ F`, (3) the `I` elementary symmetric polynomials in `g, g^n, …, g^(n^(I−1))` are constants mod `f`, every prime factor `p` of `n` is `≡ n^j (mod F)` for some `j < I`. Proof in `K = Z_p[x]/(f₁)`: (1)+(2) force `ord(ḡ)` to be a multiple of `F` via the Pocklington valuation climb — extracted as the reusable monoid lemma `dvd_orderOf_of_pow_eq_one` (third appearance, after 4.1.3/4.2.3); (3) + Vieta (`Multiset.prod_X_sub_C_coeff`) make `h(T) = ∏(T − ḡ^(n^j))` Frobenius-fixed, so `h(ḡ^p) = Frob(h(ḡ)) = 0` and the factored form pins `ḡ^p = ḡ^(n^j)`; orders determine exponents mod `F`. Worked instance: `n = 7`, `F = 6`, `f = x`, `g = 3`. `Algorithm_4_3_4.lean`: **the finite field primality test — witnesses and soundness**. Witnesses exist for prime `n` (the book's pre-algorithm discussion): condition (1) for irreducible `f` and `f ∤ g` is Lagrange in the order-`n^I` field `K = Z_n[x]/(f)` (`cond1_of_prime`); condition (3) holds for EVERY `g` (book asks `f ∤ g`; unnecessary) since Frobenius permutes `g, g^n, …, g^(n^(I−1))` cyclically, so the symmetric functions are fixed and land in the prime field (`cond3_of_prime`, via `mem_range_algebraMap_of_pow_card` + Vieta); the degree-`< I` representative of any cyclic generator of `K*` satisfies (1) and (2) for every prime `q ∣ n^I − 1` (`exists_lenstra_witness`; book slip: step 2 says `g` MONIC of degree `< I`, but its own analysis says nonzero — at `I = 1` the only monic choice is `g = 1`). Soundness of the "prime" verdict (`prime_of_lenstra_conditions`): `F ≥ √n` + verified (1)(2)(3) + the step-4 divisor search empty on `n^j mod F` (`1 ≤ j < I`) ⟹ `n` prime — a composite `n` has a prime factor `p ≤ √n ≤ F` confined by 4.3.3; class `j = 0` gives the absurd `F ∣ p − 1 < F`, `p = F` gives `F ∣ gcd(n^j, n^I−1) = 1`, else `p = n^j mod F` is a proper factor step 4 catches. Composite verdicts: `gcdOrFactor_factor` + contrapositives of `cond1_of_prime`/`cond3_of_prime`. Worked instance: `3` proven prime via `I = 1`, `F = 2`, `f = x`, `g = 2`. **Step 1's computable rail** — the optimistic irreducibility test over `Z_m[x]` for `m` of unknown primality — lives in `AzPolynomial/IrreducibleOrFactor` (see the algorithm table), built on the **Ben-Or criterion** added to GG land (`GG.irreducible_iff_isCoprime_X_pow_card_pow_sub_X`: monic `f` of positive degree is irreducible ⟺ `x^(q^i) − x` coprime to `f` for `1 ≤ i ≤ ⌊deg f/2⌋`; from GG Theorem 14.2's divisibility criterion).), and the **full certificate checker** `AzPolynomial.lenstraTest` (steps 1–4 for given witnesses) with both verdicts PROVEN: `some true` ⟹ prime (discharging `prime_of_lenstra_conditions` from the run's own checks), `some false` ⟹ composite. Guards: `3` certified prime through a genuine `GF(9)` certificate; `15` composite by Fermat; the pseudoprime `341` correctly inconclusive at base 2. **The complete probabilistic test** `AzPolynomial.finiteFieldTest` wraps the checker in the hybrid random-then-exhaustive witness search (both verdicts proven by pass-through); its guards certify `3` and `7` prime via RANDOM `GF(9)`/`GF(49)` certificates and catch `341` with a random witness. |
| `BrentZimmermann/Chapter2` | Formalization of Brent & Zimmermann, *Modern Computer Arithmetic*, §2.3 (the Fourier transform), toward the Schönhage–Strassen integer multiplication that tops the Toom–Cook ladder (`docs/fft_plan.md`). `DFT.lean` (§2.3.1): `IsPrincipalRoot ω K` (`ω^K = 1` and vanishing geometric sums, the right notion in rings with zero divisors such as `ℤ/(2^N+1)`), its inverse `ω^(K−1)` (`IsPrincipalRoot.inv`), the transform `dft ω a` on `Fin K → R`, `dft_dft` (twice forward = `K` times the index-negated vector) and `dftInv_dft` (the backward transform inverts up to the factor `K`). `FFT.lean` (§2.3.2): the recursive decimation-in-frequency FFT `fftRec`/`fft` for `K = 2^k` (sums `a_j + a_{j+m}`, twiddled differences `ω^j (a_j + ω^m a_{j+m})`, two half-size transforms at `ω²`), with `fft_eq_dft` under the sole hypothesis `ω^K = 1`; the butterfly form `fftRecSub` (`ω^j (a_j − a_{j+m})`, one twiddle multiplication per pair) with `fftRecSub_eq_dftNat` under `ω^{K/2} = −1`, and `IsPrincipalRoot.pow_half_eq_neg_one` deriving that sign flip from principal-ness when `K/2` is a unit; MCA Algorithm 2.2 `forwardFFT` (decimation in time, bit-reversed output) with Theorem 2.1 `forwardFFT_eq_dftNat` / `forwardFFT_eq_dft`, and `bitrev` with `bitrev_lt`, `bitrev_two_mul`, `bitrev_two_mul_add_one`, `bitrev_bitrev`; Algorithm 2.3 `backwardFFT` (bit-reversed input, normal-order output, twiddles `ω^{K−j}`) with Theorem 2.2 `backwardFFT_eq_dftNat` and the round trip `backwardFFT_forwardFFT` (forward then backward is `K · x`, no explicit bit reversal). `FFTMulMod.lean` (§2.3.3, the mathematics of Algorithm 2.4): `IsPrincipalRoot.of_pow_two_pow_eq_neg_one` (`ω^{K/2} = −1` makes `ω` principal, `K` a power of two), the Convolution Theorem `dft_cyclicConv`, the weighted negacyclic convolution `cyclicConv_weight` (`θ^K = −1`), the ring-level heart of Theorem 2.3 `dftInv_dft_weight_mul` / `backwardFFT_forwardFFT_weight_mul` (`= K θ^i c_i`), equation (2.2) as `sum_weight_mul_sum_weight` with `two_pow_eq_neg_one_zmod`, the coefficient bounds `negacyclicConv_bounds` and the residue recovery `int_recover_of_mem_window`. |
