(4.70) to first order, `abs_ellipticK_sqrt_sub_log_le` (`|K(k') − ln(4/k)| ≤ (k²/4)(2 − ln k)`). Deferred-proof sweep (2026-10-17): `RectangularSplitting.lean` gains the transposition identity `classical_split_eq_modular_transpose`; `StirlingRefinement.lean`: `factorial_le_stirling_refined` (`n! ≤ √(2πn)(n/e)^n e^{1/(12n)}`, from Mathlib's exact series for `log s_n − log s_{n+1}`); `AsymptoticExamples.lean` gains `harmonic_sub_log_sub_gamma_ge` (`H_N − ln N − γ ≥ 1/(8(N+1))`, so (4.31) is `Θ(1/N)`); `ErrorFunctionSeries.lean`: `erf_eq_integral` (`erf x = (2/√π)∫_0^x e^{−u²}`), `erf_neg`, (4.22) `hasSum_erf_series`, (4.23) `hasSum_erf_exp_series`/`erf_eq_exp_mul_erfExpSum` (`erf x = (2/√π)e^{−x²}∑ 2^j x^{2j+1}/(2j+1)‼`, via the ODE `S' = 1 + 2xS` of the series); `LegendreRelation.lean`: derivatives of `K`, `E` in the modulus (`hasDerivAt_ellipticK/E`, differentiation under the integral), `k E' = E − K`, `k k'² K' = E − k'² K`, and the **Legendre relation** (4.72) `legendre_relation` (`E K' + E' K − K K' = π/2`, `0 < k < 1`) with `legendre_relation_sqrt_half` (`2EK − K² = π/2` at `k = 1/√2`). `SecondAGMLog.lean` (§4.8.4): every `q ∈ (0,1)` is a nome (`nome_log`), Sasaki–Kanada for all `0 < q < 1` (`sasaki_kanada'`), the second AGM logarithm `log_eq_pi_div_four_div_AGM` (`ln x = (π/4)/AGM(θ₂²(q⁴), θ₃²(q⁴))`, `q = 1/x`), `theta2_pow_four` (`θ₂(q⁴) = q·θ₂Int(q⁴)`), generic sparse-series tails (`tsum_pow_le_of_le`, `theta3_trunc`, `theta2Int_trunc`) and the §4.8.4 truncations `theta3_pow_four_trunc` (`1 + 2q⁴ + 2q¹⁶ + O(q³⁶)`), `theta2_pow_four_trunc` (`2q + 2q⁹ + 2q²⁵ + O(q⁴⁹)`) with explicit tails, the precision condition `x ≥ 2^{n/36}` ⇒ tails `≤ 2·2^{−n}/(1 − q⁴)` (`theta3/theta2_pow_four_error_le`), and the AGM start `AGM_sq_sq` (`AGM(a², b²) = AGM(a² + b², 2ab)/2`) with Ex. 4.45's identities. `ComplexAGM.lean` (§4.8.5): `arctan_eq_im_log` (`arctan x = ℑ ln(1 + ix)`), `arcsin_eq_arctan'`, `arccos_eq_arctan`, Euler `exp_mul_I_eq`, the Karatsuba identities `complex_mul_three_products`/`complex_sq_two_products`, and the FFT transform-count model `fftCost` with the first four rows of Table 4.1 (`2/3`, `1`, `2`, `4/3`). `BinarySplitting.lean` (§4.9 intro): the arctan series `arctanTerm`/`hasSum_arctanTerm` (Mathlib `Real.hasSum_arctan`) with the tail bound `abs_arctan_sub_sum_le` and `abs_arctan_sub_sum_le_two_pow` (`|x| ≤ 1/2`, terms `j ≤ n/2` ⇒ error `≤ 2^{−n}`); the exact rational `arctanNum`/`arctanDen` (`sum_arctanTerm_div_eq`, `Q = q^{2N+1}∏(2j+1)`) with `log_arctanDen_le` (`O(n log n)`) and `log_factorial_mul_pow_le` (exp: `O(n)`); the divide-and-conquer identities `div_add_div_eq`, `sum_div_eq_sum_mul_prod_erase_div`; the cost (4.80) in the quasi-linear model `quasiLinearCost m = m lg m`: `two_pow_mul_quasiLinearCost_div_le`, `binary_splitting_cost_le`, `clog_le_logb_add_one`, and `binary_splitting_cost` (`∑_{k≤⌈lg n⌉} 2^k M(n lg n/2^k) ≤ 4 (lg n)² M(n)`, i.e. `α = 2`). `ArctanBinarySplitting.lean` (§4.9, repeated application): `arctan_sub_arctan`, the reduction `arctan_eq_arctan_rat_add` (`arctan x = arctan(p/q) + arctan((qx − p)/(q + px))`) with `delta_nonneg_le`, `delta_lt_inv` (`0 ≤ δ ≤ x − p/q`, `δ < 1/q`); the iteration `bitBurstQ i = 2^{2^{i+1}}`, `bitBurstRem`, `bitBurstNum` with invariants `bitBurstRem_bounds` (`0 ≤ r_i < 1/Q_i`), `bitBurstNum_lt` (`p_i < Q_i = √Q_{i+1}`), `bitBurstNum_div_lt`; `arctan_eq_sum_bitBurst`, `arctan_le_self`, `arctan_bitBurstRem_lt`, `abs_arctan_sub_sum_bitBurst_le`/`_clog_le` (`⌈lg n⌉` rationals give `2^{−n}`), and the `α+1` cost `repeated_binary_splitting_cost` (`≤ 8 (lg n)³ M(n)`). `SinCosBinarySplitting.lean` (§4.9.1, Algorithm SinCos as theorems): the block digits `sinCosDigit` (`0 ≤ p_i < 2^{2^i}`, `sum_sinCosDigit_div` = the `2^{k+1}`-bit truncation, error `< 2^{−2^{k+1}} ≤ 2^{−n}`), `tailSum` (`x_j = y_j + x_{j+1}`), step 6 as the complex product `sinCosProd c s k j = ∏_{i≥j}(c_i + i s_i)` (`sinCosProd_re/_im` are the two formulae, `sinCosProd_exact : = exp(i x_j)`, so `S_j = sin x_j`, `C_j = cos x_j`), the perturbation lemma `norm_prod_sub_prod_le` and the error theorem `sinCos_error` (`|S₀ − sin x| ≤ 2(k+1)ε(1+2ε)^{k+1}`), `pow_lt_two_pow_of_digit`. `Holonomic.lean` (§4.9.2 intro): `IsHolonomic` (polynomial-coefficient ODE), `IsPRecursive` (polynomial-coefficient recurrence), examples `isHolonomic_exp/sin/cos/log`, `RationalRatio.isPRecursive` (hypergeometric ⇒ P-recursive); the algebraic theory in a differential field extension `B/A` (Mathlib `Differential`/`DifferentialAlgebra`): `iterDeriv`, `derivSpan`, `IsDFinite` (derivatives span a f.g. `A`-module), closure `IsDFinite.add`/`IsDFinite.mul`, and the equivalence with a linear ODE over `A` (`IsDFinite.exists_relation`, `isDFinite_of_relation`). `BitBurst.lean` (Theorem 4.2's skeleton): bit blocks `blockNum`/`bitBlock` (bits `a+1..b`, `< 2^{b−a}`, `< 2^{−a}`), Theorem 4.2's `burstBlock x i` (numerator `< 2^{2^i}`, value `< 2^{1−2^i}`, `sum_burstBlock` = the `2^k−1`-bit truncation, exact for `n`-bit `x`), the telescoping `sum_shift_sub`/`eq_sum_shift_sub` (`f(x) = ∑ f_i^*(r_{i+1})`), `iteratedDeriv_comp_const_add`, `IsHolonomic.comp_const_add` (translation), `isHolonomic_of_inhomogeneous` (`b′E − bF`), `isHolonomic_arctan`, the translated arctan coefficient recurrence for formal power series `arctan_shift_coeff_recurrence`, `arctan_v_recurrence`, `arctan_s_recurrence`, the 3×3 `arctanStepMatrix` with `arctan_step_matrix_eq`, and (4.81) `matProd`/`vec_eq_matProd_mulVec`. `ContourIntegration.lean` (§4.10): (4.83) `hasSum_cauchy_coeff` (Cauchy's coefficient formula on the unit disc, from Mathlib's `cauchyPowerSeries`), the discrete sums `aliasSum` (4.84) with roots-of-unity orthogonality `sum_zpow_primitiveRoot`, the aliasing identity (4.85) `aliasSum_eq_tsum`/`aliasSum_sub_eq` (`S_{j,k} − a_j = ∑_{m≥1} a_{j+mk}`), the geometric error `norm_aliasSum_sub_le`, and the Bernoulli instance (4.86)/(4.87): `bernoulliCoeff` (`= B_l/l!`, `l ≠ 1`), `norm_bernoulliCoeff_le` (`≤ (π²/3)(2π)^{−l}`), `bernoulli_aliasSum_sub_eq`, and the relative error `norm_bernoulli_aliasSum_sub_le` (`O((2π)^{−k})`). `Exercises.lean` (MCA Ch. 4 exercises, formal parts; prose answers in the ledger's exercises section): Ex. 4.1 — the formal logarithm `logSeries A = ∫ A′A⁻¹` (`derivative_logSeries`, `logSeries_unique`, `logSeries_one`, `logSeries_exp`, `logSeries_mul`), the truncation locality `X_pow_dvd_logSeries_sub` (`A ≡ B mod X^{n+1}` ⇒ `ln A ≡ ln B`), `integral_add/sub/zero`, `X_pow_dvd_integral`, and the Newton doubling cost `sum_cost_le` (`M(2m) ≥ 2M(m)` ⇒ `∑_{k<L} M(2^k) ≤ M(2^L)`); Ex. 4.2 — the odd–even reciprocal: `coeff_odd_mul_rescale_neg_one` (`s(x)s(−x)` is even), `oddEvenT`, `mul_rescale_neg_one_eq_expand` (`s(x)s(−x) = t(x²)`), `inv_eq_rescale_mul_expand_inv` (`1/s = s(−x)(1/t)(x²)`), `X_pow_dvd_inv_sub_oddEven` (precision doubling), `cost_recurrence_le` (`T(n) ≤ T(n/2) + cM(n)` ⇒ `T ≤ T(1) + 2cM(n)`); Ex. 4.3 — the order-`k` Taylor iteration `taylorInverseStep` with the Lagrange remainder `taylorInverseStep_sub`, the bounds `abs_sub_taylorInverseStep_le`, `taylorInverseStep_order` (`|x − x_{j+1}| ≤ (ML^k/k!)|x − x_j|^k`), and the generic convergence `iterate_le_of_order`; Ex. 4.4 — the forward Horner scheme `forwardHorner` (`T_{j+1} = T_j y + a_{j+1}`, `y = 1/x`, `∑_{i≤j} a_i x^i = x^j T_j`), the tail bounds `abs_tsum_sub_sum_le_two_mul`/`abs_tsum_sub_sum_le_term` (tail ≤ last term for decreasing `|a_i|`, `|x| < 1/2`), and the stopping criterion `stop_criterion_iff`, `abs_tsum_sub_forwardHorner_le`; Ex. 4.5 — `exp_series_terms_of_small` (`|r| ≤ 2^{−m}`, `md ≥ n+2` ⇒ `2^{−n}`), the doubling/series cost `doubling_cost_ge` (`n/m + m ≥ 2√n`) and `doubling_cost_sqrt` (optimum `m = k − j = √n`); Ex. 4.6 — `exp` from `ln` by the order-`k` iteration `expViaLogStep k x z = z ∑_{m<k}(x − ln z)^m/m!` (`= taylorInverseStep exp log`), `abs_log_sub_log_le`, the relative error `expViaLogStep_relative_error` (`ρ' ≤ (3·2^{k−1}/k!) ρ^k`), and the cost sum `geometric_cost_le` (`∑ T(n/k^i) ≤ T(n)·k/(k−1)`); | `CohenLenstra/` | Formalization of H. Cohen & H. W. Lenstra, Jr., *Primality Testing and Jacobi Sums* (Math. Comp. 42 (1984)) — the APR-CL paper proper, successor to the `CrandallPomerance/Chapter4` arc (namespace `Azurite.CL`). `Proposition_4_1.lean` (paper §4, selection of auxiliary numbers): **the function `e(t)`** (computable; `e(t) = 2` for odd `t`, `2·∏_{q prime, q−1∣t} q^(v_q(t)+1)` for even `t`; exact prime factorization proven: `v_p(e t) = v_p(t)+1` iff `p−1 ∣ t` for odd `p`, `v_2(e t) = v_2(t)+2`; Table 1 guards `e(2)=24`, `e(12)=65520`, `e(60)=6814407600`) and **Proposition (4.1)**: the paper's condition (2.3) — `a^t ≡ 1 (mod s)` for all `a` coprime to `s` — holds **iff `s ∣ e(t)`** (`proposition_4_1` on the unit group + coprime-naturals corollary). Proof as in the paper: odd `t` via `a = −1`; even `t` by CRT-splitting the unit group (`unitsCRT`, `Nat.recOnPosPrimePosCoprime`) down to prime powers, with Mathlib's cyclicity of `(ZMod (p^k))ˣ` for odd `p` and, at `p = 2`, the element `5` of order `2^(k−2)` against an elementary two-adic bound `a^(2^(k−2)) ≡ 1 (mod 2^k)` for odd `a`. Also the paper's §4 growth bound `e_le`: `e(t) ≤ 2t·∏_{d∣t}(d+1)`, plus a wholesale Table 2 guard (`e(5040)` = its full 27-prime factorization). `Characters.lean` (paper §6): **(6.1) characters mod `q` separate points of `(ℤ/q)ˣ`** (`eq_6_1`, via Mathlib's `MulChar` duality; `card_mulChar_eq`: the character group has order `q−1`) and **(6.2) the set `Y_q`** — for each prime `p ∣ q−1` a character `χ_{p,q}` of order exactly `p^(v_p(q−1))` exists (`exists_mulChar_orderOf_eq`) and any set containing one for each `p` generates the full character group (`closure_eq_top_of_forall_orderOf`), by the general finite-group fact `closure_eq_top_of_forall_exists_orderOf` (a subset with an element of order `p^(v_p(|G|))` for each prime `p` generates `G`, by Lagrange). `Theorem_6_3.lean`: **Theorem (6.3), the master reduction** — if `t, s` satisfy (2.3), `gcd(n, st) = 1`, and for every prime `r ∣ n` and prime `p ∣ t` there is an exponent `l` with `χ_{p,q}(r) = χ_{p,q}(n)^l` (all `q ∣ s` with `p ∣ q−1`) and `r^(p−1) ≡ (n^(p−1))^l mod p^(v_p(s))` (the paper's p-adic (6.4)/(6.5) at finite level), then **(2.5)**: every divisor `r ∣ n` is `≡ n^i (mod s)` with `i < t` (`theorem_6_3`; CRT exponent + closure induction over `X_q` + separation + prime-power lift via the unit `r·n^(−l)`). Reusable: `pow_eq_pow_of_modEq` (monoid exponent-killing), `pow_pow_modEq_one` (`a ≡ 1 mod p ⟹ a^(p^k) ≡ 1 mod p^(k+1)`), `units_pow_prime_pow_eq_one`, `sub_one_dvd_of_dvd_e`/`dvd_of_two_le_factorization_e` (primes in `e(t)`), `modEq_prod_of_pairwise_coprime`. `Lemma_7_3.lean` (paper §7): **Lemma (7.3), the Gauss-sum Frobenius congruence** — for prime `n` with `q ∤ n` and any characters `χ`/`ψ` of `ℤ/q` into a commutative ring, `χ(n)^n·τ(χ,ψ)^n ≡ τ(χ^n,ψ) (mod nR)` (`lemma_7_3`; the paper's `τ(χ)^(n−σ_n) ≡ χ(n)^(−n) mod nB` multiplied free of units, with the Galois twist eliminated via `σ_n(τ(χ)) = τ(χ^n)` — no Galois action or localization needed; quotient-ring Frobenius + `gaussSum_mulShift`). `Corollary_7_5.lean`: **Corollary (7.5)** — (7.3) raised to a group-ring exponent `β = Σ ν(x)·σ_x`: `∏_x (χ^x(n)^n·τ(χ^x,ψ)^n)^ν(x) ≡ ∏_x τ((χ^x)^n,ψ)^ν(x) (mod nR)` (`corollary_7_5`; σ_x-twists = instances at `χ^x`, β-powers = products with multiplicities) + ideal form `corollary_7_5_mem` (any ideal containing `n`) + reusable `dvd_prod_sub_prod` (congruences multiply). `Lemma_7_17.lean`: **roots of unity separated by `𝔯 = rB + 𝔫`** (`lemma_7_17`: `ζ ≡ 1 mod I + (r)` forces `ζ = 1`, via `∏(1−ζ') = p^k` and `I ∩ ℤ ⊆ nℤ`). `Theorem_7_8.lean`: **Theorem (7.8), the converse** — a verified congruence `u^n ≡ ζ^(e₀)·σ(u) (mod I)` (the paper's (7.9), unit-free, with `σ_n` an abstract endomorphism the models provide) plus the finite (6.4)-data forces `χ(r) = ζ^(f·m)` = the paper's `χ(r) = η^(l_p(r))` (`theorem_7_8`; both telescopings (7.11)/(7.13), the period (7.12), unit-cancellation mod `I + (r)` via the Gauss-sum product formula, root separation via (7.17), and ℤ-divisibility exponent arithmetic). This is the theorem that turns one Gauss-sum test per pair `(p,q)` into the character condition feeding Theorem (6.3). `Proposition_7_18.lean`: **the finite (5.1) engine** — lifting-the-exponent for odd `p` (`dvd_pow_p_sub_one_step`/`dvd_pow_p_pow_sub_one`: `v_p(A^(p^j)−1) = c+j` exactly) and `exists_pow_modEq_of_exact` (if `v_p(a−1) = c ≥ 1` exactly and `p^c ∣ b−1`, then `b` is a power of `a` mod `p^D` for every `D`, by digit-lifting); **Proposition (7.18)**: `p ≥ 3` and `n^(p−1) ≢ 1 mod p²` imply (6.4) at every depth (`proposition_7_18`, the engine at `c = 1` + Fermat). **Theorem (7.19)** (in `Theorem_7_8.lean`): if (7.9) passes with a *primitive* `p^k`-th root (`p ∤ e₀`), then (6.4) holds (`theorem_7_19`; order analysis of `u = τ(χ)^β` mod `𝔯` giving the paper's (7.22), then LTE + the engine — no (5.2)/p-th-root step needed). `Lemma_7_23.lean` (the `p = 2` methods; engine's 2-adic branch `exists_pow_modEq_of_exact_two` with exact `c ≥ 2` in `Proposition_7_18.lean`): **Lemma (7.23)** — `a^((n−1)/2) ≡ −1 mod n` forces `v_2(r−1) ≥ v_2(n−1)` for every prime `r ∣ n`, equality iff Legendre `(a/r) = −1` (`lemma_7_23`; order analysis + Euler's criterion); **Proposition (7.24)** — `n ≡ 1 mod 4` + such a witness give (6.4) at `p = 2` (`proposition_7_24`); **Proposition (7.25)** — `n ≡ 3 mod 8` + `2^((n−1)/2) ≡ −1 mod n` give (6.4) at `p = 2` (`proposition_7_25`; supplementary law forces `r ≡ 1` or `n mod 8`, engine with base `n²`, unit-division by `n` in the second case). `Equation_8_2.lean` (paper §8 opening): **(8.2)/(8.3) unit-free** — `τ(χ^(a+b),ψ)·j(χ^a,χ^b) = τ(χ^a,ψ)·τ(χ^b,ψ)` for `a+b ≢ 0 mod p^k` (`eq_8_2`, via Mathlib's `jacobiSum_mul_nontrivial`) + **`jacobiSum_pow_mem`** (Jacobi sums of the χ-power family lie in `ℤ[μ]` for a primitive `p^k`-th root `μ` — the fact that makes the Jacobi-sum reformulation computable without `ζ_q`). `Theorem_8_5.lean`: **Theorem (8.5), the Jacobi-sum test** — defs `Mset`/`minv`/`mAct`/`αc`/`βc` ((8.4)/(8.11); nonneg coefficients per Remark (8.9a)); the (8.12)-identity coefficient-wise (`exponent_identity`, floor arithmetic) and at product level (`tau_product_identity` via `Pprod_reindex`); the chain `j^α = τ^((n−σ_n)β)` as the unit-free `j^α·σ(u_β) = u_β^n` (`jacobi_tau_identity`); and **`theorem_8_5`**: the congruence (8.8) `∏ j(χ^(a·x*),χ^(b·x*))^(αc x) ≡ ζ' mod I` implies hypothesis (7.9) of Theorems (7.8)/(7.19) for the family `(M, βc∘minv)` with the same `ζ'` — the whole §7 converse machinery driven by a test inside `ℤ[ζ_{p^k}]`. **Lemma (8.12) fully proven** (`lemma_8_12`): the (7.6)-condition for β from (8.6), via the ring hom `σ_x ↦ x^p mod p^(k+1)` (`pow_p_modEq_lift`), the division-algorithm identity (8.14) (`eq_8_14_coeff`), the sum-reindexing (`sum_mAct_pow_modEq`), and the (8.16) valuation `T ≡ (p−1)p^(k−1) mod p^k` (`sum_pow_M_modEq`, generator-enumeration + block-split + 1-units sum) — so the whole soundness pipeline (8.8) ⟹ (8.5) ⟹ (7.8)/(7.19) ⟹ (6.3) ⟹ (2.5) is sorry-free. `Theorem_9_1.lean` (paper §9, `p = 2` tests, `β = 1`): **(9.1)** `k = 1`: `q^((n−1)/2) ≡ ζ' mod I` ⟹ (7.9) with root `χ(−1)^((n−1)/2)·ζ'` (`theorem_9_1`, via `gaussSum_sq`); **(9.3)** `k = 2, n ≡ 1 mod 4`: `j(χ,χ)^((n−1)/2)·q^((n−1)/4) ≡ ζ'` ⟹ (7.9) same `ζ'` (`theorem_9_3`, via (8.2) + `τ(χ²)² = q`); **(9.5)** `k = 2, n ≡ 3 mod 4`: `j(χ,χ)^((n+1)/2)·q^((n−3)/4) ≡ ζ'` ⟹ (7.9) with `χ(−1)·ζ'` (`theorem_9_5`, + same-ψ (7.2) `tau_mul_tau_inv` and one domain-cancellation of `q`). Reusable: `isQuadratic_of_orderOf`, `chi_neg_one_sq`. `Theorem_9_10.lean` (paper §9, `k ≥ 3`): **Theorem (9.10), the triple-Jacobi-sum test** — index set `M2set` = {x : 1 ≤ x < 2^k, x ≡ 1 or 3 mod 8} ((9.9); a subgroup of `(ℤ/2^k)ˣ` mod `2^k`, closure needing `8 ∣ 2^k`: `mAct2_mem`/`minv2_residue`/`minv2_mem`); the per-exponent (9.8) `τ(χ^(3m))·(j(χ^m,χ^m)·j(χ^m,χ^(2m))) = τ(χ^m)³` from two applications of (8.2) (`eq_9_8`; (9.7)'s triple Jacobi sum inlined as the product of two Jacobi sums); the (9.13) identity `(n−σ_n)β = (3−σ₃)α` coefficient-wise (`eq_9_13_coeff`, just two Euclidean decompositions — no guards) and at product level (`tau_product_identity_M2`), assembled into the unit-free `j^α·σ(u_β) = u_β^n` (`jacobi_tau_identity_M2`); and **`theorem_9_10`**: the congruence (9.11) `∏ (j(χ^(x*),χ^(x*))·j(χ^(x*),χ^(2x*)))^(αc x) ≡ ζ' mod I` implies (7.9) for the family `(M2set, [3·minv(·)/2^k])` with the same `ζ'`. **The (7.6)-condition (9.14) fully proven** (`eq_9_14`): summing Euclidean decompositions over `M` and reindexing along the action of 3 (the paper's `σ_x ↦ 1` hom, (9.15)–(9.17)) gives `Σ_{x∈M} [3x/2^k] = 2^(k−2) − 1` (`eq_9_18`, via the block-of-eight enumeration `Σ_{x∈M} x = 2^(k−1)(2^(k−2)−1)`, `sum_M2`), which is odd — so the whole `p = 2, k ≥ 3` soundness pipeline (9.11) ⟹ (7.8) ⟹ (6.3) is sorry-free. `Theorem_9_19.lean` (paper §9, `k ≥ 3`, `n ≡ 5, 7 mod 8`): **Theorem (9.19), the companion test** — (9.20) `∏(j·j')^{αc}·j(φ,φ³)² ≡ ζ' mod I` (φ = χ^{2^{k−3}}, order 8 at every level) implies (7.9) for the same `(M2set, [3·minv/2^k])` family with root `χ(−1)·ζ'` (`theorem_9_19`), via the unit-free (9.21) (`jacobi_tau_identity_M2_neg`). `lambdaHom_sum` + `sub_mem_mKernel_iff` (the (i2a) comparison in ℤ/n IS the (10.3) congruence). `Impl_7.lean` (§7 test vectors): the 247-digit factor of 2^892+1 (`prime247_2_892`) and the 180-digit Table-4 prime, with every checkable §7 claim guarded against our definitions — divisibility, l∓, the eight flags, the q-prime set, and the "just sufficient" s₁s₂ > √n via `s1`/`s2`/`bigEnough` (failing without the largest q-prime). **The 1987 paper is now fully processed (§§1–7).** Implementation plan: `docs/aprcl_implementation_plan.md`. **Phase A1 DONE** — `AzPolyMod/Cyclotomic.lean` + `Equiv/Cyclotomic.lean`: the computable cyclotomic ring `CycT n p k = AzPolyMod (cyclotomicPrimePow (AzZMod n) p k)` with `sigmaT`/`sigmaInvT` ((6.1)), `findHT` ((6.2), corrected), `lambdaT` (Horner λ), all proven against the abstract model through **`reduceCycT : CycM (p^k) →+* CycT n p k`** (`reduceCycT_eq_iff`: congruence mod n in ℤ[ζ] IS equality in `CycT`). **Phase A4 DONE** — `CohenLenstra/Tables.lean`: q-primes, certified generators and index tables, and the Jacobi-sum vectors `jacobiSumT`, proven equal to the reduction of the abstract Jacobi sums (`jacobiSumT_eq_reduce`). **Phase A3 DONE** — `AzNat/JacobiSym.lean` + `Equiv/JacobiSym.lean`: the binary Jacobi symbol on `AzNat`, proven equal to Mathlib's `jacobiSym`. **Phase A2 DONE** — `AzZMod/Quad.lean` + `Equiv/Quad.lean`: the quadratic ring `QuadT n u a` with norm-one shortcut squaring, (4.10) candidates and `tryInv`, proven through the injective `toQuad`; the (4.3)/(4.4)/(4.10) verdict lemmas now take `u a : ZMod n` with a nonsquare-discriminant hypothesis (`not_isSquare_disc` recovers the Jacobi-symbol form). Further computable implementation until the paper is finished, per plan. |
| `APRCL/` | The certificate-and-checker implementation of the APR-CL test (namespace `Azurite.APRCL`): stage `Outcome`, the Lucas–Lehmer certificate `LLCert`/`llCheck`, the per-`(p,q)` Jacobi tests `jTest` in `CycT`, the full certificate `Cert` with checker `aprclCheck` and generator `generate`, `aprclTest : AzNat → Option Bool`; `Equiv/` proves both verdicts (`llCheck_true/false`, `aprclCheck_true/false`) via the common-ring chains (`JacobiChain`), the ℂ-alignment (`JacobiAlign`) and `theorem_6_3_LL`. |
| `GathenGerhard/Chapter14` | Formalization of von zur Gathen & Gerhard, *Modern Computer Algebra* (3rd ed.), Chapter 14, toward polynomial factorization over finite fields — the consumer of the exhaustive polynomial generators (deterministic candidate streams in place of randomization, termination by theorem). `Theorem_14_2.lean`: **GG Theorem 14.2** — for `d ≥ 1`, `X^(q^d) − X ∈ F[X]` is the product of all monic irreducible polynomials of degree dividing `d` (`X_pow_card_pow_sub_X_eq_prod` over the duplicate-free factor set, membership characterized by `mem_normalizedFactors_X_pow_card_pow_sub_X_iff`), via the divisibility criterion `irreducible_dvd_X_pow_card_pow_sub_X_iff` (`f ∣ X^(q^d) − X ↔ deg f ∣ d`, hypothesis-free — both sides trivial at `d = 0`). The forward direction was already in Mathlib (`Irreducible.natDegree_dvd_of_dvd_X_pow_card_pow_sub_X`); the REVERSE is ours: Fermat in `AdjoinRoot f` (a field with `q^n` elements) gives `a^(q^d) = a`, and `minpoly.dvd` + `AdjoinRoot.minpoly_root` absorb the book's gcd-descent (GG Example 6.19). Squarefreeness via separability (`galois_poly_separable`), replacing the book's double-root argument. `DistinctDegree.lean`: the **distinct-degree decomposition** `(g_1, …, g_s)` of nonconstant `f` — `ddFactor f i` = product of the degree-`i` slice of `f`'s prime-factor SET (`UniqueFactorizationMonoid.primeFactors`, each irreducible once regardless of multiplicity), `ddLength` = largest factor degree, `distinctDegreeDecomposition` the sequence. Proven: each part monic (`ddFactor_monic`) and dividing `f` (`ddFactor_dvd`, slice ⊆ factor set whose product is the radical), degree-`0` part `= 1` (`ddFactor_zero`), last part `≠ 1` (`ddFactor_ddLength_ne_one`), and for monic squarefree `f` the parts multiply back to `f` (`prod_distinctDegreeDecomposition`, fiberwise partition of the factor set by degree). The book's `F_3` example — `x(x+1)(x²+1)(x²+x+2)` decomposes as `(x²+x, x⁴+x³+x+2)` — is guard-verified on `AzPolynomial (AzZMod 3)`, with the quadratics' irreducibility checked by evaluating over the exhaustive enumeration of `F_3`. `Algorithm_14_3.lean`: **GG Algorithm 14.3 (distinct-degree factorization) + Theorem 14.4 (correctness)**, both rails, both with the book's **early abort** (once `deg f_i < 2(i+1)`, `f_i` is irreducible — `ddTail_early_abort`: the degree-`> i` factor set is a singleton — so the loop stops and emits `(1, …, 1, f_i)`, capping iterations at `max(m₁/2, m₂) ≤ (deg f)/2`). Abstract `distinctDegreeFactorization` (fuel-recursive `dddLoop`, fuel `deg f`); the computable rail lives in `AzPolynomial/DistinctDegreeFactorization.lean` (see the algorithm table). Both rails power `h_i` **mod the current `f_(i-1)`, not mod `f`** (the book's other remark; new reusable modular exponentiation `AzPolynomial.powModByMonic` in `AzPolyMod f_i`) — the invariant is the congruence `f_i ∣ h − x^(q^i)` (`dvd_powModByMonic_sub_pow`), descending along `f_(i+1) ∣ f_i`. Correctness `theorem_14_4` (= `distinctDegreeDecomposition`, one fuel induction over the invariants: `ddTail f i` closed-form loop variable, peeling `ddTail_succ`, termination `ddTail_eq_one_iff`, crux `gcd_X_pow_card_pow_sub_X_ddTail` = `gcd(x^(q^(i+1))−x, f_i) = g_(i+1)` via Theorem 14.2) transported to the Az rail by `map_toPoly_distinctDegreeFactorization` (representative-level `AzPolyMod` pow bridge `toPoly_val_pow` via the `AdjoinRoot` isomorphism), and instantiated at concrete prime moduli by `map_toPoly_distinctDegreeFactorization_azZMod` (over `AzZMod p`, `q = p` itself — via the new `Fintype (AzZMod m)` instance with `card_eq : card = m.toNat` in `AzZMod/Fintype.lean`). Guards run the book's `F_3` example plus abort-exercising cases (gapped `(x, 1, x³+2x+1)`, quartic tail skipping two rounds). `Lemma_14_6.lean`: **GG Lemma 14.6** — for `k ∣ q−1`, the k-th powers `kthPowers F k := (powMonoidHom k).range ≤ Fˣ` have order `(q−1)/k` (`card_kthPowers`) and equal `{a : a^((q−1)/k) = 1}` = `rootsOfUnity ((q−1)/k) F` (`mem_kthPowers_iff_pow_eq_one`); byproduct `card_rootsOfUnity_eq` (exactly `k` k-th roots of unity); book-faithful proof (roots bound `card_rootsOfUnity` + Fermat + homomorphism theorem `index_ker`/`index_mul_card` sandwich); field-level power-residue test `exists_pow_eq_iff_pow_card_sub_one_div_eq_one` — the counting fact behind equal-degree splitting (Cantor–Zassenhaus). `Lemma_14_7.lean`: **GG Lemma 14.7** (odd `q`, `k = 2` specialization) — the squares `kthPowers F 2` (= Mathlib `IsSquare`, `mem_kthPowers_two_iff_isSquare`) form a subgroup of order `(q−1)/2` (`lemma_14_7_card`), are exactly `{a : a^((q−1)/2) = 1}` (`lemma_14_7_mem`), and every unit lands in `{1, −1}` under `^((q−1)/2)` (`lemma_14_7_pow_eq_one_or_neg_one`); field-level Euler's criterion `isSquare_iff_pow_card_sub_one_div_two_eq_one` + dichotomy `pow_card_sub_one_div_two_eq_one_or_neg_one` — the even-split fact behind Cantor–Zassenhaus. `Algorithm_14_8.lean`: the **output contract of equal-degree splitting** (`equalDegreeSplittingStep_correct`/`equalDegreeSplitting_correct`) — any `some g` is a proper monic factor of `f` (monic, `∣ f`, `≠ 1`, `≠ f`), needing only `f` monic + `deg a < deg f` (equal-degree hypotheses govern the success probability, Thm 14.9, not the validity of a success); computable algorithm + hybrid candidate stream in `AzPolynomial/EqualDegreeSplitting.lean` (see the algorithm table). `Algorithm_14_13.lean`: spec-level lemmas for the complete factorization — generalized crux `gcd_X_pow_card_pow_sub_X_eq_prod_filter` (the 14.3 crux WITHOUT squarefreeness of the target — the left argument `x^(q^i)−x` supplies the gcd's squarefreeness, so `v` may carry multiplicities), subset-product toolkit, leaf criterion `irreducible_of_natDegree_eq_of_factors`. The algorithm bridges live with the other correctness proofs in `AzPolynomial/Equiv/Factorization.lean`: **`factorization_correct`/`canonicalFactorization_correct`** — for monic `f`, `q = card K`, the output pairs are (monic irreducible, multiplicity ≠ 0) with strictly increasing factors and `∏ uᵢ^eᵢ = f`; for arbitrary `f` the leading coefficient comes out front (`f = C(lc)·∏uᵢ^eᵢ`, the associate representatives being the monic polynomials); no oddness of `q` needed. Stack: sweep completeness `exists_sweepCandidate_toPoly` + `equalDegreeSplittingStep_isSome_of_factor` ⟹ `edsSearch_isSome` (provable termination), fuel inductions, and the proof-transparent canonicalization (`runLengths_prod_map`/`runLengths_mem`/`runLengths_chain'_lt` + `mergeSort` permutation). |
| `BasuPollackRoy/Chapter1/Section1_1` | Algebraically closed fields, zero sets (`Zer`), algebraic/constructible sets, first-order formulas in the language of fields, quantifier-free and prenex normal forms, realization, prenex normal form theorem. Exercises 1.1–1.3. |
| `BasuPollackRoy/Chapter1/Section1_2` | Euclidean division, GCD/LCM (definitions and propositions), coprimality, signed remainder sequences. Proposition 1.5, Corollary 1.6, Exercises 1.5–1.7, Proposition 1.8. |
| `BasuPollackRoy/Chapter1/Section1_3` | Projection theorem for constructible sets (in progress). Specialization `P_y(X)` of a polynomial in `C[Y₁,…,Y_k,X]` at `y ∈ C^k` (`specialize`, `eval_specialize`). Projection `Formula.proj` and fiber `Formula.fiber` of a formula-defined set in `C^{k+1}`. Signed pseudo-remainder `PRem(P, Q)` with scaling exponent `pRemExp`. Truncation `Tru_i(Q) = b_i X^i + ⋯ + b_0` (Notation 1.16) with coefficient characterization. |
| `BasuPollackRoy/Chapter2/Section2_1` | Ordered fields (Notation 2.3: sign, absolute value), ordered field examples (ℚ, ℝ), Exercise 2.3 (ℂ not orderable), Proposition 2.4 (sign preservation for large inputs), infinitesimal/unbounded elements over an ordered field extension. Cones (Def 2.6, `IsCone`/`IsProperCone`), Proposition 2.6, Lemma 2.9 (cone extension), Proposition 2.8 (Zorn-based extension to total proper cone), Theorem 2.7 (characterizations of real fields). **Notation 2.18**: `Ri R = R[T]/(T²+1)`, imaginary unit `Ri.i`, conjugation `Ri.conj`, `normSq`/`normSqR`, modulus `|z| = √(a²+b²)`. |
| `BasuPollackRoy/Chapter2/OrderZeroPlus` | **Notation 2.5**: The 0₊ order on F[ε]. `LinearOrder` and `IsStrictOrderedRing` on `F[X]` via trailing coefficient positivity. ε notation, proof that ε is infinitesimal (0 < ε < C a for positive a), `C` is strictly monotone. |
| `BasuPollackRoy/Chapter2/Theorem_2_11*` | **Theorem 2.11**: For an ordered field R, TFAE: (a) R is real closed, (b) R[i] is algebraically closed, (c) R has the intermediate value property, (d) R is real with no non-trivial real algebraic extension. Split across files `Theorem_2_11_a_b`, `_b_c`, `_b_d`, `_c_a`, `_d_a`; combined TFAE in `Theorem_2_11`. Key helpers: `conj_pair_dvd_map`, `quad_of_conj_pair`, `dvd_of_map_dvd_monic`, `irred_degree_eq_two`. |
| `BasuPollackRoy/Chapter2/Proposition_2_19` | **Proposition 2.19**: Over an ordered field R with R[i] algebraically closed, every monic irreducible polynomial is either linear or of the form `(X − c)² + d²` with `d ≠ 0`. Real-closed-field corollary `proposition_2_19_of_isRealClosed`. |
| `BasuPollackRoy/Chapter2/Proposition_2_20` | **Proposition 2.20**: Over an ordered field R with the intermediate value property, if `P ∈ R[X]` does not vanish on `(a, b)` then `P` has constant sign on `(a, b)` (everywhere positive or everywhere negative). Real-closed-field corollary `proposition_2_20_of_isRealClosed`. |
| `BasuPollackRoy/Chapter2/Proposition_2_21` | **Proposition 2.21**: If `r` is a root of `P ≠ 0` of multiplicity `µ` in a real closed field `R`, then the sign of `P` to the right of `r` equals `sign(P^{(µ)}(r))` and the sign to the left equals `(−1)^µ · sign(P^{(µ)}(r))`. Helper lemmas: `hasSignRight_of_eval_ne_zero`, `hasSignLeft_of_eval_ne_zero` (constant sign at non-vanishing points); `exists_no_root_Ioo_right/left` (non-vanishing intervals around non-roots). Real-closed-field corollaries `proposition_2_21_right/left_of_isRealClosed`. **Proposition 2.22 (Rolle's theorem)**: If `P(a) = P(b) = 0` with `a < b`, then `P'` has a root in `(a, b)`. Proved via factorization `P = (X−a)^m (X−b)^n Q`, derivative sign analysis of `Q₁ = m(X−b)Q + n(X−a)Q + (X−a)(X−b)Q'`, and reduction to closest root. Real-closed corollary `proposition_2_22_of_isRealClosed`. **Corollary 2.23 (Mean Value Theorem)**: For `P ∈ R[X]` and `a < b`, there exists `c ∈ (a,b)` with `P(b) − P(a) = (b − a) · P'(c)`. Proved by applying Rolle to `Q(X) = (P(b)−P(a))(X−a) − (b−a)(P(X)−P(a))`. Real-closed corollary `corollary_2_23_of_isRealClosed`. **Corollary 2.24**: If `P' > 0` (resp. `< 0`) on `(a,b)`, then `P` is strictly increasing (resp. decreasing) on `[a,b]`. Proved via MVT. Real-closed corollaries `corollary_2_24_increasing/decreasing_of_isRealClosed`. |
| `BasuPollackRoy/Chapter2/SignCondition` | **Definition 2.25**: Sign conditions. `SignCondition ι = ι → SignType`, `IsStrict` (values in `{1, −1}`), `IsRealizedBy` (a family of values realizes a sign condition when each value's sign matches), `realization` (the set of points realizing `σ`), `IsRealizable` (realization is non-empty). Generic over any evaluation; for polynomials use `σ.realization (fun i x => (Q i).eval x)`. **Notation 2.26**: `der P` is the list `[P, P', …, P⁽ᵖ⁾]` of successive derivatives. |
| `BasuPollackRoy/Chapter2/Proposition_2_27` | **Proposition 2.27 (Basic Thom's Lemma)**: Let `P ∈ R[X]` with `natDegree P ≤ n` and `σ` a sign condition on the first `n + 1` derivatives. Then the realization `{x | ∀ i ≤ n, sign(P⁽ⁱ⁾(x)) = σ(i)}` is either empty, a singleton, or an open interval. Proved by induction on `n`, using MVT (Cor 2.23), monotonicity from derivative sign (Cor 2.24), and IVP. Defines `IsOpenInterval` (nonempty, ord-connected, no min/max), `derReali` (derivative realization set). Helper lemmas: `sign_filter_mono/anti` (sign filtering preserves the trichotomy for monotone/antitone functions). Real-closed corollary `proposition_2_27_of_isRealClosed`. |
| `BasuPollackRoy/Chapter2/Proposition_2_28` | **Proposition 2.28 (Thom Encoding)**: Let `P ∈ R[X]` be non-zero of degree `p`, and let `x, x'` realize sign conditions `σ, σ'` on `Der(P)`. **Part 1 (Root injectivity)**: If `σ = σ'` and `σ(P) = 0`, then `x = x'`. Proved by showing the realization set is either empty, a singleton (done), or an open interval (contradicts finiteness of roots). **Part 2 (Comparison)**: If `σ ≠ σ'`, let `j` be the largest index where they differ. Then `σ(j+1) = σ'(j+1) ≠ 0`, and `x < x'` is determined by `σ(j+1)` and the ordering of `σ(j)` vs `σ'(j)`. Proved using Proposition 2.27, strict monotonicity/antitonicity from derivative sign, and Part 1 for the non-zero claim. Real-closed corollaries `proposition_2_28_part1/part2_of_isRealClosed`. **Definition 2.29 (Thom encoding)**: A sign condition `σ` on `Der(P)` is a Thom encoding of `x` if `σ(P) = 0` and `derReali P n σ = {x}`. `IsThomEncoding P n σ x`. Theorem `isThomEncoding_of_mem`: any root realization gives a Thom encoding (immediate from Part 1). Real-closed corollary `isThomEncoding_of_mem_of_isRealClosed`. **Example 2.30**: Roots of `X² − 2` are distinguished by derivative sign: `r² = s² = 2` and `sign(r) = sign(s)` imply `r = s`. |
| `BasuPollackRoy/Chapter2/Section2_2` | **Notation 2.32 (Sign variations)**: `Var(a)` counts the number of adjacent pairs of opposite signs in a finite sequence `a : List R` over an ordered ring `R`, after dropping all zeros. Helper `varNonzero` implements the pairwise recursion on zero-free lists (`varNonzero_cons_cons` unfolding lemma). `Var` extends this by filtering zeros (`Var_eq_varNonzero_filter`, `Var_of_forall_ne_zero`). Sign-helper lemmas: `varNonzero_eq_zero_of_forall_nonneg/nonpos`, `Var_eq_zero_of_forall_nonneg/nonpos` (a list of same-signed entries has zero variations), `Var_zero_cons` (prepending a zero is a no-op). **`Var(P)` and `pos(P)`**: `varPoly P = Var(a₀, …, aₚ)` is the sign variations in the coefficient sequence of `P = aₚ Xᵖ + ⋯ + a₀`. `posRoots P` is the number of positive real roots of `P` counted with multiplicity (via `Polynomial.roots.filter (0 < ·)`). **Notation 2.34 (Sign variations at a point)**: `varAt P a` is `Var(P₀(a), …, P_d(a))` for `P : List R[X]` at `a : ExtendedPoint R` (an element of `R ∪ {−∞, +∞}`). At `±∞` the sign comes from the leading monomial per Proposition 2.4: `evalPoly P posInf = leadingCoeff P`, `evalPoly P negInf = (−1)^natDegree · leadingCoeff P`. Unfolding lemmas `varAt_finite/posInf/negInf/nil`. **`Var(P; a, b)`**: `varBetween P a b = varAt P a − varAt P b : ℤ`. **`num(P; (a, b])`**: `numRoots P a b : ℕ` is the multiplicity-weighted root count of `P` in the half-open interval `(a, b]`, pattern-matched over `a, b : ExtendedPoint R` (empty when `a = +∞` or `b = −∞`; total roots when `(−∞, +∞)`). |
| `BasuPollackRoy/Chapter2/CauchyIndex` | **BPR Definition 2.53 (Cauchy index)**: `cauchyIndexOn Q P a b` for `a, b : ExtendedPoint R` is the number of jumps of `Q/P` from `−∞` to `+∞` minus the number from `+∞` to `−∞` on the open interval `(a, b)`. `cauchyIndex Q P := cauchyIndexOn Q P .negInf .posInf` is the full-line version. Helper `ExtendedPoint.openInterval` exposes `(a, b) ⊆ R` as a `Set R`. **Bounds:** `cauchyIndexOn_le_card_roots_toFinset` and `cauchyIndexOn_le_natDegree`. **Extraction:** `cauchyIndexOn_eq_natDegree_extract` — when the index attains `P.natDegree`, then `P.roots.toFinset.card = P.natDegree`, `P.roots.Nodup`, every distinct `P`-root lies in `(a, b)` and produces a `−∞ → +∞` jump, and none produces a `+∞ → −∞` jump. **Sign-analysis machinery:** `derivative_eval_at_root_of_splits`, `sign_prod_sub`, `sign_derivative_at_simple_root`, `sign_eval_at_non_root`. **Combinatorial machinery:** `card_filter_gt_Q_eq_P`, `P_root_not_Q_root`. **Composite jump lemmas:** `jumps_at_P_root` (every `P`-root produces a `−∞ → +∞` jump under the structural conditions), `exists_Q_root_in_gap` (Q has a root between consecutive P-roots, via Prop 2.21 + IVP). Includes `Interlacing P Q` predicate. **Remark 2.55(a) (fully proven, both directions):** with `0 < P.natDegree` and `Q.degree < P.degree`, `cauchyIndexOn Q P a b = P.natDegree` iff `Q.natDegree + 1 = P.natDegree`, leading-coefficient signs of `P` and `Q` agree, all roots of `P` and `Q` are simple and lie in `(a, b)`, and `P, Q` interlace. The proof uses the IVP hypothesis to apply Proposition 2.21 (left/right) for sign analysis, plus an explicit bijection from non-max P-roots to `Q.roots.toFinset` for the cardinality and interlacing arguments. The leading-coefficient sign claim uses `hasSignAtPosInfty_leadingCoeff` plus IVP-driven constancy of `Q · P` past `max_P`. **Remark 2.55(b) (fully proven)** (`cauchyIndexOn (Q % P) P a b = cauchyIndexOn Q P a b`, taking IVP as a hypothesis): the proof reduces via `Finset.filter_congr` to a per-root jump-iff and uses several proven helpers — `rootMultiplicity_mod_lt_iff_of_mod_ne` (the multiplicity-equivalence `Q.rootMul x < µ_P ↔ (Q%P).rootMul x < µ_P` with mults equal under either, via the proven `Polynomial.rootMultiplicity_add_of_lt` and `Polynomial.rootMultiplicity_neg`), and `hasSignRight_mod_iff_of_mod_ne` (sign agreement on the right of any `P`-root, using Proposition 2.21 + uniqueness of `HasSignRight` to reduce to comparing the canonical `(D^k)`-derivative signs, with the `K · P²`-difference contributing nothing via the helper `eval_iterate_derivative_eq_zero_of_lt_rootMultiplicity` proved by Leibniz expansion of `p = (X − Cx)^µ · q`). |
| `BasuPollackRoy/Chapter2/Jumps` | **BPR Chapter 2.2 jump predicates**: `JumpsFromNegInfToPosInf Q P x` and `JumpsFromPosInfToNegInf Q P x` capture when the rational function `Q/P` jumps from `−∞` to `+∞` (resp. `+∞` to `−∞`) at a root `x` of `P`. Both require `P.rootMultiplicity x > Q.rootMultiplicity x` with odd difference; the two differ in the right-hand sign of `Q · P` (`+1` vs `−1`). **Implied facts:** sign of `Q · P` flips across `x` over any ordered field with the IVP (`hasSignLeft`); over `ℝ`, `Q/P` actually tends to `±∞` from each side via `Filter.Tendsto … atTop` / `atBot` (`tendsto_atTop_nhdsGT`, `tendsto_atBot_nhdsLT`, `tendsto_atBot_nhdsGT`, `tendsto_atTop_nhdsLT`). The `ℝ` proof uses the factorisation `Q · P = (X − C x)^(m+n) · R` and `P = (X − C x)^m · P̃` together with `Polynomial.continuous`, `Tendsto.inv_tendsto_nhdsGT_zero` / `nhdsLT_zero`, and `Tendsto.atTop_mul_pos` / `atBot_mul_pos`. |
| `BasuPollackRoy/Chapter2/SturmSequence` | **Sturm sequence** (BPR Chapter 2.2 definition): `sturmSequence P = SRemS P P.derivative`, the signed remainder sequence of `P` and its derivative (Definition 1.7 specialized to `Q = P'`). Defined for any field; the "non-zero `P` over a real closed field" hypothesis is a precondition for downstream theorems, not for the definition. Includes unfolding lemmas `sturmSequence_zero` (`= P`) and `sturmSequence_one` (`= P.derivative`). |
| `BasuPollackRoy/Chapter2/TarskiQuery` | **BPR Tarski-query**: `tarskiQueryOn Q P a b` for `a, b : ExtendedPoint R` is `∑_{x ∈ (a, b), P(x) = 0} sign(Q(x)) : ℤ`, summing over `P.roots.toFinset` filtered by membership in the open interval. `tarskiQuery Q P := tarskiQueryOn Q P .negInf .posInf` is the full-line version. Includes the simp unfolding `tarskiQuery_eq_tarskiQueryOn_negInf_posInf` and the equivalent root-count form `tarskiQueryOn_eq_card_pos_sub_card_neg`: `tarskiQueryOn Q P a b = #{x ∈ (a, b) ∩ P.roots | Q(x) > 0} − #{x ∈ (a, b) ∩ P.roots | Q(x) < 0}`. |
| `BasuPollackRoy/Chapter2/Lemma_2_60` | **BPR Lemma 2.60 (fully proven)**: Cauchy index of `Q/P` vs `−R/Q` (where `R = P % Q`) under one SRemS step. Statement: `2 · (Ind(Q/P; a, b) − Ind(−R/Q; a, b)) = σ(b) − σ(a)` where `σ(t) = sign(P(t)·Q(t))` (assumed nonzero at `a` and `b`), under the `a < b` hypothesis encoded as `ExtendedPoint.Lt a b` (matching BPR's explicit "for `a < b`" assumption). Equivalently: `Ind(Q/P) = Ind(−R/Q)` when `σ(a) = σ(b)`, and `Ind(Q/P) = Ind(−R/Q) + σ(b)` when `σ(a) ≠ σ(b)`. **Components:** `ExtendedPoint.Lt` (strict order on extended endpoints, defined by case analysis: false for empty-interval pairs, true for ordered finite/infinite pairs); `cauchyIndexOn_neg_left` (`Ind(−Q/P) = −Ind(Q/P)`); `cauchyIndexOn_neg_mod` (`Ind(−(P%Q)/Q) = −Ind(P/Q)`); the leading-factor factorization `divByMonic_pow_mul_eq_mul` (`(P·Q)~(x) = P̃(x) · Q̃(x)`); jump-zero helpers `jumpContrib_eq_zero_of_rootMultiplicity_zero` and `jumpContrib_eq_zero_of_le`; the per-jump-formula helper `jumpContrib_eq_sign_leading_factor` (when `µ_P > µ_Q` and the diff is odd, `jumpContrib Q P x = sign((Q·P)~(x))`, proved via Prop 2.21 + uniqueness); `per_root_jump_sum_eq_signFlip` (per-root identity `jumpContrib Q P x + jumpContrib P Q x = signFlipAt (P·Q) x`, with both even-sum and odd-sum cases handled); `cauchyIndexOn_eq_sum_jumpContrib` (rewriting `cauchyIndexOn` as a `jumpContrib` sum); `cauchyIndexOn_swap_sum` (combines the per-root identity with `Finset.sum_union_eq_left/right` and `cauchyIndexOn_eq_sum_jumpContrib` to derive `2 · (Ind(Q/P) + Ind(P/Q)) = σ(b) − σ(a)`); the final `lemma_2_60` derived from `cauchyIndexOn_swap_sum` plus `cauchyIndexOn_neg_mod`. Sub-helpers: `sign_jump_at_root_eq_signFlipAt` (single-root jump = `2 · signFlipAt F r`); `sign_eval_eq_of_no_root_Ioo` (finite no-root interval has constant sign); `roots_filter_eq_empty_of_no_root`; `sign_eval_eq_canonical_left/right` (`sign(F(a)) = canonical s_left/right at adjacent root r`); `running_sign_flip_count_finite` (finite-endpoint version, by strong induction on root count picking `Finset.min'`); `running_sign_flip_count` (ExtendedPoint version, dispatched by case analysis on `(a, b) ∈ {finite, posInf, negInf}²`: non-empty cases reduce to the finite version via `exists_finite_left_of_negInf` / `exists_finite_right_of_posInf`; empty-interval cases dismissed by `Lt`-derived contradictions). Helper definitions: `signFlipAt`, `jumpContrib`, `sigmaPQ`. |
| `BasuPollackRoy/Chapter2/Lemma_2_59` | **BPR Lemma 2.59 (fully proven)**: Var of the signed remainder sequence at a single SRemS step. With `R' = P % Q` and `Q ≠ 0`: at any `x : ExtendedPoint K` where `evalPoly P x ≠ 0` and `evalPoly Q x ≠ 0`, `varAt (SRemSList P Q (n+2)) x = varAt (SRemSList Q (-R') (n+1)) x + (1 if (P·Q)(x) < 0 else 0)` (`lemma_2_59_pointwise`). The interval-difference form `lemma_2_59` follows by subtraction at `a` and `b`. Key infrastructure: `SRemSList P Q n = (List.range n).map (SRemS P Q)`, head decomposition `SRemSList_succ` (needs `Q ≠ 0`) and `SRemSList_head_decomp` (no hypothesis), tail identity `SRemS_succ` (`SRemS P Q (i+1) = SRemS Q (-(P%Q)) i` for `Q ≠ 0`), generic `Var_cons_cons_of_ne_zero` (`Var (a::b::L) = Var (b::L) + [a*b<0]` when `a, b ≠ 0`), and its `varAt` specialisation. |
| `BasuPollackRoy/Chapter2/Theorem_2_58` | **BPR Theorem 2.58 (fully proven)**: `Var(SRemS(P, Q); a, b) = Ind(Q/P; a, b)` for `P ≠ 0` over a real closed field, under `ExtendedPoint.Lt a b` and the "no-root-on-SRemS" hypothesis (`a` and `b` are not roots of any nonzero polynomial in the signed remainder sequence). Proven by strong induction on the truncation index `n` (with `SRemS P Q n = 0`): the `n = 0` case contradicts `P ≠ 0`; the `n = 1` case forces `Q = 0`, so both sides vanish (`varAt [P]` is `Var [P(x)] = 0` by `Var_singleton`, and `cauchyIndexOn 0 P a b = 0` by `cauchyIndexOn_zero_left` since `JumpsFrom...0 P x` is impossible); the `n + 2` case splits on `Q = 0` (`SRemSList P 0 (n+2) = [P, 0, …, 0]` reduces by `varAt_singleton_with_trailing_zeros`) versus `Q ≠ 0` (combine Lemma 2.59, Lemma 2.60, and the IH applied to the tail `(Q, -R)` at index `n + 1` via the tail identity `SRemS Q (-R) i = SRemS P Q (i+1)`). The σ-correction algebra uses `two_mul_indicator_eq` (`2·(if P·Q < 0 then 1 else 0) = 1 - sigmaPQ`, by trichotomy of `sign`). Helper lemmas: `varNonzero_eq_zero_of_length_le_one`, `Var_singleton`, `varAt_singleton_with_trailing_zeros`, `not_jumpsFromNegInfToPosInf_zero`, `not_jumpsFromPosInfToNegInf_zero`, `cauchyIndexOn_zero_left`. |
| `BasuPollackRoy/Chapter2/Proposition_2_57` | **BPR Proposition 2.57 (fully proven)**: `tarskiQueryOn Q P a b = cauchyIndexOn (P.derivative * Q) P a b`, taking IVP as a hypothesis. The proof goes via per-root iffs `jumpsFromNegInfToPosInf_derivative_iff_pos` (`JumpsFromNegInfToPosInf (P'·Q) P x ↔ 0 < Q(x)`) and `jumpsFromPosInfToNegInf_derivative_iff_neg` (`JumpsFromPosInfToNegInf (P'·Q) P x ↔ Q(x) < 0`). Key helpers: `rootMultiplicity_derivative` (`P'.rootMul x + 1 = P.rootMul x` when `µ ≥ 1`, via the factorization `P' = (X−Cx)^{µ−1} · g` with `g(x) = µ · P̃(x) ≠ 0` using char-0); `derivative_ne_zero_of_mem_roots` (in char 0, P-with-a-root has nonzero derivative); `hasSignRight_derivative_mul_self` (when `Q(x) ≠ 0`, `P'·Q·P` has sign `sign(Q(x))` on the right of `x`, via the factorization `P'·Q·P = (X−Cx)^{2µ−1} · (g · Q · P̃)` whose value at `x` is `µ · P̃(x)² · Q(x)`, plus IVP). The main theorem combines these via `Finset.filter_congr` after rewriting `tarskiQueryOn` as a difference of root counts. **Corollary `cauchyIndexOn_derivative_self_eq_card_roots_in_openInterval`** specialises `Q = 1` to recover the number-of-distinct-roots formula: `#{x ∈ P.roots.toFinset | x ∈ (a, b)} = cauchyIndexOn P' P a b`. |
| `BasuPollackRoy/Chapter2/Theorem_2_33` | **Theorem 2.33 (Descartes' rule of signs, fully proven)**: Over a real closed field `R`, for `P ∈ R[X]`, `Var(P) ≥ pos(P)` and `Var(P) − pos(P)` is even. Stepping-stone identity `varPoly_eq_varBetween_der` proves `Var(P) = Var(Der(P); 0, +∞)` via two fully-formalized helpers: `varAt_der_posInf` (leading coeffs of successive derivatives all share the sign of `leadingCoeff P`, using `coeff_iterate_derivative_at_natDegree_sub` and `natDegree_and_leadingCoeff_iterate_derivative`) and `varAt_der_finite_zero` (derivative-at-zero equals `i! · aᵢ`, so positive scaling preserves `Var`, via `varNonzero_map_mul_pos` and `Var_map_range_scale_eq`). The main theorem `descartes_rule_of_signs` reduces via `varPoly_eq_varBetween_der` and `posRoots_eq_numRoots` to `budan_fourier_posInf` on the half-open interval `(0, +∞)`. |
| `BasuPollackRoy/Chapter2/Exercise_2_11` | **Exercise 2.11**: The real algebraic numbers `ℝ_alg = algebraicClosure ℚ ℝ` are real closed. Proves `IsRealClosed R_alg` by establishing: (1) `IsSemireal` — sums of squares transfer along the embedding to ℝ, ruling out `−1`; (2) `isSquare_or_isSquare_neg` — for nonneg `x`, `√x` is algebraic via `p.comp(X²)` witness using `leadingCoeff_comp`; (3) `exists_isRoot_of_odd_natDegree` — odd-degree real polynomials have roots by strong induction on degree using `Irreducible.natDegree_le_two`, then roots of polynomials with algebraic coefficients are algebraic by `IsIntegral.trans_isAlgebraic`. |
| `BasuPollackRoy/Chapter2/Theorem_2_31` | **Theorem 2.31**: Every algebraically closed field of characteristic zero contains a real closed subfield. Proves existence of a maximal semireal intermediate field `R` over `ℚ` via Zorn's lemma (`zorn_le₀`), using `Subfield.mem_sSup_of_directedOn` for chain completeness and `IsAlgClosed.lift` to show maximality implies `HasNoNontrivialRealAlgebraicExtension`, then applies Theorem 2.11 (d⇒a). |
| `BasuPollackRoy/Chapter8/Section8_1` | Complexity structures (D₀–D₇), bitsize of integers and rationals, bitsize bounds for sums and products, monomial counting (Lemma 8.6 with `MonicMonomial` bridge), bitsize of polynomial addition/multiplication. Notation 8.7 (Horner polynomials with sum/eval identities), Algorithm 8.7 (polynomial evaluation), Algorithm 8.8 (special evaluation with `horSpecial` sum characterization, field identity, and bitsize bound `τ + iτ' + bit(p+1)`), Algorithm 8.9 (translation), Algorithm 8.10 (special translation). Cross-references to Azurite implementations of Algorithms 8.1–8.10. |
| `BasuPollackRoy/Chapter8/Section8_2` | §8.2.1 **Proposition 8.12 (Hadamard's inequality)**: `\|det(M)\| ≤ ∏ᵢ ‖cᵢ‖` for the columns `cᵢ` of an integer matrix `M`. Via Gram–Schmidt (Proposition 4.41): square-root-free core `det_sq_le_prod_dotProduct_col` (`det(M)² ≤ ∏ᵢ (cᵢ·cᵢ)` over any ordered field, change of basis `W = M·Cᵀ` with `C` unit lower-triangular plus `WᵀW = diag(‖wᵢ‖²)`), euclidean-norm form `abs_det_le_prod_euclideanNorm_col` over a real closed field, then `proposition_8_12` for ℤ-matrices by casting to ℝ. **Corollary 8.13 (bitsize of a determinant)**: for entries of bitsize ≤ τ, `\|det(M)\| < 2^{n(τ + bit(n)/2)}` (`corollary_8_13`); square-root-free ℕ core `corollary_8_13_sq` (`\|det(M)\|² < 2^{n(2τ + bit(n))}`). **Proposition 8.14 (degree of a determinant)**: `det(M)` of a matrix of polynomials in `Y₁…Y_k` of total degree ≤ d has total degree ≤ `d·n` (`proposition_8_14`); via the Leibniz expansion + `totalDegree` of sum/product/sign. **Proposition 8.15 (degree + bitsize)**: for `ℤ[Y₁…Y_k]` entries of degree ≤ d and coefficient bitsize ≤ τ, `det(M)` has degree ≤ `d·n` and coefficient bitsize ≤ `n(τ + bit(n) + k·bit(d+1))` (`proposition_8_15`, n ≥ 1); via Remark 8.10 (per-product coeff bitsize) + `Int.size_finset_sum_le'` over the `n!` Leibniz terms (`bit(n!−1) ≤ n·bit(n)`). §8.2.3 **Proposition 8.16 (characteristic polynomial)**: coefficients of `charpoly M` (for `ℤ[Y₁…Y_k]` entries of degree ≤ d, bitsize ≤ τ) have degree ≤ `d·n` and coefficient bitsize ≤ `n(τ + bit(n) + k·bit(d+1) + 1)` (`proposition_8_16`, n ≥ 1); via `Matrix.charpoly_coeff_eq_sum_minors` (coeff = ±sum of principal minors) + Prop 8.15 per minor (reindexed `↥s`→`Fin c`) + `Int.size_finset_sum_le'` over `C(n,c) ≤ 2ⁿ` minors. §8.2.4 **Proposition 8.24 (signature via Descartes)**: for a symmetric matrix `M` over a real closed field, `Sign(M) = Var(1,a_{n-1},…,a₀) − Var((−1)ⁿ,…,a₀)` where the `aᵢ` are the charpoly coefficients (`proposition_8_24`, stated as `Sign (quadraticForm M) = varPoly M.charpoly − varPoly (M.charpoly.comp (−X))`); since all eigenvalues are real (Theorem 4.43), Descartes' rule is exact (Remark 2.38), so `varPoly = posRoots` (new `varPoly_eq_posRoots_of_all_roots_real`, the `(0,∞)` specialization in `Section2_2/Remark_2_38`), and `Sign = #pos − #neg roots` is Corollary 4.45 (with `roots_comp_neg_X` mapping `−X`'s positive roots to `M`'s negative ones). **Algorithm 8.18 (Signature Through Descartes)**: the computable `AzMatrix.signature M = Var(1,a_{n-1},…,a₀) − Var((−1)ⁿ,…,a₀)` (in `Section8_2/Algorithm_8_18`, namespace `Azurite.AzMatrix`), reading the charpoly coefficients off `charPoly` (Alg 8.17) and counting sign variations with `Azurite.BPR.Var`; over an ordered field (`AzRat`), tested by `#guard`. **Proven correct** (`signature_eq_Sign`: `M.signature = Sign (quadraticForm (toMat M))` for symmetric M over a real closed field) via `toPoly_charPoly` (Alg 8.17 correctness) + the new `Azurite.BPR.Var_reverse` (reversal-invariance of `Var`, linking the decreasing-degree algorithm lists to the increasing-degree `varPoly` of Prop 8.24) + `proposition_8_24`. |

## Known Textbook Errors (BPR)

While formalizing *Algorithms in Real Algebraic Geometry* (Basu, Pollack, Roy), the following mathematical discrepancies were identified and corrected in Azurite:

*   **Lemma 1.10(b) (Signed Remainder Determinant Sequence):** The textbook states the identity $U_i V_{i+1} - V_i U_{i+1} = (-1)^i$. However, the textbook's definition of the cofactor sequence ($U_{i+2} = U_i - Q_{i+1} U_{i+1}$) drops the negation required to compute signed remainders ($P_{i+2} = - P_i + Q_{i+1} P_{i+1}$). When the strictly correct recurrence is used, the sequence determinant evaluates structurally to $1$ unconditionally. Azurite's formalization proves `lemma_1_10_b` equal to `1` and applies the corrected identity in subsequent proofs (e.g., Proposition 1.12).

our instantiation supplies nontriviality of `χ_{p,q}` (`ofRootOfUnity_ne_one`), primitivity of the additive character, and `#(ZMod q) = q`. **Lemma 4.4.2** (`lemma_4_4_2`): `G(p,q)^(n^(p−1)−1) ≡ χ_{p,q}(n) (mod n)` for primes `p, q, n` with `gcd(pq, n) = 1` — as ring divisibility `n ∣ G^(n^(p−1)−1) − χ(n)` in any domain (= the book's coefficientwise congruence in `ℤ[ζ_p,ζ_q]` by freeness); proof runs in `R⧸(n)` where char = `n` (or the quotient collapses, trivializing the claim): Frobenius `sum_pow_char_pow` is the multinomial step, Fermat gives `χ(m)^(n^(p−1)) = χ(m)`, `gaussSum_mulShift` is the `m ↦ m·n^(p−1)` substitution costing `χ(n)` (since `χ(n^p) = 1`), then multiply by `conj G`, apply 4.4.1, and cancel the Bézout-invertible `q`. **Lemma 4.4.3** (`lemma_4_4_3`): distinct powers of `ζ_m` stay distinct mod `n` when `n ∤ m` (ring form `¬(n:R) ∣ (m:R)` over a domain — the book's `n ∤ m` via freeness of `ℤ[ζ_m]`); reduces to `ζ^e ≡ 1` by multiplying with `ζ^((m−1)k)`, then the offending factor `1 − ζ^(e mod m)` would divide `∏_{l=1}^{m−1}(1 − ζ^l) = m` (Mathlib's `IsPrimitiveRoot.prod_one_sub_pow_eq_order`) forcing `n ∣ m` — the pigeonhole letting the test read off character values from residues. `Definition_4_4_4.lean` builds the **concrete model of `ℤ[ζ_p, ζ_q]`** as the symbolic tower the book prescribes — `CycPQ p q = ℤ[x]/(Φ_p)[y]/(Φ_q)` via double `AdjoinRoot` — where the unique-representation claim is a free theorem: `cycBasis` (product of the two monic power bases, `AdjoinRoot.powerBasis'` + `Basis.smulTower`) with `cycBasis_apply` identifying the basis vectors as the monomials `ζ_p^i ζ_q^k`. **Definition 4.4.4**: `content α` = gcd of the coordinate grid (`c(0) = 0` free), with `natCast_dvd_iff_dvd_content` — ring-divisibility by `n` ⟺ `n ∣ c(α)`, bridging the lemmas' ring congruences to the book's coefficientwise ones. `CycPQDomain.lean` proves the model FAITHFUL for distinct primes: `isDomain_cycPQ` + `isPrimitiveRoot_zetaP`/`zetaQ` — the facts instantiating the abstract Lemmas 4.4.1–4.4.3 at the model — via an embedding `cycEmb : CycPQ p q →+* ℚ(ζ_pq)` (double `AdjoinRoot.lift`, `ζ_p ↦ η^q`, `ζ_q ↦ η^p`); also `natCast_dvd_natCast_iff` (integers are faithful in the model, converting Lemma 4.4.3's ring-form hypothesis into the book's `n ∤ m`; no distinctness needed). The `ℤ[ζ_p, ζ_q]` unique representation + mod-`n` congruence are planned as the `AzPolyMod` tower mod `x^(p−1)+…+1`, `y^(q−1)+…+1` over `AzZMod n` when the test arrives. `Exercise_4_24.lean` opens the **Algorithm 4.4.5 (Gauss sums primality test) / Theorem 4.4.6 correctness arc** with the q-side engine: **Exercise 4.24** (`exercise_4_24`) — a character mod `q` of maximal order `q−1` is one-to-one on `Z_q` (cyclic units + `orderOf_mulChar_eq`, the order of a character = the order of its value at a generator; nonunits go to 0) — and the **product-character engine** `eq_of_forall_primeFactor_char_eq`: for `q−1` squarefree, characters of order `p` for each prime `p | q−1` multiply into a character of order `∏p = q−1` (`orderOf_prod_primes`, commuting factors of coprime prime orders; unit-evaluation via `prod_mulChar_apply_isUnit`), so units agreeing under every `χ_p` are EQUAL — the final step of Theorem 4.4.6, concluding `r ≡ l^a (mod q)` per prime `q | F` (then mod `F` by CRT, and the divisor search finds `r`). The route avoids induction through the division algorithm: constant multiplication never grows the degree, so no `modByMonic` fires — `ofPoly_of_isReduced` (canonicity by `toAdjoin`-injectivity), `val_ofCoeff_mul` (the representative of `ofCoeff d * S` is literally `C d * S.val`), and scaled-coefficient semantics give a `d`-divisible grid for any `d`-multiple (`grid_dvd_of_natCast_mul`), whence `d` divides the seeded gcd fold. (En route, a config-instance gotcha: raw `AzPolynomial` products in new files must elaborate with the same `AzPolynomialMulConfig` as the `AzPolyMod` layer — the Karatsuba import was dropped from the rail pending config-generic `toPoly_mul`.) And the **rail-verdict core is assembled** (`AzPolyMod/GaussSumsTest.lean`): `prime_of_rail_checks` — if every check of Algorithm 4.4.5 passes at the level of the computable tower (the step-3/4 `powAzNat` equalities against `zetaPPowT`, `step5Check`, the generator/table checks, a clean step-6 scan), then **`n` is prime**. Proof by contradiction through the tower-form correctness theorem, each rail check crossing via the bridge stack (kernel theorem + G-element correspondence for steps 3/4; content correspondence with the `∀ j`-extension by `ζ_p`-periodicity for step 5). **And the checker itself is DONE** (`gaussSumsTest` + `gaussSumsTest_eq_true`): a computable Boolean verdict over a `GaussSumsCert` (factor lists of `I`/`F`, the `w`/`u`/`l(p,q)`/`l(q)`/`l` tables, the `q₀` choices, the primitive roots `g`) — verifying the arithmetic side (distinct-prime lists → squarefree via `squarefree_prod_of_nodup_primes`; gcd and `F² > n` at the limb level), the generator power checks, the step-3/4 tower equalities, the step-5 content gcds, the step-6 tables, and the divisor scan — **with a `true` verdict PROVING `n` prime**. Guards: `n = 7` certified prime end-to-end through the Gauss sums machinery (`I = 2`, `F = 6`, the quadratic Gauss sum, `l = 1`); the same certificate rejects 9. **The verdict form and completeness are DONE, making this the SECOND fully two-sided test in the library** (after Alg. 4.3.4): `gaussSumsTestO : Option Bool` answers `some true` (PROVEN ⟹ prime: membership in the certified-prime lists, or the full test), `some false` (PROVEN ⟹ composite: the step-2 gcd via `step2_composite_of_gcd`, or a factor detector — `step5FactorFound`, a content gcd strictly between 1 and `n`, or `scanFactorFound`, a scanned residue properly dividing `n`; both exhibit a proper factor since the content gcd divides `n` AND every grid entry, `towerContentGcd_dvd`), or `none` (no claim). Completeness (`gaussSumsTest_complete` + `gaussSumsTestO_complete`): for PRIME `n` and genuinely valid certificate data (true generator family, step-3/4 congruences in `ℤ[ζ_p,ζ_q]`, step-5 minimality, step-6 tables) every check passes — the one nontrivial branch is step 5, by the prime-`n` shortcut `towerContentGcd_eq_one_of_ne_zero`: the content gcd divides the prime `n`, and the value `n` would zero out the whole sub-`n` coefficient grid, hence the element (`gaussTower_eq_zero_of_grid`, via the no-trailing-zero invariants), contradicting the step-5 non-congruence through the kernel theorem and the shared check-element reduction `reduceT_sub_pow`. O-guards: `some true` on 7, `some false` on 9, `none` on 25. Remaining for practicality: the limb-level port of the large certificate scalars and a certificate generator. **The Jacobi-sums groundwork for APR-CL is laid** (`JacobiSums.lean`, the book's closing §4.4 passage): `bValue p` = the book's `b(p)` (least positive `b` with `(b+1)^p ≢ b^p+1 mod p²`; existence proven by telescoping to `p^p ≡ p mod p²`, computable via `Nat.find`, `= 1` iff `p` is not Wieferich — the book's "may take b=2" describes valid choices, not the least); `chi_neg_one_eq_one` (odd-order characters kill −1); `jacobiSum_pow_eq_sum` (the book's `J(p,q) = Σ χ(m^b(m−1))` IS Mathlib's `jacobiSum (χ^b) χ`); `gaussSum_mul_eq_jacobiSum_mul` (the fundamental relation `G(χ)G(χ^k) = J(χ,χ^k)G(χ^(k+1))` at `χ_{p,q}`); and the payoff `gaussSum_pow_eq_card_mul_prod`: **`G(χ_{p,q})^p = q·J(χ,χ)⋯J(χ,χ^(p−2)) ∈ ℤ[ζ_p]`** (`jacobiSum_mem_adjoin_zetaP`) — since step-3/4 exponents `p^w·u` have `w ≥ 1`, the tested powers are entirely Jacobi-sum expressions in the `(p−1)`-coordinate ring instead of `(p−1)(q−1)`, the structural upgrade that made APR-CL practical. The book's ideal-splitting connection (`(n) = N_1⋯N_((p−1)/f)`, norms `n^f`) is deferred to the APR-CL-paper phase.) | C&P Alg. 4.3.4 | `AzPolynomial/FiniteFieldTest` | per attempt: one `lenstraTest` |

## Building

```bash
lake build            # build the library
lake test             # build the #guard test suites (AzuriteTests)
lake build benchmark  # build the benchmark executable
```

CI (`.github/workflows/lean.yml`) builds the project, runs the tests, and
verifies that every `Azurite.*` declaration depends only on allowed axioms
via `scripts/check_axioms.lean`: exactly the standard three (`propext`,
`Classical.choice`, `Quot.sound`). `native_decide` is NOT permitted —
its last use (the Möller–Granlund reciprocal table) was replaced by a
kernel-checked pointwise `decide` — so `sorry`, `native_decide`, or any
other newly introduced axiom fails the build.

## Running Benchmarks

```bash
lake env .lake/build/bin/benchmark
```

## Project Structure

```
Azurite.lean          -- Root import file (all library modules)
AzuriteTests.lean     -- Root import file for the #guard test suites (lake test)
Examples.lean         -- Usage examples
lakefile.lean         -- Lake build configuration
scripts/              -- check_and_build.sh, check_axioms.lean
Azurite/
  Algorithm/            -- Generic algorithms (e.g., fast exponentiation)
  AzNat/              -- Computable multi-limb natural numbers
    Equiv/            -- Equivalence proofs with Nat
  AzInt/              -- Computable sign-magnitude integers
    Equiv/            -- Equivalence proofs with Int
  AzRat/              -- Computable lowest-terms rationals
    Equiv/            -- Equivalence proofs with Rat
  AzPolynomial/       -- Univariate polynomials
    Equiv/            -- Equivalence proofs with Polynomial R
  AzMvPolynomial/     -- Multivariate polynomials
    Equiv/            -- Equivalence proofs with MvPolynomial σ R
  AzPolynomialQ/      -- Rational polynomials (shared denominator)
    Equiv/            -- Equivalence proofs with AzPolynomial ℚ
  AzVector/           -- Fixed-length vectors
    Equiv/            -- Equivalence proofs with Fin n → R
  AzMatrix/           -- Fixed-size matrices
    Equiv/            -- Equivalence proofs with Matrix (Fin m) (Fin n) R
  AzFormula/          -- Computable first-order formulas (AzFieldAtom)
    Equiv.lean        -- Equivalence proofs with BPR Formula (FieldAtom)
  BasuPollackRoy/     -- Formalized textbook (BPR)
    Chapter1/
    Chapter2/
    Chapter8/
  Rounding/           -- Abstract rounding framework (RoundingMode, RoundingTarget)
  UInt64/             -- Helpers on Lean's built-in UInt64 type
    Equiv/            -- toNat-semantics proofs for UInt64 helpers
  Random/             -- Random generation for testing
  Benchmark/          -- Performance benchmarks
  Oracle/             -- The Malachite oracle (lake exe oracle)
  charts/             -- Benchmark result charts (SVG) and their data
```
